Under (ii) the root integer stretch already scales the container's 640×360 base units to the window, and a script-set container scale then multiplies it a second time — the **double-scale hazard** — so the effective scales end up as products and, once the ladder or `H ≠ k` is involved, as floats; under (i) the root stretch is disabled, the pipeline root sets the world `SubViewport` size on `size_changed`, places the `SubViewportContainer` and sets its integer scale from script, and sets `CanvasLayer.scale = (H, H)` (integer) by hand, so every scale in the window is an integer the script chose. (i) is the expected answer, not a decision: the render-pipeline ADR decides, and if (i) wins the PR-15 stretch keys become `mode = disabled` and the startup read-back follows. **What is gated on the ADR** and nothing else: `world_to_hud` (D.14), the cursor-unit conversion of `mouse_to_world_aim` (D.14b: the physical-px cursor source; the leading-candidate (i) needs none, (ii) needs the inverse final transform) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4)*, the `RESCALING` mechanics, the `Q = 1/k` path and the readback-chain criteria (ADR-0008 § 5.4). **What is not gated**: the solver (D.6–D.10), the sort contract and D.12, and the PR-4 placement rules — they hold under either wiring. The NOT SOURCEABLE list is thereby reduced to what is genuinely unknown: how a ladder step reaches `picture / k_z` under the winning wiring, and what the engine draws in the letterbox residual. **Renderer:** Forward+ (`project.yaml` `rendering`; the art bible's working assumption, reversible at the lighting spike); the Compatibility and Mobile renderers are assumed nowhere in this GDD, and a reversal re-runs PR-3's `use_hdr_2d` and the pass-B figures of D.16.

**What must be identical across A and B** (so no rule in this GDD, and no dependent, needs to know which won): the logical coordinate space and PR-2's policy; k, H and the zoom step; nearest sampling and the placement rules of PR-4; the PR-5 sort contract and z-bands; the whole camera contract of PR-6 (the `1/k` quantum is the one `[UNV]` item under B); the HUD layer and `world_to_hud`; the Filter hook of PR-10 (the container's material receives the world at *screen* resolution in both passes — under A because the container upscales, under B because the viewport is that size); the fringe at its registered width (registry `RIM_FRINGE_PX`, default 2 base px, range 1–3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from perception-filter)* (under B the shader takes k as a uniform, 8.10); the ambient-floor mechanism; the presentation contract of PR-8. **What may differ**: viewport size and memory (2–3 MB vs 22 MB of HDR render targets at 1080p, 8.6); light-texture `size_limit` and mipmaps (1024, off vs 2048, on — 8.3/8.4); the look of a cone edge and of a rotated horror segment (PR-4 rule 5); frame time.

---

**PR-8. The presentation contract every visual system inherits.** A visual system — Lighting, Trails, Instruments, HUD, the Filter, the rig animator, the `CameraSolver`'s host node — is a `PRESENTATION` subscriber (CR-10) and:

1. subscribes as `PRESENTATION` (or `INPUT_SURFACE` if it also issues commands — the HUD's prompts do not; the chart table holds the class for VS-B and submits nothing at MVP, Chart Table CT-27 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from chart-table-web-map-and-menus)*); the Debug Overlay alone is `DIAGNOSTIC`;
2. reads **last-committed** state through `SIM` and `PERCEPTION_OUT` read views; nodes the Perception Filter's tag contract marks *corruptible* obtain their displayed value from the Filter, truth-channel nodes read `SIM` directly, and the Debug Overlay reads the true channel for everything — which nodes default to which is the Filter GDD's to set (CR-10's two channels);
3. treats a notification as a dirty mark and does its reading and drawing in the presenter's `present` callable, run by the `PresentationDriver` stage inside the single `PipelineRoot._process` at `PRESENTATION` (ADR-0009 § 3, ADR-0008 § 6.2; `RenderingServer.frame_pre_draw` is reserved for the camera host's two canvas writes, the world `canvas_transform` and the parallax offsets, C2, and the camera solve is the `CAMERA_SOLVE` stage) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0009 sync)* — never inside the notification handler, where writes are disabled and a long handler stalls P5 for every other subscriber;
4. writes **only** `CanvasItem`, `Material`, `Light2D`, particle and audio properties (8.11's manifest rule); never a block, never a command (render presentation has no submit API — CR-14 H4), never a physics body (that is a `PROJECTOR`); **never a camera or viewport property unless it is the pipeline root or the camera host** (C1);
5. interpolates every moving value with the kernel's `tick_alpha` and the read view's previous/current pair (D.3), then floors to base px in the body frame (PR-4 rules 2–3) — **one interpolator** (PR-4 rule 8);
6. animates on `present_time` (PR-9), never on wall-clock and never counts ticks; it may read the committed `T` and `tick_alpha` for clock-locked phases (the beacon's drawn aim; a hook-divisor presenter's interpolation alpha, PR-9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from hud-and-suit-layer)* (a presentation that counts ticks would freeze under a render hitch while the world moved — the opposite of the honest hold D.3 wants);
7. **never causes a tick** (CR-11) and never blocks on one; if the state it needs has not been committed yet it draws the last committed state;
8. places every item at an integer base-px position in the body frame and declares its band (PR-5) — a node that sorts declares its sort origin as its floor-contact line, or its host face's if wall-mounted;
9. obeys the flash clamp of PR-13 for anything it animates in luminance.

Rationale: this is the renderer's copy of the concept's "corruption is presentation-only" rule, written so a lint can check it (8.11 routes the manifest rule to the lead programmer). *Alternative rejected:* letting presentation own small bits of state (a trail's fade timer, a gauge's needle lag) — the moment a trail's visibility depends on presentation state it is no longer the truth channel every hallucination is checked against (Failure & Trail's "pure reader" rule in the index). Camera state is one of the two presentation-owned states the pipeline admits, and C5 makes it non-persistent and reset on every cut so it can never diverge from the sim. **The other is the bounded exemption of registry `settle_response`** (In-World Instruments IW-11, K-2): a gauge needle's settle is closed-form in `present_time` from one `(from_pm, to_pm, t_jump)` triple per instrument plus `prev_honest_pm`, drawn for HONEST readings only, never persisted or replicated and snapped on restore — it changes how a needle arrives, never what it settles to *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view)*.

---

**PR-9. The presentation clock.** The pipeline owns `present_time`: a render-rate clock in seconds that advances by the render delta while the kernel is `RUNNING`, `SAVING` or `LOADING`-with-hold, is **frozen while the kernel is `PAUSED`**, and is the only clock layer-0 presentation may animate on (flow scroll, dust, the fringe's 4–6 s breathing, the Filter's stage-2 wobble, and only the non-geometric phase of the beacon — its drawn aim is the kernel clock `beacon_aim(T)` with `tick_alpha`, Lighting's *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from 2d-lighting-and-occlusion)*). Horror rig stepping is the exception: the rig's pose is sim-driven (every 5th tick) and presentation interpolates nothing between steps (5.4: stepped playback is the look) — the segment pose is stepped and never tweened; only the record's *position* may be interpolated, with the five-tick alpha `alpha_5 = ((T_now − T_run) + tick_alpha) / HORROR_RUN_TICKS` that a presenter on a divisor-5 hook derives from the kernel's one-tick `tick_alpha` (Horror HE-F18, C-HE-14) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from horror-entities)*. `present_time` reconciles three rules that otherwise collide: CR-16 ("`PAUSED` stops the tick but not rendering"), 7.4 ("the world simply stops, lit exactly as it was" — so the flow in the pipes must stop too, not just the sim) and CR-11 ("pausing presentation effects (reduced motion) never pauses the sim" — reduced motion scales *individual effects*, never `present_time`). Layers 3–4 run on the engine's process time, because the chart table must respond while paused; the HUD (layer 2) is frozen under pause with the world *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from hud-and-suit-layer)*. **`SceneTree.paused` `[BIN]` is not the kernel's pause** (H10 — the kernel is scene-tree independent): the pipeline root maps game pause to kernel `PAUSED` via the control plane and applies the engine's `paused` to presentation nodes only if at all → ADR (tick driver). Under pause the world viewport may stop re-rendering (`render_target_update_mode` `[BIN]`, last frame held → ADR), which is also the `HIDDEN` state's behaviour; step 11 confirms no stale frame survives a resize. **A `RESCALING` (a window resize, a display change, a zoom press) while updates are disabled forces `UPDATE_ONCE` on the world viewport for the frame of change** and then restores `UPDATE_DISABLED`, so a paused resize never presents the reallocated viewport's black or stale buffer; the held frame is re-rendered at the new size from the same committed state (no sim tick, `present_time` still frozen) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

---

**PR-10. The Perception Filter hook point.** The pipeline provides exactly one full-screen hook: **a `ShaderMaterial` slot on the `SubViewportContainer`**, assigned and nulled only by the pipeline root — the Filter writes shader parameters only (registry `filter_route`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from perception-filter)* — receiving the world viewport's output at screen resolution, drawn **below every `CanvasLayer`** — so the Filter pass **never samples or writes** the HUD, inspect card, menus, text chat, pings, letterbox bars or debug overlay (C-R; HR-1, HR-6, R-7 by construction). This is the fifth material of 8.10's "≤ 5 materials" row. Constraints on what the pass may do (ux-designer, adopted; the Filter GDD owns the effects): it **writes colour only** — no displacement, blur, chromatic aberration, pixelation or resolution change; it **only adds** violet or flesh at 4.4's places and never remaps sane hues, erodes shape or trail contrast, or drops hull below the measured floor; it obeys the flash clamp (PR-13); it never writes a camera or viewport property (C1). Its output is **multiplied by a truth-channel mask** — character sprites, hand tools, crewmate beams, trails, the true cone edge — **or decomposed into per-element materials** so truth-channel pixels are exact (C-R). Everything stage 2/3 does to the *whole image* goes in this slot; everything *per thing* — a lying gauge's gleam, a decoy light, a wholly substituted sprite (5.0) — goes on nodes inside layer 0 under PR-8: the Filter's own per-node materials; modifier state that Lighting reads (LO-18); and decoy overlays — Filter-owned lies that Lighting draws as unshaded sprites on light-mask bit 3 (LO-21, LO-24) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from perception-filter and 2d-lighting-and-occlusion)*. **The mask's production is → ADR**: (a) a second `SubViewport` sharing the camera pose that draws only truth-channel items as unlit silhouettes (cheap, no sort problem because it is a mask, not a composite), or (b) no mask, per-element materials only; the Filter GDD chooses — and has chosen (b) for MVP (`filter_route = PER_ELEMENT`: the container slot is `NOT_USED`, its material null by design; route (a) is a VS-A option only if the lighting spike proves an exact mask) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from perception-filter)*. *Alternative rejected:* compositing truth-channel items in a second viewport **above** the filtered world — those items sort against corruptible ones on the same Y-sorted layer, and splitting a sorted layer across two viewports breaks PR-5. `BackBufferCopy` without the material slot is the engine alternative (8.10) → ADR.

---

**PR-11. Frame-rate independence.** The sim runs at 60 Hz (CR-1); the pipeline renders at whatever rate the display and settings allow and never changes the tick. **The acceptance test is the kernel's:** identical `state_hash` after a scripted 10-s input replay at `max_fps` 30, 60, 144 and 0 (G3 + CR-11; spike step 13).

| Render rate | Ticks per frame | What presentation shows | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 144 fps | 0 or 1 (ticks on ≈ 42 % of frames) | interpolated positions between the last two commits at `tick_alpha ∈ [0,1)`, floored: camera and sprites step one base px at the interpolated instant, **never twice in a frame (this holds for every f_render ≥ 60 Hz = the tick rate; below 60 a frame spans more than one tick and a 1 px/tick body steps 1 or 2 base px — the 40 and 30 fps rows) and never backward** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4)*; without interpolation 58 % of frames would repeat a pose | no sub-base-pixel motion exists at any fps unless the `1/k` quantum is adopted (C3); `L_render` shrinks to ≤ 6.9 ms, input latency is otherwise unchanged above 60 |
| 60 fps | 1 | with tick-before-render, `tick_alpha` at draw time is ≈ 0, so an **interpolated transform shows approximately the *previous* tick's position**: continuous motion carries **one tick (≈ 17 ms) of visual latency by construction**, because D.3 forbids extrapolation. **Discrete committed state** (a light toggled, a gauge value, a trail appearing) reads `current` and shows in the same frame — D.4's best case applies to it. Look-ahead reads the **raw** aim (C4) and so carries no tick | the design target; the latency is the price of the honest clamp and is the same at every fps |
| 30 fps | 2 (catch-up) | the latest commit; `tick_alpha` clamps at 1 after the second tick; motion shows every second tick's pose — 2-base-px steps for a 1 px/tick body, honest, not smoothed | input-to-pixel worst case 50 ms (D.4); acceptable; **the Deck floor under pass A** |
| 40 / 90 Hz (Deck panels) | 1.5 / 0.67 average | the interpolation path is what keeps motion even at rates that are not multiples of 60. At 90 Hz (0.67 ticks per frame) the step is 0 or +1 base px, the same promise as 144 fps. At 40 Hz (1.5 ticks per frame, ticks per frame alternating 2, 1) a 1 px/tick body steps +1, +2, +1, +2 base px per frame: whole base pixels, never fractional, never backward, honest and unsmoothed like the 30 fps row; the "never twice in a frame" promise does not apply below 60 fps *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4)* | spike at 40 and 90 fps caps (step 13) |
| < 20 fps | up to `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME`, then sim-time lag (CR-17) | the latest commit; the world *slows* rather than extrapolates; a catch-up burst is ≤ 12 px and eases; only a named ≥ 48 px source (weld delta, Body shove, restore) triggers the camera cut (C5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | the pipeline reports frame time to the Debug Overlay; never a visual compensation |

`tick_alpha` is clamped at 1 so a hitch holds the last pose rather than extrapolating (D.3's Pillar 1 argument). Presentation reads `delta` only to advance `present_time` and the solver's smoothing. **Tick driver (settled in ADR-0001, Proposed; kernel CR-1a):** the kernel's `TickDriver` runs from the `_process` of one always-processing host node, so the physics step is not the clock and there is one accumulator (kernel D.5). `physics_jitter_fix`, `max_physics_steps_per_frame` and `physics_ticks_per_second` stay at engine defaults; spike KRN-LAT-02 records their measured effect on the tick pattern rather than assuming none. `delta_smoothing` `[BIN]` acts on the `_process` delta the driver converts to `delta_us` `[UNV]`, and the spike records it. **Order inside a frame** is fixed by process priority (Adapter, then driver, then presentation; ADR-0001 § 2), which the spike verifies on a dev build and a release export. **Position ownership (settled):** Ship Body owns ship-local kinematic position and integrates it inside the tick (kernel CR-4), so kernel `tick_alpha` is the one interpolator. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0001 GDD sync)* **V-sync and caps (PR-14):** ship `VSYNC_ENABLED` (engine default `[BIN]`); expose `MAILBOX` as "uncapped, no tearing" and `DISABLED` + `max_fps` for benchmarking; never `ADAPTIVE` by default (behaviour on Windows D3D12 vs Vulkan NOT SOURCEABLE — the binary reports `driver.windows = vulkan` `[BIN]` while `[REF]` says D3D12 is the 4.6+ default; 8.12 step 1 prints the active driver and the GDD assumes neither).

---

**PR-12. HUD scale.** The HUD's integer scale is `H ∈ {H_default, …, 2·H_default}` with **`H_default = max(k, 3)`** — **never below 3× on any display** (registry: `HUD_MIN_SCALE` = 3) (C-R), player-raisable to 2× the default in integer steps (HR-12's 100–200 %), labelled in Settings **by resulting cap height in screen px** (e.g. "21 px / 28 px / 35 px / 42 px" at 1080p), not by percentage (ux-designer). At 1080p the steps are 3×–6×; on the Deck and at 720p **3×–6× over a 2× world**; the Settings screen offers only the steps where the HUD's `bottom_row_fit` holds (registry; `H_default` always — the fallback when `fit` is false; H 5 and 6 are not offered at a 1280-px picture until CF-HS-11 is resolved) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from hud-and-suit-layer)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*; at 1440p 4×–8×; at 2160p 6×–12×. H is **independent of k and of zoom** (8.1: "the frame grows, the world does not"). The reading face (7.1) scales continuously and is the only non-integer text in the game. **Reading-face rasterisation** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: reading-face text is rasterised by the font server at its **final screen pixel size** (the label's font size is set to `round(base_size · scale_pct · window_scale)` on a `Control` chain whose `CanvasLayer.scale` and `Control.scale` are `(1, 1)` and whose own size is in screen px), and is never drawn under an integer-scaled `CanvasLayer` (`scale = H`) or under the world container's scale, where the same glyphs would be bitmap-stretched and blur; the pixel-grid faces (HUD) keep the integer `H` path. The reading face sits on its own sibling `CanvasLayer` (layer 4, scale (1,1), sizes in screen px) beside the pixel face (layer 3, scale `H`), so no reading-face text is drawn under an integer-scaled layer (ADR-0008 § 2.5) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)*; the acceptance test is a screenshot at 100 %, 125 % and 200 % showing crisp glyph edges (manual). Consequences: (i) because the Deck ships at H = 3 over k = 2, **two integer scales in one window is an MVP requirement**, not a post-spike nicety — it is PR-7's wiring ADR; the engine-programmer's note that "the HUD's integer scale comes from the root stretch with `Control.scale = (1,1)`" holds only when H = k, and `gui_snap_controls_to_pixels = true` `[BIN]` stays on the root in every wiring; (ii) **7.0's critical-strip size cap (12 % × 5 % of frame) is exceeded by default on the Deck** at H = 3 over a 2× world (the plate is 1.5× larger relative to the picture than at 1080p) — routed to art-director as a known, accepted cost of C-R's 3× floor — **resolved by the HUD & Suit Layer GDD**: the cap is defined at the 1080p reference frame and the Deck overage is accepted; a letterbox hairline, if used, is a layer-1 element excluded from the HUD count *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from hud-and-suit-layer)*; (iii) the arcminute floors per device class (desk monitor, TV, handheld) that justify the 3× floor are the accessibility-specialist's table — routed. HUD elements are anchor-relative to the picture (PR-2) and survive a fractional HUD canvas. **The mechanism** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: "anchors absorb the fraction" is not one, because a `Control`'s anchors resolve against the root viewport, not the picture; instead the pipeline root sizes a **HUD-root `Control` to `hud_rect / H`** (a possibly fractional size, at the HUD rect's origin, the HUD rect being pinned to the `k_base` picture, PR-2) on every `RESCALING`, and every HUD element anchors to that Control; `world_to_hud` (D.14) returns coordinates relative to it. `H_default = max(k_auto, 3)` (unchanged).

---

**PR-13. Photosensitivity and brightness.** The pipeline hosts the frame-level invariants; Lighting owns the per-light clamp through its modifier contract. (a) **Per light:** no light may produce more than **3 flashes per rolling 1-s window**, a flash being a pair of opposing luminance changes ≥ 10 %, red included (C-R); the alarm beacon's 2–3 s sweep (2.8) and the cryo lamp's gutter (0.3–0.5 s dips no more often than every 10–20 s, 2.2) pass; **the deployable's flicker (3.2) must be specified as a number and pass the clamp** — routed to art-director and technical-artist; the pipeline-side ceiling the art-director authors inside is at most 4 transitions per second (`F = 2`, one pair under `F_MAX`) at any amplitude ≥ 10 % (Open Question 6). **The clamp is analytic, not measured** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: Lighting computes each light's requested luminance transitions per tick from its modifier state (beacon sweep, cryo gutter, a deployable's flicker, a toggle), applies D.15's transition rule to that sequence and delays transitions *before* they are drawn; the regional and frame-mean sums use each light's luminance times its footprint in the region as an upper bound. A GPU readback is never in the clamp's path — it would either stall the frame or arrive frames late. (b) **Regional and frame-mean:** the same ceiling applies to each region of a 3 × 3 grid over the displayed picture at the current field (a ninth of it; 213 × 120 base px only at the default 640×360 field, 128 × 72 at the 384×216 field of `k_z` = 5 at 1080p — registry `flash_governor`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from 2d-lighting-and-occlusion)* and to the frame mean, so overlapping lights cannot sum past it; **no full-frame flash in any state, on any layer** (7.3, HR-13). (c) **A "reduce flashing" toggle, separate from reduced motion**: deployables steady, power arcs held as glow, the beacon becomes a static mark + sound + HUD glyph (HR-8's form), the cryo gutter kept. **The toggle defaults ON on a fresh profile, at first launch** (default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): creative-director recommendation, design-review decision D79 / X10; a player who wants the full effects turns it off in settings); reduced motion keeps its default OFF. While it is on, the strict ceiling of (g) applies to every source with no exemptions *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. (d) **No full-frame gamma or brightness**; HR-15's bounded hull-material step is the only brightness control (PR-3 b). (e) A **first-launch photosensitivity notice** (chart-table register); it states that reduce flashing is already on and where to change it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. (f) A **flash scan** of the 2.8 breach composition and the 2.4 lost state is a spike step (routed to the technical-artist's 8.12 list); the scan — a 3 × 3 region and frame-mean luminance reduction of the displayed picture, read back asynchronously so it is at least two frames late — is **verification only**, in dev builds and the flash-scan tool, absent from release builds, and costs at most 0.5 ms of GPU time on RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK (pending spike measurement; Open Question 20, closed by R12) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*. (g) **Counting unit and area weighting** (user ruling 2026-10-27, design-review decision D91 / X9): the counting unit is `F = ceil(n_transitions / 2)` (D.15; registry `flash_count`), and the ceiling `F_MAX` = `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` = 3 is **weighted per area**: a region or the frame mean counts a transition only when the *area-weighted* change of its mean luminance reaches 10 % (D.15), so a change confined to a small patch does not count at region level. Two source classes are **exempt from the element-level count** (they still enter every region and frame-mean sum at their area weight): *sub-threshold digit swaps* (a HUD numeral change whose area-weighted change is under the 10 % rule) and *prompt show-starts* (a single step, spaced by the HUD's own floor of `ceil(TICK_RATE / F_MAX)` = 20 ticks). **Strict, never exempt:** the Alarm Red mark (counted per element with no area weighting) and **every source while reduce flashing is on** (exemptions suspended). The no-full-frame-flash rule of (b) is unchanged. The HUD owns its digit grid (`HONEST_TICK_MS` 500, ratified by the same ruling) and its mark behaviour; this section owns the unit and the rule *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The Filter pass obeys (a)–(b) (PR-10).

---

**PR-14. What Settings may expose (all integer; no non-integer option anywhere).** Display mode (windowed / fullscreen / borderless); resolution; **integer world scale** (Auto + list; a manual k below Auto forces `keep`, PR-2); HUD scale (labelled by cap height, PR-12); reading-face scale (continuous — the one exception, and it is text, not pixels); aspect `keep` / `expand` (post-spike); frame cap (30 / 60 / 120 / 144 / 240 / off); V-sync (enabled / mailbox / off); reduced motion (disables look-ahead, jolt → 0, world effects per HR-8 — never trail propagation, never `present_time`); reduce flashing (PR-13; **default ON at first launch**, default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20)) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; the HR-15 ambient-floor step (Lighting's, surfaced here). Never: a camera smoothing slider that could introduce overshoot, a zoom level, a FOV, gamma, brightness, sharpening, or any filter.

---

**PR-15. Guard rails (engine-programmer, adopted).** (1) The `[display]` stretch block (`mode = disabled` (wiring (i), ADR-0008 s2), `aspect = keep`, `scale_mode = integer` written explicitly because the built-in default is `fractional` (probed 2026-10-28), 640×360) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)*, `default_texture_filter = 0`, both `snap_2d_*` keys, `hdr_2d` and `use_debanding` are **set to their required values in `project.godot`** (approval path 8.11 flag 7 — not Section C's to write; a value equal to the engine default is not serialised by the editor, so the tests read the *effective* value, not the key's presence) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **`use_debanding` and the "palette exact" promise** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the engine's debanding is a dither of about one least-significant bit added to the 8-bit output, which would perturb a flat palette fill and contradicts the SDR-exact palette promise of layer 0 (PR-3). The required value is therefore **`use_debanding = false` on the world viewport** (so also its engine default) **unless a spike item (step 1) shows both: (a) a flat fill of every palette entry reads back bit-exact with debanding on, and (b) a dark gradient that matters (the 0–1 % ambient) bands visibly with it off**; it may be switched on only if (a) and (b) both hold, and stays off otherwise (an AC reads back the flat fills, Startup assertions). The same item also carries: together with *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `rendering/environment/defaults/default_clear_color = Color(0, 0, 0, 1)` — the "true black" letterbox bars are the engine clear colour, which defaults to a dark grey, so without this key PR-2's bars are not black; `display/window/size/window_width_override = 1280` and `window_height_override = 720` — otherwise an editor-launched game opens at the 640×360 viewport size, below `MIN_WINDOW`; the 4.7 **HDR-output flag explicitly off** (the ProjectSettings key is `display/window/hdr/request_hdr_output`, default false, probed 2026-10-28; the debanding key is `rendering/anti_aliasing/quality/use_debanding`; formerly NOT SOURCEABLE here — the 4.7 reference lists the feature but not the key — the name was read from the 4.7.2 class dump; *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)* the 4.6 palette is SDR-exact, PR-3 layer 0); and `transparent_bg = false` stated explicitly on the world `SubViewport` and per-pixel window transparency not allowed on the root (so no layer ever composites against an alpha background). Wiring (i) is adopted (ADR-0008 § 2), so the effective stretch mode is `disabled` (the engine default, absent from the file is correct). (2) A **debug-build startup assertion** in the pipeline root reads back `content_scale_mode == DISABLED`, `content_scale_factor == 1.0`, `Window.min_size == (1280, 720)`, the ADR-0008 s5.4 world and HUD scale readbacks, `fallback_to_opengl3` and `fallback_to_d3d12` false, `thread_model == 1`, the world `SubViewport`'s filter / snap / hdr / debanding members, the container filter (`== CanvasItem.TEXTURE_FILTER_NEAREST`, the right enum per target), `physics_interpolation == false`, `position_smoothing_enabled == false`, `Camera2D.zoom == (1,1)`, the clear colour `== (0,0,0,1)`, the window size override `== (1280, 720)`, the HDR-output flag `== false`, the world `SubViewport` `transparent_bg == false`, and prints the driver name — failing loudly on any mismatch *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; (3) a **headless gdUnit4 test parses `project.godot`** for the same keys so the check runs in CI without a window; (4) a **debug-only tree walk** (every frame behind a console toggle) over the world viewport flags any `CanvasItem` whose position has a fractional part after presentation has written it, any `rotation != 0` under human-built groups, any `Camera2D.zoom != (1,1)`, any `Control.scale != (1,1)` on the HUD, Y-sort enabled on the floor layer, and a `TileSet.tile_size` of 16; (5) a scene-tree lint: no `Camera2D` descends from a `PhysicsBody2D`. The three failure modes these guard against — shipping the engine defaults, two clocks moving one thing, fractional transforms leaking into the world canvas — are the ones a solo first-time Godot developer most plausibly builds.

---

**Unresolved conflicts carried to Open Questions (not decided here).**
1. **Zoom on 2× displays.** C-R "no zoom-out below a 2× world" removes the zoom step on the Steam Deck, at 720p and under a manual 2× — but the game-designer made the player's zoom step one of the four mitigations for "across the room" readability and wants it "available in every state", and 5.6's 50 % gate is what makes a 1× world unreadable. Options for the user: accept no zoom on 2× displays (Deck players rely on look-ahead and kits alone); permit the 1× step on the Deck only, as a deliberate press (the 50 % marks test is still met — 5.6 tier 2 is defined at 50 %); or raise the Deck's base presentation (not possible without breaking 8.1). Routed to game-designer, ux-designer and creative-director. *(Superseded by the user-ruling note below — the ladder reaches 1× on every display; consistency check 2026-10-26.)*
2. **Exponential follow vs avatar jitter.** Decided in favour of `τ_follow = 0.18 s` (gameplay-programmer); the systems-designer's ±1 px avatar wobble concern is an A/B at step 12, not a reopening.
3. **C-R21 first-boot black-level calibration** reframes a user decision (no brightness control) — recorded as an Open Question for the user and creative-director, not adopted (Resolved 2026-10-26 — user decision, guide-only calibration; Open Question 5) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21: stale open wording closed)*.

> **User ruling, decided (2026-10-26) — zoom ladder, supersedes the single held step out in C6 and the note below.** The player may zoom **in and out, Project Zomboid-style, to a bounded degree**: the world scale `k` steps through the integer ladder `k ∈ [1, k_auto + 2]` *(read `k_base`, C6)* (so at 1080p: 1×, 2×, **3× default**, 4×, 5×; on a 2× display: 1×, 2× default, 3×, 4×), by scroll wheel / button, **persistent** (not held), each step a cut (no tween — no non-integer scale ever exists), `Camera2D.zoom` still always `(1,1)`, HUD scale unchanged, prompts and the Filter mask re-derived per step. Zoom-in shows a smaller field at larger pixels; zoom-out to 1× shows up to ~9× the default field at 1080p. The art bible's 8.1 "one integer step out" becomes "integer steps in either direction" — amendment routed to art-director. Consequences the game-designer's analysis still carries: the default frame is no longer guaranteed to be *the* frame, so the "share of time away from default zoom" playtest metric becomes the check on whether the tight frame survives in practice; 5.6 tier-2 marks are defined at 50 % so identity bands meet spec down to 1× on 3× displays and the Deck acuity check (Open Question 4) still applies at 1×; zoom level is presentation state (never saved, reset to default on re-wake — the room must frame identically); zoom changes are never driven by world state (a window, not a sensor). *(Former held-step note trimmed 2026-10-26 — consistency check; the Deck acuity routing lives in C6 and Open Question 4.)*

> **Amendments from Section D (adopted — decision log 2026-10-26; these supersede any conflicting value stated above in Core Rules):** (1) the pan-speed ceiling is `1.25 × v_max` (not 1.25 × walk) and the lag cut `LAG_CUT` is **48 px**, not 24 — under first-order follow the steady-state lag is exactly `v·τ` and the camera's steady speed equals the avatar's, so a ceiling below `v_max` is unreachable by a legal follow of a runner and a 24 px cut would fire continuously at run speed; build invariant `LAG_CUT > v_max · τ_follow`. (2) The cut predicates evaluate the **follow component only**; look-ahead has its own slew clamp `L_SLEW_MAX = 144 px/s`, so a 180° aim flick does not trigger a cut (full reversal reaches 95 % in ≈ 1.4 s). (3) Look-ahead reads the **aim regardless of the beam's lit state** — a tile shift on beam toggle would read as a jump. (4) Predicate order: the inner-box clamp applies to the final pose, while lag/pan cuts read the unclamped follow term. Also: `T_PAN` is 0.10 s (6 / 15 / 3 frames at 60 / 144 / 30 fps) so the solver's predicates are frame-rate independent (written in seconds; the pose itself is bounded to within 1 base px across fps, D.6); the inner box is frame-relative (0.30 × field); Y-sort ties on an equal row break by layer order (tile layers before free items); *(superseded 2026-10-27 — design review: the free-item tie-break is now the class rank and stable entity ID of PR-5, never `x`)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

> **Accepted from Section H (2026-10-26):** the explicit `project.godot` block written under PR-15 (1) also pins `physics/common/physics_interpolation = false`, and the headless parse test checks it alongside the read-back assertion. Test directory: `tests/unit/render/` (sibling of the kernel's `tests/unit/kernel/`). Performance figures in Section H are the qa-lead's proposals, provisional until the spike.

> **Creative-director recommendation (CD-GDD-ALIGN, 2026-10-26), RATIFIED by the user 2026-10-26** — *`expand` aspect on wide displays*: accept `expand` with the 960×540 cap after spike step 11. The extra width shows honest void in every dark state; the extra lit floor appears mainly in the stable home-ship state where there is no threat; there is no competitive stake (anti-pillar); and a third of the screen black at 1600×900 is the worse experience. Recorded as an Edge Case under window handling. Also adopted: the Settings surface (PR-14) includes **look-ahead strength** (off / short / default); jolt strength is not exposed until the VS-A jolt decision.

> **Decided (orchestrator, consistency check 2026-10-26) — zoom-in vs the inner-box invariant (C7):** the look-ahead bound scales with the visible field, `L_MAX_eff = LOOKAHEAD_PX × field_h / 360` (72 → 54 px at the 270-row field of k = 4 at 1080p, 43.2 px at the 216-row field of k = 5 *(figures re-run 2026-10-26 — consistency check)*), so `v_max·τ_follow + L_MAX_eff ≤ 0.30·field_h` holds at every ladder step and running is never clamped by zoom; Section G's invariant is asserted at every reachable `field_h`, not only the default. Registry `camera_lookahead` and `speed_ceiling` carry the scaling. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check: with the Player Controller's `v_max` = 180 px/s the steady-state lag `v_max·τ_follow` = 32.4 px does not shrink with the field, so scaling `L_MAX` alone fails at every zoom-in step — 32.4 + 54 = 86.4 > 81 at 270 rows. The decision is therefore carried as "the solver's length parameters scale by `s = field_h / 360`": `τ_follow_eff`, `L_MAX_eff`, `L_SLEW_MAX_eff`, `LAG_CUT_eff` and `JUMP_CUT_eff` all scale, the pan ceiling `1.25·v_max` does not (it can never sit below `v_max`), and the invariant then holds at every field iff it holds at 360 — 24.3 + 54 = 78.3 ≤ 81 at 270; 64.8 + 144 = 208.8 ≤ 216 at 720; 36 + 80 = 116 ≤ 120 and 72 + 160 = 232 ≤ 240 at the Deck's 400 / 800. D.6, D.7, D.10 and Section G carry it.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `τ_follow_eff` scales by `min(1, s)`, not `s` — scaling τ up at `s > 1` made the frame keep drifting for 1.6 s after stopping at 1× on 1080p and 3.2 s on 2160p, against Game Feel "Stillness", and put `τ_eff` on 1600×900 `expand` (`s` = 1.25) outside its safe range; the other length parameters (`L_MAX`, `L_SLEW_MAX`, `LAG_CUT`, `JUMP_CUT`) still scale by `s`. The invariant becomes `0.30·field_h > v_max·τ·min(1, s) + L_MAX·s`, which is the unchanged scale-free check for `s ≤ 1` and reads `36·s > 32.4` for `s > 1` (true for all `s > 0.9`); re-run: 720: 32.4 + 144 = 176.4 ≤ 216; Deck 400: 32.4 + 80 = 112.4 ≤ 120; Deck 800: 32.4 + 160 = 192.4 ≤ 240.

### States and Transitions

**Pipeline states**

| State | World viewport | Layer 1 (hold) | `present_time` | HUD / chart table | Enter from | Exit to |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `BOOTING` | not yet built; window measured; k and H computed; viewports allocated; startup assertion (PR-15) runs | black | 0, frozen | hidden | process start | `PRESENTING` on the first `RUNNING` tick commit, via a camera `CUT`; if the kernel enters `PAUSED` directly from `LOADING`, one `CUT` and one rendered frame precede `PAUSED_FROZEN` so the hold has a frame to hold; assertion failure → dev build halts |
| `PRESENTING` | rendering every frame at k | hidden | advancing | visible | any | — |
| `RESCALING` | held for the frame of change; k, H, logical size, letterbox, camera limits and `world_to_hud` re-derived; viewport reallocated; HUD re-anchored | n/a | advancing | re-anchored to the new picture rect | `Viewport.size_changed` / `dpi_changed` (window resize, display or mode change), a `zoom_in` / `zoom_out` step *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check)*, `keep`/`expand`, world-scale or UI-scale setting change | `PRESENTING` next frame; no frame ever shows a fractional transform (step 11 screenshots the first frame after each change); a continuous drag of the window edge is a sequence of one-frame `RESCALING`s debounced to the final size → ADR |
| `TRANSIT_HOLD` | rendering (so an exterior leg can show) or update off if nothing is loaded | **black** (`#000000`; no text, spinner or tip), fade 120 ms in, cut out | advancing | HUD visible (HR-3); chart table available | `pod_hop` `Applied`; kernel `LOADING` entered mid-session (death stub, load) | `PRESENTING` via a camera `CUT` when `web.view.destination_ready` and the pipeline's own projector latch both hold, or on `LOADING → RUNNING` — except that a restore into `ARRIVING` re-enters the hold while `web.view.transit_state == ARRIVING` and a restore into LEG presents the exterior leg; the first `RUNNING` frame after any `LOADING` is already filtered for the restored sanity (Edge Cases) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from web-map-and-transit and save-load-and-versioned-format)* |
| `PAUSED_FROZEN` | **last frame held; not dimmed, not blurred, not desaturated** (7.4); re-render off → ADR | hidden | **frozen** | HUD visible (frozen readings, except that an active warning mark is held ON solid, HUD HS-23) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from hud-and-suit-layer)*; chart table live on process time | kernel `RUNNING → PAUSED` | `PRESENTING` on `PAUSED → RUNNING` — no transition effect; the world resumes mid-motion exactly where it stopped |
| `HIDDEN` | not rendered | n/a | advancing (minimising does not pause the sim unless Settings route it to the kernel's control plane) | not rendered | window minimised or occluded | previous state on restore, through one `RESCALING` if the size changed |
| *(modifier)* `DEBUG_OVERLAY` | debug draws and the fractional-transform tree walk enabled in layer 0 (dev builds) | — | — | layer 5 visible with true values, per-viewport draw counts, camera state | toggle | toggle; **absent from release builds** (R-7) — a flag over any state, not a state |

Every `(state, trigger)` pair not listed is ignored and reported to diagnostics. `FAULTED` in the kernel is not a pipeline state: the pipeline keeps presenting the last committed state (dev builds halt with the overlay; release returns to the boot-flow main menu, Chart Table CT-26, matching KRN:151 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from chart-table-web-map-and-menus)*) — the renderer must never be the thing that hides a fault.

**Camera states** (the `CameraSolver`'s state machine; every transition is a player input, a kernel state change or a solver rule — never world state, C1). **The camera's state is three independent fields, not one enum** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: **follow** ∈ {`FOLLOWING`, `CLAMPED`} (`CLAMPED` if either axis is clamped, driven only by the limits rect and the unclamped pose), **zoom** ∈ {default, `ZOOMED`} (`k_z ≠ k_base`, driven only by presses and `RESCALING`), and **hold** ∈ {live, `HELD`, `CUT`} (driven only by kernel and pipeline state and the cut rules). The rows below name the common combinations; every combination is legal and the transitions are independent: a zoom step changes only the zoom field (and is a `RESCALING` frame that re-derives the field, rescales the solver state per D.6 and re-evaluates the clamp, so a step can move `FOLLOWING` to `CLAMPED` and back); a `CUT` clears the smoothing history and the jolt but never the zoom field (except the VS-A re-wake) and never the follow field's rect; `HELD` freezes the pose without clearing zoom or clamp, and `PAUSED_FROZEN → PAUSED` resumes to live with no `CUT`; `LOADING` holds, and `RUNNING` cuts. Missing transitions made explicit: `ZOOMED` + `CLAMPED` is legal; a zoom press while `HELD` is discarded (Edge Cases); a rebase (`BODY_TRANSFER` with `frame_delta_px`) is a live-to-live transition that shifts state and clears nothing.

| State | Pose rule | Interpolating? | Enter from | Exit to |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FOLLOWING` | `quantise(smooth(target = avatar(α) + m·lookahead(raw_aim), τ_follow))` (+ jolt offset, default 0) | yes (`tick_alpha`, prev/curr; raw aim for look-ahead) | `CUT` complete; `CLAMPED` when the unclamped pose re-enters limits; `ZOOMED` when a press returns `k_z` to `k_base` *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check)* | `CLAMPED`, `ZOOMED`, `CUT`, `HELD` |
| `CLAMPED` | as `FOLLOWING`, then clamped per axis by the solver (the only clamp) to the body's limits rect (int base px, `PROJECTOR`-set, solver-consumed; `Camera2D.limit_*` untouched) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | yes | `FOLLOWING` when the unclamped pose leaves the rect on any axis | `FOLLOWING` when back inside; also the pipeline's only fixed frame (a rect the size of the picture) |
| `ZOOMED` (formerly `ZOOMED_OUT`) | as `FOLLOWING`/`CLAMPED` with the world at the ladder step `k_z ∈ [1, k_base + 2]`, `k_z ≠ k_base`, logical size `picture / k_z`; every step is a one-frame **cut** (`RESCALING`); the solver's length parameters are scaled by `s = field_h / 360` (`τ_follow` by `min(1, s)`; D.6–D.10) and the solver state is rescaled on the step's `RESCALING` frame (lag by the `τ_eff` ratio, `L` by `s_new/s_old`, D.6) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | yes | `zoom_out` pressed while `k_z > 1`, or `zoom_in` pressed while `k_z < k_base + 2` (edge-triggered; one step per press; persistent, never held; a press beyond either end is ignored and `diag.zoom_step_refused` increments) | `FOLLOWING`/`CLAMPED` when a press returns `k_z` to `k_base`; **re-wake resets `k_z` to `k_base`** (through that `CUT`); otherwise the step survives `CUT` (body transfer, in-session load, transit), `HELD` and `PAUSED_FROZEN`; a `RESCALING` that changes `k_base` rebuilds the ladder and clamps `k_z` to its ends *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check)* |
| `CUT` | teleport: solver `f` reset to the target; `L` reset to 0 and eased in on a wake or load (kept on a solver-initiated cut); jolt cleared; smoothing history cleared *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "look-ahead at full value")* | no — the first frame is final | `TRANSIT_HOLD` ending; every `LOADING → RUNNING`; re-wake (framing identical to first wake); a pod or transit body transfer, or a weld/detach whose hull jump is not guaranteed zero; the solver's own cut rule — pan ceiling exceeded ≈ 6 frames, lag > 48 px, or target jump ≥ 48 px in one frame (C5, backstops). A weld/detach `BODY_TRANSFER` with `frame_delta_px` is a **rebase**, not a `CUT` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `FOLLOWING` (or `CLAMPED` if the new body has limits) |
| `HELD` | frozen at the last pose | no | pipeline `PAUSED_FROZEN`; `TRANSIT_HOLD` with nothing to follow; kernel `LOADING` — kernel and pipeline state only, never world state (C1). Crew loss needs no camera rule: "holds exactly where it was until the hard cut" is `FOLLOWING` a stationary body with zero lag | `FOLLOWING` on resume; `CUT` on arrival or re-wake |
| *(not provided)* cinematic lock, free-look, peek, shared camera | — | — | — | none exists (C9); a fixed frame is `CLAMPED` |

Camera state is never persisted (C5). Body re-parenting without a load (detach, weld) with a `frame_delta_px` is a **rebase** (live-to-live; lag and `L` kept, nothing cleared); only a re-parenting whose hull jump is not guaranteed zero, or a pod or transit body transfer, is a `CUT` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier line called every detach and weld a `CUT`, against C5, the Edge Cases and the rebase AC)*.

### Interactions with Other Systems

**All interfaces below were authored as provisional; every row's system except Settings & Input Rebinding and Accessibility Settings (Alpha) now has a GDD, which confirms its own half** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The pipeline owns the mechanism (viewports, scales, layers, sort contract, the `CameraSolver` and its host, `present_time`, `world_to_hud`, the Filter slot, the flash-clamp invariants); every dependent owns what it draws. Rows marked **(index)** are dependents the systems index does not currently list and are index-correction candidates. Routed items from the four specialist inputs are carried in the notes column.

| # | System | Flows in (to the pipeline) | Flows out (from the pipeline) | Interface owner | Provisional notes and routed items |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Command & Simulation Layer** | kernel state (`RUNNING`/`PAUSED`/`LOADING`…) for C.2; `tick_alpha`; read views with previous/current; P5 notifications; the control plane for pause | nothing — the pipeline never submits and never writes a block; it is itself a `PRESENTATION` subscriber | Kernel: views, `tick_alpha`, classes · Pipeline: how they are consumed | **Settled:** tick driver is `_process`-driven (kernel CR-1a, ADR-0001 Proposed); `SceneTree.paused` is not kernel pause; tick-before-render ordering is by process priority, verified by spike KRN-LAT-02. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0001 GDD sync)* **Settled (ADR-0008 § 6.1):** the kernel's `snap_baseline` on entry to `RUNNING` is the only restore-continuity mechanism, so the first frame after a load does not interpolate from a stale pose; there is no owner-side `post_restore` rule *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)*. PR-11's CR-4 position-ownership tension → Open Q 3 |
| 2 | **2D Lighting & Occlusion** | `Light2D`, occluders, the ambient (default carrier: the per-section `ambient_pm` material slot; `CanvasModulate` a spike-selectable fallback, LO-12), the hull-floor material and the HR-15 raise, light textures, the emissive overlay (which sorts with its host face), placed in layer 0 | k and `RENDER_MODE` (light-texture `size_limit`/mipmaps; fringe uniform); PR-5 bands and light-mask bits; `present_time` for flicker and other non-geometric phase (the beacon's drawn aim is the kernel clock `beacon_aim(T)` with `tick_alpha`, not `present_time`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from 2d-lighting-and-occlusion)*; the pass A/B decision made *with* Lighting at 8.12 step 2 | Pipeline: viewport, mode flag, bands · Lighting: every light value, ambient, the **per-light flash clamp** (PR-13 a) through its modifier contract | Lighting owns ambient; the pipeline owns no light value. **Routed:** the deployable flicker number must be specified and pass the ≤ 3 flashes/s clamp (art-director, technical-artist); `glow_hdr_threshold` re-found under 4.6 glow (step 8); light-mask behaviour (step 4). Lighting's six shadow slots and ≤ 24 non-shadow lights are what pass B multiplies by up to 9× |
| 3 | **Player Controller & Input** | the local avatar as camera target (binding table); the **local raw beam aim** for look-ahead (not the coalesced `set_aim` intent), which for a mouse is computed from the pipeline's open-loop `mouse_to_world_aim` (D.14b: `f_q + cursor_off`, with the published `f_q`) — PC-5 reads that mapping and never `mouse_to_world_view` (Open Question 18) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the 5-facing sprite and per-facing chest point (beam origin) as a Y-sort item with foot-row sort origin | `tick_alpha` and the floor-to-base-px rule the avatar sprite and its chest point must obey (same rounded position); the reduced-motion flag; the `zoom_in` / `zoom_out` edge inputs (the controller's PC-16; player inputs the pipeline consumes) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check)* | Pipeline: camera, placement · Controller: sprite, aim, intent, the two zoom edge inputs | **Open boundary inherited from the kernel (Open Q 3):** who owns position; the camera reads whichever view owns it and the Controller's aim. **Routed:** walk and run speeds in **whole base px per tick** while the camera quantum is 1 (PR-4 rule 3); whoever integrates position exposes previous/current |
| 4 | **Failure & Trail System** | trail sprites in three sizes: floor trails on the floor-mark band; wall trails in the Y-sort layer with their host face's sort origin | the band table and the sort-origin rule; `present_time` for drip/mist animation (never for propagation — Graph state; HR-8 forbids suppressing it) | Pipeline: bands · Trails: every mark | Trails are a truth channel: exact under the Filter's mask or per-element decomposition (PR-10); the Filter pass may never erode trail contrast (C-R) |
| 5 | **Perception Filter** | shader parameters for the container `ShaderMaterial`, which the pipeline root alone assigns and nulls (registry `filter_route`; slot `NOT_USED` at MVP, route PER_ELEMENT); the Filter's own per-node materials inside layer 0; modifier state Lighting reads (LO-18) and decoy overlays drawn by Lighting on mask bit 3 (LO-21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from perception-filter and 2d-lighting-and-occlusion)*; perceived values as data for HUD/Instruments; the truth/corruptible tag contract; the truth-channel mask if route (a) | the slot, the world texture at screen resolution, k as a uniform, `present_time` for wobble; nothing above layer 0 is reachable; **no camera or viewport property is writable** | Pipeline: the slot and its assignment, its position in the stack, the constraints of PR-10 · Filter: everything drawn with it, which nodes read which channel, the mask route | **Routed:** camera framing (position, zoom, rotation, offset) joins the never-shift anchor list — art bible §1 Corollary / 2.9 and the Filter GDD. The Filter never changes the true reveal radius (index row 20 — Lighting's modifier layering enforces it, not the pipeline). Debug Overlay compares against the Filter by reading the true channel (row 10) |

## Formulas

*Every value below is **PROVISIONAL until the lighting spike** (art bible 8.12 steps 2 and 5; the engine-programmer's proposed steps 11 "stretch and window", 12 "motion integrity", 13 "pacing"). Values that depend on the avatar's locomotion speeds — `v_walk`, `v_run`, `v_max` — are additionally **bound to the Player Controller GDD**, which owns them and fixes `v_walk` = 2 px/tick = 120 base px/s and `v_run` = `v_max` = 3 px/tick = 180 base px/s (registry `V_WALK` / `V_RUN`; whole base px per tick per Section C PR-4 rule 3); they are not this system's knobs, and every derived number below was re-run against them *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check)*. At a zoom-ladder step the solver's length parameters scale by `s = field_h / 360` (D.6, D.7, D.10) so every invariant that holds at the 360-row field holds at every reachable field. Notation: base px unless stated; `dt` is the render-frame delta in seconds; `W × H` is the window in screen px; `L_w × L_h` is the world viewport's logical size (640×360 by default). Formulas D.6–D.11 are the `CameraSolver` (Section C PR-6 C3) and are required to give the same quantised pose to within 1 base px at 60 and 144 fps on a continuous target trace (a bound, not identity; D.6 states where lag is measured), which is why every predicate is written in seconds, never frames; a discontinuous input (a one-frame step, an aim flick) is a sampled event whose response depends on `dt` by construction. At a zoom-ladder step `τ_follow` scales by `min(1, s)` and the other length parameters by `s` (D.5–D.10).* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Four reconciliations found while deriving these formulas, adopted into Section C's meaning (adopted — decision log 2026-10-26):**
1. *Pan ceiling and lag cut.* Under first-order follow the steady-state lag is exactly `v × τ_follow` and the camera's steady-state speed equals the avatar's, so a ceiling of "1.25× walk" is unreachable by a legal follow of a running avatar, and a 24 px lag cut fires continuously at run speed (180 × 0.18 = 32.4 px *(re-run 2026-10-26 — consistency check)*). The ceiling is **1.25 × `v_max`** (the avatar's fastest locomotion), `LAG_CUT` is **48 base px**, and the invariant `LAG_CUT > v_max × τ_follow` is written into D.10 so Section G flags it whenever either knob moves.
2. *Cut predicates evaluate the follow component only; look-ahead has its own slew clamp.* A 180° aim flick under `τ_lookahead = 0.40 s` would otherwise start a 360 px/s look-ahead swing that exceeds any ceiling for ≈ 16 frames and cut the camera on every flick. Look-ahead is clamped to `L_SLEW_MAX = 144` base px/s (D.7); a flick is the player's input, so C-R "never reverses within 100 ms unless the player did" is satisfied by construction.
3. *Look-ahead reads the aim regardless of beam lit state.* The game-designer's rule is "beam aim", not "beam on"; a camera that shifted a tile on beam toggle would read as a jump.
4. *Predicate order.* The inner-box clamp (D.8) is applied to the pose; the lag and pan cuts (D.10) are evaluated on the **unclamped** follow term — otherwise, since `LAG_CUT + L_MAX = 120 > 108` (the vertical half-extent), the lag cut would be unreachable on the vertical axis.

---

**D.1 World scale**

The `world_scale` formula (registry: `world_scale_k`) is defined as:

`k = floor(min(W / 640, H / 360))`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| W | int | 1280 – unbounded (screen px) | engine (window pixel size, never DPI) | Window width |
| H | int | 720 – unbounded (screen px) | engine | Window height |
| 640, 360 | int | fixed | art bible 8.1 (decided) | Base logical resolution |
| k | int | 2 – unbounded | calculated | Screen px per base px for the world |

**Output Range:** Bounded below by the minimum window (1280×720 → `k = 2`; Section C PR-1); unbounded above (3840×2160 → 6; 7680×4320 → 12). Never fractional by construction; a window that is not an integer multiple of 640×360 yields a remainder (D.2) or an expanded field (D.3), never a fractional scale. `k` is re-derived on every `size_changed` (one `RESCALING` frame).
**Example:** 1920×1080 → `floor(min(3.0, 3.0)) = 3`. 1280×800 (Steam Deck) → `floor(min(2.0, 2.22)) = 2`. 3440×1440 → `floor(min(5.375, 4.0)) = 4`.

**D.2 Letterbox remainder under `keep`**

The `letterbox_bars` formula is defined as:

`bars_x = (W − 640·k) / 2`, `bars_y = (H − 360·k) / 2` (an odd remainder splits `floor` left/top, `ceil` right/bottom)

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| W, H, k | int | as D.1 | D.1 | Window and world scale |
| bars_x | int | 0 – unbounded (screen px) | calculated | Black bar width on each side |
| bars_y | int | 0 – unbounded (screen px) | calculated | Black bar height top and bottom |

**Output Range:** On the axis that limited `k` the remainder is strictly less than one base-picture step, so `bars_x < 320` or `bars_y < 180` on that axis; the other axis is unbounded (an ultrawide limited by height has 440 px bars each side). Bars are true black, outside the world `SubViewport`, never touched by the Filter pass or a jolt (Section C PR-2). What the engine actually fills the remainder with is NOT SOURCEABLE — step 11.
**Example:** 1600×900, `k = 2` → `bars_x = (1600 − 1280)/2 = 160`, `bars_y = (900 − 720)/2 = 90` (36 % of the screen black — the case `expand` exists for). 1365×768, `k = 2` → `bars_x = 42.5` → 42 left, 43 right; `bars_y = 24`.

**D.3 Expanded logical size under `expand`**

The `expanded_field` formula is defined as:

`L_w = min(2·floor(W / (2·k)), CAP_W)`, `L_h = min(2·floor(H / (2·k)), CAP_H)` (always even, so the camera is never on a half base pixel and `(L_w, L_h)/2` is an integer); residual bars `(W − L_w·k) / 2`, `(H − L_h·k) / 2` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| W, H, k | int | as D.1 | D.1 | Window and world scale |
| CAP_W, CAP_H | int | 960, 540 (safe 640–960 × 360–540) | tuning (G) | Cap on the default-zoom field = 1.5× base |
| L_w, L_h | int | 640–960 × 360–540 (base px) | calculated | World viewport logical size at default zoom |

**Output Range:** Never below 640×360 (because `k` was chosen as the largest integer that fits) and never above the cap; the cap exists to bound the information gap between displays (Section C PR-2) and the pass-B lit-pixel cost. When `k` is set manually below `k_auto`, aspect is forced to `keep` and this formula is not applied. An odd or half column from an awkward window is letterboxed, never rendered fractionally, and the logical size is never odd *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The residual per axis has two causes: (a) where the cap does not bind it is the rounding remainder, 0 to `2k − 1` screen px in total on that axis (split floor left/top, ceil right/bottom, so at most `k` on the ceil side); (b) where the cap binds on an axis (`2·floor(W/(2k)) > CAP_W`, i.e. W ≥ k·(CAP_W + 2), 2886 px at k = 3; likewise for H against CAP_H) the residual is `(W − CAP_W·k)/2` per side plus the rounding part, which is unbounded above (pillar or letter bars like `keep`'s, never touched by the Filter or a jolt, PR-2) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4)*.
**Example:** 3440×1440, `k = 4` → `L_w = min(860, 960) = 860`, `L_h = min(360, 540) = 360`; residual 0. 1280×800, `k = 2` → `640×400` (+40 rows). 1600×900, `k = 2` → `800×450`; residual 0. 1366×768, `k = 2` → `682×384` (was 683×384, an odd base that put the camera on a half pixel); residual 2 screen px, 1 each side. 2560×1080, `k = 3` → `852×360` (was 853); residual 4 px, 2 each side *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. 3840×1080, `k = 3` → `L_w = min(1280, 960) = 960`, `L_h = min(360, 540) = 360`; the cap binds, residual `(3840 − 960·3)/2 = 480` px each side (a 2880×1080 picture; `keep` would show a 1920×1080 picture with 960 px bars). 5120×1440 (32:9), `k = 4` → `L_w = min(1280, 960) = 960`; residual `(5120 − 3840)/2 = 640` each side *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4)*.

**D.4 HUD multiplier and HUD canvas**

The `hud_scale` formula (registry: `hud_multiplier`) is defined as:

`H_default = max(k, 3)`; `H ∈ {H_default, H_default + 1, …, 2·H_default}`; `cap_px = 7·H`; HUD canvas `(P_w / H, P_h / H)`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| k | int | 2 – unbounded | D.1 | World scale |
| H_default | int | 3 – unbounded | calculated | HUD scale at 100 % UI scale (C-R: never below 3×; floor = registry `HUD_MIN_SCALE`) |
| H | int | H_default – 2·H_default | Settings (G, labelled by `cap_px`) | HUD scale chosen by the player |
| cap_px | int | 21 – unbounded (screen px) | calculated | Instrument-face cap height on screen (7 art px, art bible 7.1) |
| P_w, P_h | int | screen px | D.2/D.3 | Picture size (window minus bars) |
| HUD canvas | float pair | may be fractional | calculated | HUD logical size in HUD px = `hud_rect / H`; the size of the HUD-root `Control` the pipeline root sets on every `RESCALING` (PR-12) — elements anchor to that Control, not to the root viewport *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** `cap_px ≥ 21` on every display by construction (the HR-12 floor at 1080p becomes the floor everywhere). `H = k` only when `k ≥ 3`; on 2× worlds `H > k` from MVP, which is what makes two integer scales in one window an MVP requirement (Section C PR-7/PR-12). The HUD canvas is fractional whenever `P / H` is not integer (1280/3 = 426.67 on the Deck), so HUD elements are anchor-relative, never absolutely positioned.
**Example:** Deck, `k = 2` → `H_default = 3`, range 3–6, `cap_px = 21` (≈ 2.5 mm at 215 ppi); HUD canvas at 1280×800 = 426.67 × 266.67. 1080p, `k = 3` → 3–6, labels "21 / 28 / 35 / 42 px". 1440p → 4–8.

**D.5 Zoom ladder and visible field** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check: rewritten from the single held step out to the user's integer ladder)*

The `zoom_ladder` formula (registry: `zoom_out_k`) is defined as:

`k_z ∈ {1, 2, …, k_base + 2}`, `k_z ← clamp(k_z ± 1, 1, k_base + 2)` on each `zoom_out` / `zoom_in` edge press — **the lower bound `1` is the default `ZOOM_MIN_K`; the effective ladder is `[ZOOM_MIN_K(k_base, target), k_base + 2]`, a static data table that withholds the bottom steps that fail D.16's cap (predicted-withheld table in D.16; at the large targets this is the expected outcome, not an edge case), and every "ladder" in this GDD is read as the effective ladder** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (persistent; `k_z ← k_base` on re-wake and at boot); field under `expand` `(2·floor(W / (2·k_z)), 2·floor(H / (2·k_z)))` (uncapped); field under `keep` `(2·floor(320·k_base / k_z), 2·floor(180·k_base / k_z))` — **always an even integer pair, the remainder letterboxed** (a 426.67×240 field on the Deck at `k_z` 3 and a 548.57×308.57 field at 2160p, `k_z` 7, are not renderable; they are 426×240 and 548×308), so the world picture is `field · k_z`, centred inside the HUD rect, which stays pinned to the `k_base` picture (remainder < 2·`k_z` px per axis) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; `tiles = field / 48`; sprite ratio `k_z / k_base`; field-scale factor `s = field_h / 360`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| k_auto | int | 2 – unbounded | D.1 | Automatic world scale for the window |
| k_base | int | 2 – k_auto | D.1 / Settings | **Effective base scale**: the manual `k` when Settings sets one below Auto (PR-2, PR-14; aspect is then `keep`), else `k_auto`. It is the default zoom step, the anchor of the ladder `[1, k_base + 2]` and the `k` of the HUD rect's picture, everywhere this GDD writes it; **`H_default` stays `max(k_auto, 3)`** — the HUD follows the display, not the player's manual world scale, so the registry `hud_multiplier` is unchanged *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| k_z | int | 1 – k_base + 2 | calculated (player's edge presses) | Current ladder step; `k_z = k_base` is default; never held, never saved |
| field | int pair (even) | 320×180 (k_base + 2 on a 1280×720 picture; 384×216 at 1080p) – 1920×1080 (1× at 1080p); 1440 rows at 1× on 1440p; 3840×2160 at 1× on 2160p *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from web-map-and-transit)* | calculated | Logical size of the world viewport at the step |
| tiles | float pair | 8 × 4.5 – 40 × 22.5 at 1080p | calculated | Visible field in 48-px tiles |
| k_z / k_base | float | 1/k_base – (k_base + 2)/k_base (0.33 – 1.67 at 1080p) | calculated | Apparent sprite scale; integer scaling never resamples, so marks are intact at every step — the *perceived* size below 50 % is the acuity question routed to the accessibility-specialist |
| s | float | 0.5 – 6.0 (180 – 2160 rows: 0.5 at `k_z` 4 on a 1280×720 `keep` picture, 6.0 at `k_z` 1 on 2160p; the 0.6 – 4.0 printed before excluded both ends) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from 2d-lighting-and-occlusion)* | calculated | Scales the solver's length parameters (`L_MAX`, `L_SLEW_MAX`, `LAG_CUT`, `JUMP_CUT`; D.7, D.10) and the inner box (D.8) by `s`, and `τ_follow` (D.6) by `min(1, s)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** Bounded on both ends by the ladder; a press beyond either end is ignored (`diag.zoom_step_refused`). The field is uncapped on purpose (Section C PR-6 C6): PR-2's 960×540 cap applies to the default step only. Each step is a one-frame cut; no intermediate scale exists; `Camera2D.zoom` stays `(1,1)`. `k_base + 2` at 1080p (5×) shows 8 × 4.5 tiles — less than a beam's reach — accepted as the player's own choice. 1× at 1080p is 9× the default field (lit pixels under pass A, Y-sorted content); 1× on the Deck is 4×.
**Example:** 1080p (`k_base = 3`): ladder 1 / 2 / **3** / 4 / 5 → fields 1920×1080 / 960×540 / 640×360 / 480×270 / 384×216 (40 × 22.5 … 8 × 4.5 tiles), sprite ratios 0.33 / 0.67 / 1 / 1.33 / 1.67, `s` = 3.0 / 1.5 / 1 / 0.75 / 0.6, and the `τ_follow` factor `min(1, s)` = 1 / 1 / 1 / 0.75 / 0.6 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. 1440p (`k_base = 4`): ladder 1–6; one step out (`k_z` = 3) is 852×480 = 17.75 × 10 tiles, two steps out (`k_z` = 2) is 1280×720 = 26.7 × 15 tiles *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Deck under `expand` (`k_base = 2`): ladder 1 / **2** / 3 / 4 → 1280×800 / 640×400 / 426×266 / 320×200 (all even); `keep` on the Deck, 1280×720 picture: 1 / **2** / 3 / 4 → 1280×720 / 640×360 / 426×240 (2 px residual per axis, 1 black column each side) / 320×180; 1× is 26.7 × 16.7 tiles — under pass A its render target is 4× the lit pixels of the default field (pass B: unchanged); see E for the draw-call and lighting consequence.

**D.6 Follow (first-order exponential approach)**

The `follow` formula (registry: `camera_follow`) is defined as:

`dt' = min(dt, DT_MAX)` with `DT_MAX` = 0.05 s, and the update is **skipped when `dt ≤ 0`**; `τ_follow_eff = τ_follow × min(1, s)`, `s = field_h / 360` (D.5; `s = 1` at the default step); **if `τ_follow_eff ≤ τ_EPS` (1e-4 s) then `a = 1`** (rigid follow, no `0/0`), else `a = 1 − exp(−dt' / τ_follow_eff)`; `f_n = f_{n−1} + a · (target_n − f_{n−1})` per axis; **settle: if `|target_n − f_n| < SETTLE_EPS` (= 0.5·Q) then `f_n = target_n`**; lag is measured **after** the update, `lag_n = |target_n − f_n|`, and for a constant-velocity target `lag_ss(dt) = v · τ_follow_eff · h / (e^h − 1)` with `h = dt/τ_follow_eff` — strictly below, and approaching as `dt → 0`, the limit `lag_∞ = v · τ_follow_eff` that Section G uses as the conservative bound; settle times `t_95 = 3 · τ_follow_eff` and `t_settle_f = τ_follow_eff · ln(|d₀| / SETTLE_EPS)` (the whole-pose settle is `max(t_settle_f, t_settle_L)`, `t_settle_L` from D.7, ≈ 2.5 s after a full aim reversal; `SETTLE_EPS` is per axis, on `f` and `L` separately) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and an exact-oracle time `t_exact = 10 · τ_follow_eff` (1.8 s at the default; at `s < 1` it scales with `τ_follow_eff`) after which, for a run that starts from rest at constant velocity, the unsettled part `lag_ss(dt) · e^(−t/τ_follow_eff)` is below 0.002 px at every shipped `dt`; **`t_95` is a 95 % claim and must not be used as the precondition of any floor-exact or equality oracle** (the D.6b and D.14b ACs use `t_exact`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; solver state is float64 scalars per axis *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check: field scaling added; Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `min(1, s)`, guards, settle, lag measured after the update, float64)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| dt | float | 0.004 – 0.05 s (clamped to `DT_MAX` = 0.05; a hitch longer than that advances the solver by 0.05 s and the remainder is a target discontinuity for D.10) | engine (render delta) | Frame time *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| τ_follow (registry: `FOLLOW_TAU`) | float | 0.18 s (safe 0 – < 0.20 at the 360-row field: 0.20 gives `108 > 108`, which fails the strict inner-box invariant; > 0.267 breaks the lag-cut invariant) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, registry alignment; Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: strict bound)* | tuning (G) | Follow time constant; 0 = rigid follow (the step-12 A/B) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| target_n | float pair | body px | kernel read view, interpolated (D.9) | Avatar's interpolated position this frame |
| f_n | float pair | body px | calculated | The follow component of the camera pose, before look-ahead, clamp and quantisation |
| v | float | 0 – v_max (base px/s) | Player Controller GDD (`v_walk` 120, `v_run` = `v_max` 180; registry `V_WALK` / `V_RUN`) | Avatar speed |
| s | float | 0.5 – 6.0 (180 – 2160 rows, D.5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, registry alignment)* | D.5 | Field-scale factor; `τ` scales by `min(1, s)` so the frame never keeps drifting longer than at the default step (the old `τ·s` meant a 1.6 s settle after stopping at `s = 3`, against Game Feel "Stillness"); lag as a fraction of the frame is constant for `s ≤ 1` and smaller for `s > 1`; the on-screen lag in screen px is constant for `s ≤ 1` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| lag_ss | float | 0 – v_max·τ_follow_eff | calculated | Distance the camera trails a constant-velocity avatar, measured after the update (D.6 formula; `lag_∞ = v·τ_follow_eff` is its dt→0 limit and upper bound) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| SETTLE_EPS | float | 0.5·Q base px (0.5 at `Q = 1`) | tuning (G) | Per-axis snap distance for `f` and `L` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| t_95 | float | 0 – 0.9 s | calculated | Time to close 95 % of a step |
| t_exact | float | 0 – 1.8 s | calculated | `10 · τ_follow_eff`; the time after which a rest-start constant-velocity run has lag within 0.002 px of `lag_ss(dt)`, the precondition for integer-exact oracles *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |

**Output Range:** `a ∈ (0, 1]` for any positive `dt'` (1 only for rigid follow), so `f` moves monotonically toward the target with zero overshoot — the camera never reverses unless the target does (C-R) — and equals the target exactly once the settle fires. For a target held constant through the frame, two 144-fps frames and one 60-fps frame of equal total `dt` give the same `f` to floating-point precision (per-frame factor 0.0884 at 60 fps, 0.0378 at 144 fps); for a **moving** target the per-frame update is not exact and the lag it holds depends on `dt`. **Where lag is measured: after the update, against `target_n`** (the pose the clamp and `P_lag` see, and the frame that is drawn). At `v_max` = 180 and `τ = 0.18`: 32.0 px at 240 fps, 31.8 at 144, 31.4 at 90, 30.9 at 60, 30.2 at 40, 29.5 at 30, 28.1 at `DT_MAX` — always below `lag_∞` = 32.4 (at `v_walk` = 120: 21.4 / 21.2 / 20.9 / 20.6 / 20.1 / 19.7 / 18.7 against 21.6). The 0.9 px spread between 60 and 144 fps is the whole frame-rate dependence on a continuous trace, which is why the contract is "within 1 base px" (C3). The **inner-box margin therefore does not erode at low fps down to about 15 fps** (the finding that it does measured the lag before the update; after it, the lag only shrinks) — **below 15 fps it does**: the catch-up cap moves the target up to 12 px per frame and the after-update lag reaches 37.4 px, so `37.4 + 72 = 109.4 > 108` and the box engages by 1.4 px (Section G) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: Section G keeps `lag_∞` as the build bound and additionally asserts the invariants with the after-update value at `DT_MAX`. `lag_∞` must stay below `LAG_CUT` (D.10) or a steady run cuts forever — 180 × 0.18 = 32.4 px < 48 at the default field; `LAG_CUT` scales by `s` and `τ` by `min(1, s)`, so the margin only widens at `s > 1` *(re-run 2026-10-26 — consistency check; Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **State transition at a ladder step:** `f` is an absolute body position and does not change, but the steady lag and the lean depend on `s`; on the `RESCALING` frame of a zoom step the solver sets `d = target − f ← d · τ_follow_eff(new) / τ_follow_eff(old)` and `L ← L · s_new / s_old`, so for `s ≤ 1` on both sides the avatar's offset from picture centre in screen px is unchanged by the step, and zooming in at `v_max` neither engages the inner box nor cuts *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
**Example:** A synthetic 40 px single-frame target step (no legal movement produces one; D.10) at 60 fps, default field: frame 1 `f` moves 3.54 px, after 6 frames (0.1 s) 17.05 px, and the 38.0 px level (95 %) is first reached on **frame 33** (38.12 px; frame 32 is 37.93 px); a 100 px step is within 5 px from frame 33 (4.71 px). Running at `v_max` = 180 px/s the camera trails by `lag_∞` = 32.4 px (30.9 px measured after the update at 60 fps); at walk (120 px/s) 21.6 px (20.6 at 60 fps) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. At the 270-row zoom-in field (`s = 0.75`, `τ_follow_eff = 0.135 s`) the same run trails by 24.3 px and the walk by 16.2 px — 9 % and 6 % of the field in both cases; at the 540-row zoom-out field (`s = 1.5`) `τ_follow_eff` stays 0.18 s (`min(1, s)`), the run trails by 32.4 px (6 % of the field) and the frame settles in the same 0.54 s as at the default step *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

**D.6b Offset-quantised follow (default candidate for the camera-solver story)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

The `follow_offset_quantised` formula is defined as:

`d_n = f_n − target_n` per axis (D.6's exponential state, float64, with its settle, `DT_MAX`, zoom rescale and cut handling, **unchanged**); `o_n = clamp(d_n + L_n, −B, +B)` (D.8's inner-box clamp applied to the **offset**: `c − target` in D.8 is exactly this `o_n`); **`pose_q = p + floor(o_n / Q) · Q`** per axis, where `p` is D.9's integer sprite position **taken from the same value the avatar's presentation node wrote this frame** (one camera host computes `p` once per frame and hands it to both the actor and the camera; the camera never recomputes it); the limits clamp (D.8b) is then applied to `pose_q`. The cut predicates (D.10) read `f_n`, `target_n` and `d_n` exactly as before, **never `pose_q`**, so a cut's meaning is unchanged. `τ_follow = 0` gives `d = 0`, `pose_q = p + floor(L / Q)·Q`: the pure rigid follow is the degenerate case of D.6b, not a separate branch.

**Why it exists.** In D.6 / D.9 the camera `floor(c)` and the sprite `floor(target)` are two independent floors of two floats whose fractional parts drift against each other, so the avatar's screen position `p − pose_q` changes about twice per base px of travel once the render rate exceeds the tick rate (C3's recorded risk, which predicts a miss at 90 and 144 fps). Here the avatar's screen position is `−floor(o_n/Q)·Q`: it changes only when the *offset itself* crosses a quantum, i.e. while the lag or the lean is changing, and is constant in a steady run. The lag is kept (it is carried by `d_n`); what changes is that the camera steps with the sprite instead of against it. The exponential-vs-rigid A/B of Open Question 12 is therefore a false choice; the three candidates for step 12 are D.6b (default), the D.6 / D.9 floor-of-sum, and D.6b at `τ_follow` = 0.

**Variables:** `d_n` (float pair, base px, calculated, `f − target`; `|d| ≤ lag_∞`), `o_n` (float pair, base px, calculated, `|o| ≤ B` per axis), `p` (int pair, D.9), `B` and `Q` (D.8, D.9). No new tuning knob.

**Output Range / settle.** Once `f` and `L` have settled, `d = 0` and `o = L_target`, so `pose_q = p + floor(L_target/Q)·Q` — this replaces D.9's "`pose_q = floor(target + L_target)`" for the D.6b path and differs from it by at most 1 base px (it is the same when `target` is integral, as it is whenever the avatar stands on a tick commit with `tick_alpha` = 1). **Offset-change count** (the C3 step-12 threshold): from `t_exact` = 10·`τ_eff` = 1.8 s (D.6; the run starts from rest — at `3·τ_eff` about 5 % of the lag is still unsettled and the offset still steps) of a constant-speed walk or run with a fixed aim and a fixed `dt`, `p − pose_q` changes **0 times** at any frame rate (`d_n` is constant, so `o_n` is) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; G4 recompute)*; with ±20 % `dt` jitter at most 2 per second; during a change of speed it changes at most `ceil(|Δlag|)` ≤ 33 times over the transient, monotonically.
**Example:** default field, `Q = 1`, `L = (0, 0)`, an eastward run at 180 px/s, 60 fps: `d_x = −30.9`, `o_x = −30.9`, `floor(o_x) = −31`; every frame `pose_q.x = p.x − 31`, so the avatar sits at a constant 31 px ahead of picture centre while the world scrolls 3 px per tick with it; at 144 fps `d_x = −31.8`, `floor = −32`, again constant. D.6 / D.9 for the same run: `pose_q.x = floor(target.x − 30.9)` against `p.x = floor(target.x)`, giving `p − pose_q` ∈ {30, 31} that alternates with the fractional parts.

**D.7 Look-ahead from aim**

The `look_ahead` formula is defined as:
