`L_MAX_eff = L_MAX × s`, `L_SLEW_MAX_eff = L_SLEW_MAX × s`, `s = field_h / 360` (D.5); lean magnitude `m = smoothstep(LEAN_R0·s, LEAN_R1·s, |aim|)` for a mouse aim and `m = 1` for a stick aim beyond its dead-zone; `L_target = L_MAX_eff · ((1 − w) · m · û(aim) + w · û_v)` where `û(aim) = aim / |aim|` if `|aim| ≥ ε`, else the previous `û` and `m`; `w = LOOKAHEAD_RUN_WEIGHT · r`, **`r = 1` if the Controller's committed `loco_state == RUN` and the avatar's committed position changed on at least one of the last 3 ticks, else `r = 0`**; **`û_v` = the unit vector of the Controller's committed movement octant centre** (8 constant directions; the octant the Controller publishes with `loco_state`), `w = 0` for a standing avatar, a walk, a carry-walk, a run pressed into a wall, and on the first frame after a load or wake (where `snap_baseline` has set `previous := current`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: run weighting, D6; re-review: `w` and `û_v` were keyed on the per-tick whole-pixel displacement `p_curr − p_prev`, which alternates on diagonals (a diagonal walk is 1.41 px per axis per tick, so the whole-pixel `|v|` jitters between about 1.4 and 2.8 and `r` between 0 and 0.93, and `û_v` wobbles about 11° from tick to tick), against "never pops"; the Controller's `loco_state` and octant are constant between player inputs, and the step in `L_target` they cause is smoothed by `L`'s own ease, `τ_lookahead` and the slew clamp)*; `ΔL = (L_target − L_{n−1}) · (1 − exp(−dt' / τ_lookahead))` (`dt'` as D.6; skipped when `dt ≤ 0`), **clamped by vector length**: if `|ΔL| > L_SLEW_MAX_eff · dt'` then `ΔL ← ΔL · L_SLEW_MAX_eff · dt' / |ΔL|`; `L_n = L_{n−1} + ΔL`; **settle** per axis: if `|L_target − L_n| < SETTLE_EPS` on an axis then that component of `L_n` `= L_target` *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check: `L_MAX_eff` per the zoom-ladder decision; Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: magnitude, vector slew clamp, guards, settle)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| aim | float pair | any (body px) | Player Controller, **local raw aim** (not the coalesced intent); read regardless of beam lit state; for a mouse it is `cursor_world_aim − AIM_ORIGIN` (D.14b; `AIM_ORIGIN = floor(pos) + (0, AIM_ORIGIN_Y)`, PC-5, computed by the Controller) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4)* — **never** from `mouse_to_world_view`, so the look-ahead cannot feed back into it | Where the chest-mounted beam points *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| m | float | 0 – 1 | calculated | Lean magnitude: a smoothstep of the aim vector's length for a mouse (so a cursor crossing the avatar does not swing the frame by `2·L_MAX`), 1 for a stick *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| LEAN_R0, LEAN_R1 | float | 16, 48 base px at the 360-row field (safe R0 8 – 24, R1 32 – 72, R1 > R0); `× s` | tuning (G) | Aim magnitude at which the lean starts and reaches full strength; R0 sits above PC-5's 8 px mouse dead-zone *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| ε | float | 0.01 | fixed | Dead threshold against a zero or NaN aim |
| LOOKAHEAD_RUN_WEIGHT (`W_RUN`) | float | 0.50 default (safe 0 – 0.75; 0 = no run weighting, the pre-ruling behaviour) | tuning (G); [PLACEHOLDER — default derived below, playtest-tuned at spike step 12 and the MVP playtest] | Share of the lean target that follows the direction of movement at full run speed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| loco_state, octant, û_v | enum, int 0..7, unit float pair | `loco_state` ∈ the Controller's enum (`RUN` is the only value that gives `r = 1`); `û_v` one of 8 constant unit vectors (diagonals (±0.7071, ±0.7071)) | Player Controller's committed `controller.state` (published read; the same commit the follow target is read at); never the camera and never a per-tick displacement | The gait the player chose and the direction of the committed movement octant; the only new input to D.7 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: replaces the displacement-keyed `v`, `V_WALK`, `V_RUN` and `smoothstep` ramp)* |
| moved | bool | — | `p_curr ≠ p_prev` on any of the last 3 ticks (Controller commits) | A run pressed into a wall (displacement 0, `loco_state` still `RUN`) does not lean; a window of 3 ticks cannot flicker on a diagonal, where every tick moves at least 1 px on each axis *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| r | float | {0, 1} | calculated | `1` iff `loco_state == RUN` and `moved`, else `0`; a binary gait flag that changes only on a player input, so it has no per-tick jitter; the on-screen smoothing is `L`'s ease (`τ_lookahead`, slew clamp), so the frame never pops *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| L_MAX | float | 72 base px at the 360-row field (`LOOKAHEAD_PX`; safe 48 – 75 — the inner-box invariant `INNER_BOX_FRAC·field_h > v_max·τ_follow + L_MAX_eff` (INNER_BOX_FRAC = 0.30, D.8) binds at 75.6 for `v_max` = 180, so the 2.5-tile bound is unreachable; 0 under reduced motion) | tuning (G) / Settings | Maximum look-ahead distance = 1.5 tiles |
| L_MAX_eff | float | `L_MAX × s`: 43.2 at 216 rows, 54 at 270, 72 at 360, 80 at 400 (Deck), 108 at 540, 144 at 720, 160 at 800 (Deck 1×), 216 at 1080 | calculated | The bound actually applied at the current ladder step |
| τ_lookahead | float | 0.40 s (safe 0.2 – 0.8) | tuning (G) | Ease time constant (time, not length — does not scale) |
| L_SLEW_MAX | float | 144 base px/s at the 360-row field (safe 96 – 240); `L_SLEW_MAX_eff = L_SLEW_MAX × s` | tuning (G) | Rate clamp so a flick never out-paces the pan ceiling |
| L_n | float pair | |L| ≤ L_MAX_eff | calculated | Look-ahead offset added to `f_n` |

**Output Range:** `|L| ≤ L_MAX_eff` always (the target is a convex combination `(1 − w)·m·û(aim) + w·û_v` of two vectors of length ≤ 1, so it is inside the circle of radius `L_MAX_eff` for any `w ∈ [0, 1]`, and the approach is contractive) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: run weighting)*; as a fraction of the frame the bound is the same 20 % of the field height at every step. The slew clamp acts on the vector's length, so a diagonal swing cannot exceed `L_SLEW_MAX_eff` (the per-axis clamp let it run about 41 % faster) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Without the slew clamp a 180° flick starts at `2·L_MAX / τ = 360 px/s`; with it, the first `(144 − 57.6)/144 ≈ 0.6 s` run at the clamp and the exponential tail takes over when the gap falls below `L_SLEW_MAX · τ = 57.6 px`, so a full reversal reaches 95 % in ≈ 1.4 s. Look-ahead is player-caused and is excluded from the cut predicates (D.10).
**Run-weighting derivation and constraints** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, D6)*: the offset of the avatar from picture centre is `lag·û_v − L` (the avatar is ahead of the follow term by `lag_∞` along its motion, and the pose adds `L`). Constraint 1: `|L| ≤ L_MAX_eff` for every `w ∈ [0, 1]` (convexity, above), so the invariant `INNER_BOX_FRAC·field_h > v_max·τ_follow_eff + L_MAX_eff` of D.8 is untouched, because `|lag·û_v − L| ≤ lag + |L|`. Constraint 2 (the purpose): with the aim held opposite to the run (`m = 1`, the retreat), the net lean along the run is `L_MAX_eff · (w − (1 − w)) = L_MAX_eff · (2w − 1)`; it is zero at `w = 0.5`, so the **default `W_RUN` = 0.5 is the smallest weight at which a runner aiming straight back is no longer pushed to look backward** (at `w < 0.5` the retreat still leans against the run by `(1 − 2w)·L_MAX_eff`; at `w > 0.5` a backward aim leans forward and the frame no longer shows where the beam points). The range stops at 0.75 so that a full-run frame never leans more than half of `L_MAX_eff` against the aim. **Worked example** (60 fps, default field, `L_MAX_eff` = 72, `v_max` = 180 px/s so `r = 1`, `lag_∞` = 32.4 px, `W_RUN` = 0.5, `w` = 0.5): running east and aiming west (`m = 1`): `L_target = 72·(0.5·(−1, 0) + 0.5·(1, 0)) = (0, 0)`; aiming east: `(72, 0)`; aiming north: `72·(0.5·(0, −1) + 0.5·(1, 0)) = (36, −36)`, `|L| = 50.9`. The avatar's offset from picture centre, `lag·û_v − L`, is `(32.4 − 72, 0)` = (−39.6, 0) aiming east, `(32.4, 0)` aiming west and `(−3.6, 36)` aiming north — all inside the ±192 × ±108 box, against 104.4 for the worst case before the ruling (aim opposite to the run at `w` = 0). Walking (`loco_state == WALK`, `r` = 0) gives `w` = 0 and the pre-ruling lean exactly. When the run stops, `r` becomes 0 and `L` eases to the pure-aim target over `τ_lookahead` under the slew clamp. **Example:** Aim north, `L = (0, −72)` (standing, `w` = 0): a 6-tile north beam shows 3.75 + 1.5 = 5.25 of its tiles instead of 3.75. Flick to south: `L_target = (0, +72)`; frame 1 at 60 fps the unclamped step would be `144 × 0.0408 = 5.9 px`, the clamp allows `144 × 0.0167 = 2.4 px`, so the clamp governs; after 0.6 s `L ≈ (0, +14.4)`; after 1.4 s `L ≈ (0, +64.2)` (the 95 % point of the 144 px swing, `L.y = +64.8`, is reached at ≈ 1.43 s) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: +68 → +64.2)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**D.8 Inner-box clamp**

The `inner_box_clamp` formula is defined as:

`B = (floor(INNER_BOX_FRAC · L_w), floor(INNER_BOX_FRAC · L_h))` with `INNER_BOX_FRAC = 0.30`; `c = clamp(f + L, target − B, target + B)` (per axis); steady-state invariant `INNER_BOX_FRAC · field_h > v_max · τ_follow_eff + L_MAX_eff` *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check: constant named for the registry)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| L_w, L_h | int | any ladder field — 216 to 1080 rows at 1080p; 200 to 800 on the Deck (D.5) | D.3 / D.5 | World viewport logical size at the current step |
| INNER_BOX_FRAC | float | 0.30, fixed (box = 0.60 of frame; registry `INNER_BOX_FRAC`) | gameplay-programmer (decided) | Half-extent fraction |
| B | int pair | (192, 108) at 640×360; (258, 108) at 860×360 | calculated | Half-extents of the box the avatar may not leave |
| f + L | float pair | body px | D.6 + D.7 | Unclamped pose |
| target | float pair | body px | D.6 | Avatar position |
| c | float pair | body px | calculated | Clamped pose, before quantisation |

**Output Range:** `|c − target| ≤ B` per axis, so the avatar is never drawn outside the central 60 % of the frame whatever the lag or look-ahead — **while `FOLLOWING`; while the camera is `CLAMPED` on an axis the limits clamp wins and the guarantee is suspended on that axis (D.8b)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. At the defaults the clamp is inactive in steady state (`lag_∞ + L_MAX = 32.4 + 72 = 104.4 < 108` at the 360-row field; because `L_MAX` and `B` scale by `s` and `τ_follow` by `min(1, s)`, the same inequality holds at every ladder field — 270: 24.3 + 54 = 78.3 < 81; 216: 19.4 + 43.2 = 62.6 < 64.8; 540: 32.4 + 108 = 140.4 < 162; 720: 32.4 + 144 = 176.4 < 216; 1080: 32.4 + 216 = 248.4 < 324; Deck 400: 32.4 + 80 = 112.4 < 120; Deck 800: 32.4 + 160 = 192.4 < 240 *(re-run 2026-10-26 — consistency check; re-run 2026-10-27 — design review for `min(1, s)`; with the after-update lag at `DT_MAX`, 28.1 px, every margin only widens)*); it engages only during the transient after a target jump below the cut thresholds, where it is the thing that keeps the avatar in frame. The clamp acts on the pose; the cut predicates read the unclamped `f` (reconciliation 4). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
**Example:** A synthetic jump of 40 px **south**, opposite the lean (no legal walk makes one; a weld delta or a Body shove could) while `L = (0, −72)` (aim north, pose 72 px north of `f`): the avatar's offset from the unclamped pose is `40 + 72 = 112 > 108`, so `c` is pinned at 108 px north of the avatar (`c.y = target.y − 108`) for the first frames, then released as `f` catches up. A 40 px jump **north**, toward the lean, gives `|target − c| = |−40 + 72| = 32` and then 25 on the next frames: the box never engages *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier example jumped north, which cannot engage the box)*.

**D.8b Limits clamp and its order against the inner box** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

The `limits_clamp` formula is defined as:

**Order: (1) the inner-box clamp (D.8) on the offset, then (2) the limits clamp on the pose; the limits win.** `c_1 = target + clamp(d + L, −B, +B)` (D.8; in the D.6b variant the offset form `o`); `[lo, hi] = [rect_eff.min + field/2, rect_eff.max − field/2]` per axis (`lo = hi = rect_eff` centre on an axis where `field` is larger than the rect, C8); **`c = clamp(c_1, lo, hi)`** per axis, then quantisation (D.9). **The clamp acts on the pose, not on the follow target**, so `f` and the cut predicates never see it (reconciliation 4 stands) and the aim mapping's `f_q` is unaffected. In a static rect this is continuous: walking into a wall slows the pose to a stop at `hi` with no pop (the unclamped pose crosses `hi` continuously). **A change of the rect never pops the pose**: the solver keeps an eased copy `rect_eff` whose four edges approach the `PROJECTOR`'s rect with the D.6 factor (`rect_eff ← rect_eff + a·(rect − rect_eff)`, settle at `SETTLE_EPS`), so a pose that falls outside the new rect is carried to its edge along the same exponential — `t_95 = 3·τ_follow_eff` = 0.54 s, zero overshoot — and `rect_eff` snaps to the rect on a `CUT`, on a `RESCALING` frame (a zoom step is a cut by C6) and on load. **`CLAMPED` suspends the 60 % guarantee** on the clamped axis: with the avatar near a hull edge the clamped centre can sit up to `field/2` from it, so the avatar may leave the central 60 % (D.8's `|c − target| ≤ B` holds only while `FOLLOWING`); it stays inside the picture because the avatar is inside the rect, and no prompt is drawn outside the picture (C9). `diag.limits_engaged` records the axis and frames.
**Example:** 360-row field (`field_h` = 360), rect rows 0 – 1 000, `lo_y` = 180, `hi_y` = 820; the avatar at `y` = 990 with `f` = 960 and `L_y` = +72: `c_1.y` = 990 + clamp(−30 + 72, −108, 108) = 1 032; `c.y = hi_y` = 820 — the avatar is 170 px below centre, inside the 180-px half-field but outside the 108-px box (the stated `CLAMPED` exception). A detach that moves `rect.max.y` from 1 000 to 700 with the pose at 820: `hi_y` eases from 820 to 520 and the pose follows it, 95 % of the 300 px in 0.54 s (frame 33 at 60 fps), no frame jumps by more than `a·300` = 26.5 px.

**D.9 Quantisation and sprite placement**

The `quantise` formula is defined as:

`pose_q = floor(c / Q) · Q` with `Q ∈ {1, 1/k}`; sprite `p = floor(prev + (curr − prev) · tick_alpha)`; container offset `o = −round((1 + frac(pose_q)) · k)` screen px if `Q = 1/k`, else 0

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| c | float pair | body px | D.8 | Clamped pose |
| Q | float | 1 (MVP) or 1/k | tuning (G, set-once per build) | Camera quantum |
| pose_q | float pair | multiples of Q | calculated | Camera position written to `Camera2D` (floored; `floor`, never `round`, everywhere) |
| prev, curr | float pair | body px | kernel read views (CR-10) | Committed positions of the previous and current tick |
| tick_alpha | float | 0.0 – 1.0 | kernel D.3 (clamped) | Interpolation fraction |
| p | int pair | body px | calculated | Integer base-px position every presentation node writes |
| o | int pair | −2k … −k screen px | calculated | `SubViewportContainer` position under the `1/k` quantum (world viewport rendered 2 base px larger) |

**Output Range:** `p` is always an integer in the body frame — the viewport snap flags are a safety net (Section C PR-4 rule 2). With `Q = 1` the picture scrolls in whole base px (k screen px); with `Q = 1/k` it scrolls in screen px while every item stays on one shared base grid. `tick_alpha` is produced clamped by the kernel driver; a value outside [0, 1] is impossible by D.3 and is asserted (E). Once `f` and `L` have settled (D.6, D.7) `pose_q` is constant and equals `floor(target + L_target)` (on the D.6b path, `p + floor(L_target/Q)·Q`, within 1 base px of it) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `pose_q` is quantised per axis from the sum `c`, never from a term, so the floor of an asymptotic term cannot land a pixel late *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
**Example:** `prev = 100`, `curr = 102`, `tick_alpha = 0.42` → `p = floor(100.84) = 100`; at `tick_alpha = 0.6`, `p = 101`. Camera `c = 311.7`, `Q = 1` → `pose_q = 311`; `Q = 1/3` → `311.667`, `o = −round(1.667 × 3) = −5` screen px.

**D.10 Pan-speed ceiling and the cut predicates**

The `camera_cut` formula (registry: `camera_cut_predicates`) is defined as:

`v_follow = |f_n − f_{n−1}| / dt`; `P_pan = (v_follow > 1.25 · v_max)` held continuously for `≥ T_PAN`, **evaluated in the integer domain**: the hold time is a running `int64` sum of `dt_us = ceil(dt · 10⁶)` (whole µs, reset to 0 on the first frame `v_follow` is at or below the ceiling) compared with `T_PAN_us = 100 000`, so the boundary never depends on float summation order and `T_PAN` is exactly 3 frames at 30 fps, 6 at 60, 9 at 90, 15 at 144 (14.4 rounded up) and 24 at 240 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `P_lag = (|target_n − f_n| > LAG_CUT)`; `P_jump = (|target_n − target_{n−1}| ≥ JUMP_CUT)`; `cut = P_pan ∨ P_lag ∨ P_jump ∨ body_transfer_cut ∨ loading_to_running`, where `body_transfer_cut` is a `BODY_TRANSFER` that is a pod or transit transfer, or a weld/detach whose hull jump is not guaranteed zero; a weld/detach `BODY_TRANSFER` carrying a `frame_delta_px` is a **rebase** (C5): `f`, `target` and `c` shift by the delta, lag and `L` are kept, and the cut predicates compare deltas taken after the shift, so the delta itself never fires `P_jump` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; at a ladder step `LAG_CUT_eff = LAG_CUT × s`, `JUMP_CUT_eff = JUMP_CUT × s` and `τ_follow_eff = τ_follow × min(1, s)` (D.5, D.6) while the ceiling `1.25 · v_max` does **not** scale (it can never sit below `v_max`); invariants `LAG_CUT > v_max · τ_follow` and `INNER_BOX_FRAC · field_h > v_max · τ_follow_eff + L_MAX_eff` (D.8; INNER_BOX_FRAC = 0.30) — scale-free for `s ≤ 1` (every length term carries `s`) and with more margin for `s > 1` *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check; Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `min(1, s)`)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| f_n | float pair | body px | D.6 (unclamped follow term, without look-ahead or jolt) | What the predicates read |
| v_max | float | 180 base px/s (3 px/tick) | Player Controller GDD (registry `V_RUN`) | Avatar's fastest locomotion |
| 1.25 | float | fixed (safe 1.1 – 1.5) | ux-designer (decided; "walk" → `v_max`, reconciliation 1) | Pan ceiling multiplier |
| T_PAN | float | 0.10 s (safe 0.05 – 0.2) | tuning (G) | Sustain time before a pan cut (= 6 frames at 60 fps, 15 at 144, 3 at 30) |
| LAG_CUT | int | 48 base px at the 360-row field (safe > v_max·τ_follow = 32.4 … 96); `× s` at other steps | tuning (G) | Lag beyond which the camera cuts instead of panning |
| JUMP_CUT | int | 48 base px at the 360-row field (safe 24 – 96); `× s` at other steps | tuning (G) | Single-frame target displacement that cuts immediately |
| cut | bool | — | calculated | Solver state reset to the target this frame: `f` to the target, `L` reset to 0 and eased in on a wake or load (kept on a solver-initiated cut), jolt cleared (the earlier "look-ahead at full value" is superseded by the States table `CUT` row) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

**Output Range:** Because D.6 never exceeds the target's speed in steady state, `P_pan` can only fire on a target discontinuity: a jump `J` gives an initial follow speed `J / τ_follow` that decays as `exp(−t/τ)`; it stays above the ceiling for `T_PAN` only if `J > J_pan`, where the named quantity **`J_pan = 1.25·v_max·τ_follow·exp(T_PAN/τ_follow)`** = `225 × 0.18 × 1.743 ≈ 70.6 px` at the defaults (84.7 px at multiplier 1.5; at the 270-row field with `τ_follow_eff = 0.135`: `225 × 0.135 × 2.097 ≈ 63.7 px` against `JUMP_CUT_eff = 36`) *(re-run 2026-10-26 — consistency check)* — so at the defaults `P_jump` (48 px) fires first and `P_pan` is the backstop for multi-frame drifts that need a named source (a Body shove sustained over several frames; hitch recovery) — a `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` catch-up delivers at most 12 px in one frame and never arms it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. `P_lag` covers a target that out-runs the follow for any reason. The invariant guarantees a legal run never cuts: 48 > 32.4 at `v_max` = 180; Section G flags any `(τ_follow, v_max)` pair that breaks it.
**Example:** Re-wake: the target moves ≈ 2 000 px in one frame → `P_jump` → cut. A synthetic 40 px burst (no legal walk produces one; a weld delta or a Body shove could): `P_jump` false; initial `v_follow = 40 / 0.18 = 222 px/s` in the `dt → 0` limit (per frame, as measured: 212 px/s at 60 fps, 218 at 144), **below** the 225 px/s ceiling at every rate, so `P_pan` never arms → no cut *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; D.8 pins the avatar inside the box while D.6 eases. A 47 px burst (just under `JUMP_CUT`): initial `v_follow = 261 px/s > 225` in the limit (249 per frame at 60 fps, 256 at 144), back below the ceiling at `t = 0.18 × ln(261/225) = 0.027 s` in continuous time (2 frames = 0.033 s at 60 fps, 4 frames = 0.028 s at 144) `< T_PAN` → no cut *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Running at 180 px/s: lag 32.4 px, `v_follow = 180 < 225` → no predicate fires. *(Re-run 2026-10-26 — consistency check.)*

**D.11 Jolt (default 0)**

The `jolt` formula is defined as:

`j(t) = round(J_0 · max(0, 1 − t / T_J) · σ(t))`, `σ(t) = (−1)^{floor(2 · f_J · t)}` (`round` = round half away from zero, applied to the signed product; the sign term was `s` in the first draft, which D.5/D.6 use for the field-scale factor *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*); on overlapping events `J_0 = max(J_0, J_new)` (never summed); `|J_0| ≤ JOLT_MAX`; applied as `Camera2D.offset` inside the world `SubViewport`, excluded from `world_to_hud` (D.14)

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| J_0 | int | 0 – JOLT_MAX | event (P1 physical only) | Initial amplitude; **default knob 0 at MVP**; 0 under reduced motion |
| JOLT_MAX (registry: `JOLT_PX`; default 0, cap 2) | int | 2 base px (safe 0 – 2) | tuning (G) / Settings | Hard amplitude cap |
| j_berth | int | 0 – min(`BERTH_SETTLE_PX`, JOLT_MAX) | registry `BERTH_SETTLE_PX` (Web Map & Transit) | One-shot Settle on `BERTHED` (half on the release): a single damped displacement, no alternation; gated by C7's MVP default 0, so 0 at MVP (Open Question 16, resolved: held to VS-A); 0 under reduced motion *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from web-map-and-transit)* |
| T_J | float | ≤ 0.30 s (safe 0.1 – 0.3) | tuning (G) | Decay duration |
| f_J | float | ≤ 8 Hz (safe 4 – 8) | tuning (G) | Alternation frequency |
| t | float | 0 – T_J s | `present_time` since the event | Elapsed time |
| j(t) | int | −2 … +2 base px | calculated | Integer offset added after quantisation |

**Output Range:** Integer base px, bounded by `JOLT_MAX`, zero after `T_J`; alternation at `f_J ≤ 8 Hz` is positional, not luminous, so the flash clamp (D.15) does not apply — flagged for the accessibility-specialist as 7.3 flags the tremor. Never caused by world state (Section C C1/C7).
**Example:** `J_0 = 2`, `T_J = 0.3`, `f_J = 8`: sampled at `t` = 0, 62.5, 125, 187.5, 250 and 312.5 ms the decay factors are 1, 0.79, 0.58, 0.375, 0.17 and 0, so `j = +2, −2, +1, −1, 0, 0`, gone by 0.3 s *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (the first draft listed +2, −2, +2, −1, +1, −1, 0, which does not follow from the formula).

**D.12 Y-sort key (up-screen convention)**

The `sort_key` formula is defined as:

`sort_y(actor) = y_foot`; `sort_y(wall with footprint rows [y_0, y_0 + 24)) = y_0 + 24`; `sort_y(wall-mounted item) = sort_y(host face)`; face rows `[sort_y − 72, sort_y)`; cap rows `[sort_y − 96, sort_y − 72)` (for a wall; **a door's drawn rows are fixed by its cell, `[y_0 − 48, y_0 + 24)` plus the cap, whichever sort key it has — only its key moves when it opens**); `sort_y(door or doorway with area > 0) = y_0` (footprint north edge; a closed door is a wall, `y_0 + 24`); draw order ascending by the key **`(sort_y, class_rank, entity_id)`** — `class_rank` 0 wall-face tiles < 1 wall-mounted < 2 doors, props, machinery, deployables, empty suits, web < 3 actors and horrors; `entity_id` the publisher's globally unique, namespace-tagged persistent ID (not a bare per-block instance ID); never `x`, never creation order *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| y_foot | int | body px | presentation (D.9 `p`) | Actor's floored foot row |
| y_0 | int | multiples of 24 | layout | North edge of a wall's floor footprint |
| 24, 72, 96 | int | fixed | art bible 8.2 (set-once) | Footprint depth, face height, total screen rows |
| sort_y | int | body px | calculated | The floor-contact line every Y-sorted item sorts by |
| class_rank | enum | 0 wall-face tiles < 1 wall-mounted < 2 doors, props, machinery, deployables, empty suits, web < 3 actors, horrors | Section C PR-5 | First tie-breaker on an equal row: independent of `x` (a tile's `x` is its left edge and an actor's its foot-centre, so `x` cannot order them), so an actor flush south of a prop is in front at every `x` and the order never changes as it walks along the prop *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| entity_id | int64 | persistent, **globally unique across publishers** | publisher-allocated and namespace-tagged (the actor publisher's tagged form of Body's block-local `actor_id`, BDY-20; Layout's namespace-tagged `entity_id`, LY-31; each other publisher registers its namespace tag with this GDD; the kernel allocates no IDs, CR-8) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | Second tie-breaker among items of one class; stable across save and reload, unlike a creation counter. A per-block instance ID without a namespace tag is not valid: IDs from two blocks collide across `class_rank` 2, and the fallback to tree order is not save-stable. If two items still share `(sort_y, class_rank, entity_id)` the pipeline records `diag.sort_id_collision = [id, publishers]` (a defect, not a tie) and orders them by `(publisher namespace tag, tree order)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; design review)* |
| area | int | 0 – `area_max` | Graph opening (Layout LY-9) | `area > 0` makes a door or doorway passable, so it sorts by its footprint's north edge *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** Integer rows, so an interpolated actor and a wall never swap on a sub-pixel difference. Predicate the spike tests (step 5): an actor is hidden by a wall iff `y_foot < sort_y(wall)` and their rows and columns overlap — standing just north of a wall (behind it) is hidden; standing just south (in front, `y_foot ≥ sort_y`) draws over the face. Under the convention the face covers 72 rows of floor **north** of the footprint (the room behind); the one-clause amendment of 8.2 has landed (art bible 8.2, user decision 2026-10-26). A passable door is the exception that proves the predicate: with `sort_y = y_0` an actor in the gap (`y_foot ∈ [y_0, y_0 + 24)`) or south of it draws over the frame, an actor north of it (`y_foot < y_0`) is behind it, and the head is visible throughout the crossing from either side *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
**Example:** Wall footprint rows [240, 264): `sort_y = 264`, face rows [192, 264), cap rows [168, 192). Rows 240–263 are collision, so the first legal row south of the wall is `y_foot = 264`: it sorts equal to the wall and the class-rank tie-break (PR-5) draws the tile layer (rank 0) first and the actor (rank 3) after → the actor is drawn over the face, standing at the wall's base — correct. Actor at `y_foot = 232` (the foot box's north rest row, 240 − FOOT_BOX_H 8, registry `foot_box`; just north, in the room behind): drawn before the wall; its rows [177, 232) overlap the face rows [192, 264) → hidden behind the wall — correct. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from ship-body-and-interior-physics)* An actor reported at `y_foot = 250` is inside the footprint and is a collision defect, not a sorting case (E) — unless the footprint is a passable door's gap, where it is a legal crossing. Equal-row examples *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: an actor at `y_foot = 264` flush south of a console whose footprint rows are `[216, 264)` (rank 2, `sort_y` 264) sorts after it at every `x`; the same console and a closed door both lose to an actor on the same row; a doorway with `area > 0` and footprint rows `[240, 264)` has `sort_y` 240, so actors at `y_foot` 232 (behind), 240 and 250 (in the gap), and 264 (south) draw behind, over, over and over the frame respectively.

**D.13 z-band fallback** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

The `z_band` formula is defined as:

`band = floor(sort_y / BAND_H) − band_0` (world-anchored; **`band_0 = floor(union_min_y / BAND_H)`, `union_min_y` = the first row of the union rect of every body drawn in the interior view** — the loaded body and any docked, welding or detaching body, not the loaded body alone *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: anchored to the loaded body only, a docked or detaching body above it gave `band < 0`, so `z` collided with the floor and the marks bands)*); `z = Z_WIN_LO + band`; `N_bands_body = ceil(union_rows / BAND_H) + 2` (`union_rows` the height of the union rect); bound `Z_WIN_LO + N_bands_body ≤ Z_WIN_HI < range_z_max`; bands in view `N_view = ceil((L_h + 96) / BAND_H) + 1`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| sort_y | int | body px | D.12 | Item's sort row |
| union_min_y, union_rows (was `body_min_y`, `body_rows`) | int | body px; up to 21 336 rows | Body read views (the union of the rects of every body drawn: loaded, docked, welding, detaching) | The union rect's first row and height; bands are anchored to it, not to the camera or to one body; **recomputed, and every banded item's `z` rewritten once, on dock, weld and detach (the same events that rebase the body frame)**, never per frame; `band ≥ 0` is asserted for every item (`diag.zband_negative` and the fallback refuses rather than clamping) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| BAND_H | int | 24 base px (set-once; = wall footprint depth) | tuning (G) | Band height — a wall is alone in its band |
| Z_WIN_LO, Z_WIN_HI | int | 10, 899 (proposed) | → ADR | The dedicated z window of the Y-sort row; caps (+900) and dust (+910) sit above it |
| L_h | int | 360 – 2160 | D.3 / D.5 | Logical height of the field, including the zoomed field (used only for `N_view`) |
| range_z_max | int | 1024 `[BIN]` default | `Light2D.range_z_max` | Lights ignore items outside their z range |
| z | int | Z_WIN_LO … Z_WIN_LO + N_bands_body | calculated | `z_index` of a band node, written when an item's band changes |

**Output Range:** A 60-tile-tall body (2 880 rows) needs 122 bands, `z ≤ 132`; the window holds 889 bands = 21 336 rows (444 tiles), so any shippable body fits, and a body or a `BAND_H` that would exceed it (`BAND_H` 1 on a 2 880-row body needs 2 882) refuses the fallback (`diag.zband_overflow`) rather than clamping `z`. Bands in view: 20 at `L_h = 360` (`ceil(456/24) + 1`), 39 at the Deck's 1× field (800), 65 at 1440 rows, 95 at 2160 — the count that bounds the per-band draw cost. Far inside ±1024 and `CANVAS_ITEM_Z_MAX = 4096` `[BIN]`. The fallback is only engaged if 8.12 step 5 shows the Y-sorted layer above ≈ 300 draws **and** its go/no-go passes; it changes nothing visible when the mapping is right. Not recomputed per frame: bands are anchored to the body *(re-run 2026-10-26 — consistency check; rewritten 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
**Example:** union rect `union_min_y = 0` (one body), wall `sort_y = 264` → `band = 11`, `z = 21`; **a docked body whose rows start at `y = −240`** (above the loaded body): `union_min_y = −240`, `band_0 = floor(−240/24) = −10`, a wall of the loaded body at `sort_y = 264` → `band = 11 − (−10) = 21`, `z = 31`, and a wall of the docked body at `sort_y = −216` → `band = −9 + 10 = 1`, `z = 11` — never below `Z_WIN_LO` = 10, where the earlier loaded-body-only anchor (`band_0 = 0`) gave `band = −9`, `z = 1`, colliding with the floor (z 0 – 1) and the marks band *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; an actor at `y_foot = 270` → `floor(270/24) = 11`, `z = 21`, same band, ordered within it by the PR-5 key; an actor at `y_foot = 290` → band 12, `z = 22`, drawn after every band-11 item; none of these change when the camera moves. The wall's face rows `[192, 264)` and cap rows `[168, 192)` reach into bands 8–10, but sorting is by `sort_y`, so the overhang adds no bands to the count *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

**D.14 World-to-HUD projection (unjolted)**

The `world_to_hud` formula is defined as:

`pose_i = floor(pose_q)` and `o_f = −round(frac(pose_q) · k_z)` (both unjolted; `o_f` is 0 at `Q = 1`); `q = (p − pose_i + (L_w, L_h)/2) · k_z + o_f` is the **picture-relative** screen position (origin: the world picture's top-left); `q_hud = q + rem` with `rem = floor((hud_rect − world_picture) / 2)` (0 at the default step, ≤ 2·k_z − 1 px per axis at a ladder step, PR-1 / D.3); `h = floor(q_hud / H)`, relative to the **HUD-root Control**, a Control the pipeline root sizes to `hud_rect / H` (a possibly fractional size) at the HUD rect's origin on every `RESCALING` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| p | int pair | body px | D.9 | Object's integer body-frame position |
| pose_q | float pair | body px | D.9, **without** D.11's jolt | Camera position (unjolted — C-R); its fraction enters once, through `o_f` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| (L_w, L_h) | int pair | even logical size | D.3 / D.5 | World viewport logical size (renamed from `L`, which D.7 uses for the look-ahead offset); even by D.3, so `(L_w, L_h)/2` is an integer *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| k_z | int | D.1 / D.5 | D.1 | World scale at the current ladder step |
| hud_rect, world_picture | int rects | screen px | D.3 / D.5 | The HUD rect (pinned to the `k_base` picture) and the world picture inside it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| o_f | int pair | screen px | calculated | Sub-base-px scroll under `Q = 1/k`; **not** D.9's container offset `o`, which already contains this fraction, so adding both double-counted it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| q | int pair | screen px | calculated | Picture-relative screen position (was `s`, which D.5/D.6 use for the field-scale factor) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| H | int | D.4 | D.4 | HUD scale |
| h | int pair | HUD px | calculated | Position on the HUD layer, quantised to the HUD grid |

**Output Range:** `q` is integer because every term is; `h` is floored to the HUD grid so a prompt never sits on a fractional HUD pixel. An object outside `[0, L_w·k_z) × [0, L_h·k_z)` projects outside the picture and **gets no prompt** (never clamped to the frame edge, Section C C9). The function is the single projection every HUD anchor uses; nobody re-derives it.
**Example:** 1080p, `k_z = 3`, `H = 3`, `(L_w, L_h) = (640, 360)`, `pose_q = (1000, 500)`, object `p = (1100, 440)`: `q = ((100, −60) + (320, 180)) × 3 = (1260, 360)`; `h = (420, 120)`. The result is picture-relative, so it is the same on a letterboxed picture: 1600×900 `keep`, `k_z = 2`, `H = 3` (picture at window offset (160, 90)) gives `q = (840, 240)`, `h = (280, 80)`; so does the Deck at 1280×800 `keep` (picture at (0, 40)); under `expand` at 1280×800 (`L_h` = 400) the same object gives `q = (840, 280)`, `h = (280, 93)` (`floor(93.33)`). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**D.14b Mouse-to-world for aim (open loop)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

The `mouse_to_world_aim` formula is defined as:

`c_pic = cursor_px − picture_origin` (cursor in **physical window px**, picture-relative; this is Player Controller's `cursor_vp`); `cursor_off = (c_pic − (L_w, L_h) · k_z / 2) / k_z` (base px from picture centre; Player Controller's `(cursor_vp − picture_centre) / k_eff` with `picture_centre = (L_w, L_h) · k_z / 2` and `k_eff = k_z`); **`f_q = floor(f_n / Q) · Q`** per axis, the follow term of D.6 quantised with the same `floor` and `Q` as the pose but before look-ahead, the inner-box clamp and jolt (the pose this frame would have with `L = 0`), **published by the pipeline** every frame; `cursor_world_aim = f_q + cursor_off`. The Controller then takes `aim = cursor_world_aim − AIM_ORIGIN` with `AIM_ORIGIN = floor(pos) + (0, AIM_ORIGIN_Y)` (PC-5, user ruling D10) and applies PC-5's `AIM_MOUSE_DEADZONE_PX` hold around `AIM_ORIGIN`; **this mapping subtracts no chest point and publishes no aim vector**. The full-pose companion used for picking is `cursor_world_view = pose_q + cursor_off` (unjolted) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4: the anchor is `f_q`, as Player Controller's form; the chest-based subtraction is removed; `cursor_px` is physical window px)*.

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| cursor_px | float pair | **physical window px** | engine: the mouse event / window position; with root stretch disabled (ADR-0008 s3.3) it is already physical window px and no inverse final transform is needed *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)*. **`Viewport.get_mouse_position()` on the root is window px under wiring (i) because root stretch is disabled (ADR-0008 § 3.3); had a root `canvas_items` stretch (wiring (ii)) been kept it would return stretched base units and dividing by `k_z` would be wrong by a factor of `k`; the in-engine AC in "Camera follow, look-ahead, cuts" still reads the unit back** | Mouse position *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4)* |
| picture_origin, (L_w, L_h), k_z | | | D.2 / D.3 / D.5 | The **world picture's** top-left (the HUD rect's origin plus `rem`, D.14, since at a ladder step the world picture sits inside the HUD rect), its logical size and its scale |
| f_q | float pair | multiples of `Q` | calculated: `floor(f_n / Q) · Q` (D.6 `f`, before look-ahead, clamp and jolt) | **Published** look-ahead-free follow pose; the anchor of the aim mapping. A function of the avatar's motion only, never of aim or `L` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4)* |
| AIM_ORIGIN | int pair | body px | Player Controller PC-5: `floor(pos) + (0, AIM_ORIGIN_Y)`, `AIM_ORIGIN_Y` = −35 | Fixed reference chest the Controller subtracts; the beam is still drawn from the true chest point. Not defined or read here except to state the contract *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4)* |
| cursor_off | float pair | base px | calculated | Cursor offset from picture centre |
| cursor_world_aim | float pair | body px | calculated: `f_q + cursor_off` | The look-ahead-free world point under the cursor; Player Controller turns it into the aim vector `cursor_world_aim − AIM_ORIGIN`, whose direction is the raw mouse aim and whose length drives D.7's lean magnitude `m` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4)* |

**Output Range:** `cursor_world_aim` is a function of the cursor, `k_z` and the **follow term** `f` only: `∂cursor_world_aim/∂L = ∂cursor_world_aim/∂jolt = ∂cursor_world_aim/∂(inner-box clamp) = 0` identically, and `f` itself is driven by the avatar's motion alone (D.6), so the look-ahead, which the aim drives, cannot feed back into it. The two mappings differ by exactly the look-ahead plus the clamp, `cursor_world_view − cursor_world_aim = pose_q − f_q = L + (clamp correction, zero when the box is inactive)` (up to `L_MAX_eff` = 72 px at the defaults); that difference is the loop the finding named, and it is why `mouse_to_world_view` is forbidden to aim code. A still mouse and a still avatar give a constant `aim` while `L` swings from 0 to `L_MAX_eff`. **Stated consequence of anchoring on `f_q` rather than on the avatar's `p`** (delegated ruling R4, the Player Controller's form is canonical): `f_q` trails the avatar by the follow lag, so with a still mouse and a **moving** avatar the aim point is displaced from the standing aim by the **aim shift** `S` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; G4 recompute)*. **Definition of `S` and its oracle:** `S = f_q.x − floor(pos_curr.x)` is the displacement of the aim point from the standing aim (the standing `S` is 0, because `f` settles onto the target). Player Controller's `AIM_ORIGIN` uses the committed `floor(pos_curr)`, while `f` follows the **interpolated** target `target_n = prev + tick_alpha·(curr − prev)` (D.3, PR-4 rule 8), so `S = floor(target_n.x − lag_ss(dt)) − floor(pos_curr.x)`. For an eastward run at 3 whole px/tick, `target_n.x = pos_curr.x − (1 − α_draw)·v_tick` with `v_tick = v/TICK_RATE` = 180/60 = 3 px (2 px at walk), hence for integer `pos_curr.x`: `S = floor(−(lag_ss(dt) + (1 − α_draw)·v_tick))`, with `lag_ss(dt) = v·τ·h/(e^h − 1)`, `h = dt/τ` (D.6, measured after the update). **`32.4` px (`= v_max·τ_follow`) is the dt→0 UPPER BOUND of `lag_ss`, not its value at any shipped rate** (30.9 at 60 fps, 31.4 at 90, 31.8 at 144). **Bounds:** `S ∈ (x − 1, x]` for `x = −(lag_ss + (1 − α_draw)·v_tick)`; `|S| ≤ floor(32.4 + 3) + 1 = 36` at the run (`x ≥ −35.4`, so `S ≥ −36`) and `≤ 24` at the walk (`x ≥ −(21.6 + 2) = −23.6`). `S` is exactly constant only when `α_draw` is constant (60 fps, tick-before-render) and `pos_curr` is an integer; otherwise it varies by at most `ceil(v_tick)` = 3 px over the tick phase (the variation is set by the 60 Hz tick sawtooth sampled at the render rate, not by `v/f_render`). **Fixture values** (all at `pos_curr.x` = 1000, `v` = 180 px/s, `τ` = 0.18, `TICK_RATE` 60, `α_k = frac(k·60/fps)` with `α_0 = 0` for the 90/144 traces): *60 fps*, `dt` = 1/60, tick-before-render, `α_draw` = 0.05 (the `motion_latency` example value): `lag_ss` = 30.9; `x = −(30.9 + 0.95·3) = −33.75`; `S = −34` on every frame (constant). *90 fps*, `dt` = 1/90, `lag_ss` = 31.4; `α_k` cycles {0, 2/3, 1/3} so `(1 − α)·3` = {3, 1, 2}, `x` = {−34.4, −32.4, −33.4}, `S ∈ {−35, −33, −34}`, spread 2. *144 fps*, `dt` = 1/144, `lag_ss` = 31.8; `α_k = frac(k·5/12)`, `(1 − α)·3` = {3, 1.75, 0.5, 2.25, 1, 2.75, 1.5, 0.25, 2, 0.75, 2.5, 1.25}, `x` = {−34.8, −33.55, −32.3, −34.05, −32.8, −34.55, −33.3, −32.05, −33.8, −32.55, −34.3, −33.05}, `S ∈ {−35, −34, −33}`, spread 2. All spreads ≤ `ceil(v_tick)` = 3. So with a still mouse and a **moving** avatar the aim is constant only once the lag has settled (a stand, or a steady run at a constant `α_draw`); while the lag is changing (run start, run stop, a direction change) the aim point moves, while the lag changes, by at most `|ΔS| ≤ 36 px` (lag part ≤ 32.4 px, tick phase ≤ 3 px, floor 1 px), and the committed aim angle moves with it (an example is in the Example below). That is Player Controller PC-5's stated behaviour (its edge case "cursor held still while the avatar walks"); it is a playtest item (Open Question 18).
**Example:** 1080p, `k_z = 3`, `(L_w, L_h) = (640, 360)`, `Q = 1`, avatar standing at `p = (1000, 500)` with `f` settled (`f_q = (1000, 500)`), cursor at picture-relative physical px `(1080, 540)`: `cursor_off = ((1080, 540) − (960, 540))/3 = (40, 0)`; `cursor_world_aim = (1040, 500)`; `AIM_ORIGIN = (1000, 500 − 35) = (1000, 465)`; the Controller's `aim = (40, 35)`, length 53.2, so `m = 1`. With the lean at full north (`L = (0, −72)`, `pose_q = (1000, 428)`) the full-pose mapping would give `cursor_world_view = (1040, 428)` and `aim = (40, −37)` — a 72 px vertical difference and the loop; `mouse_to_world_aim` still gives `(40, 35)`. **Cursor at the picture centre:** `c_pic = (960, 540)` gives `cursor_off = (0, 0)` at `k_z` = 3, and `c_pic = (640, 360)` (picture 1280×720, `k_z` = 2) gives `(0, 0)` at `k_z` = 2 (the AC below), under both wirings. **Running example (the stated consequence):** the avatar runs east at 180 px/s at 60 fps, `α_draw` = 0.05, `pos_curr.x` = 1000, with the same still cursor (`cursor_off = (40, 0)`): `target_n.x = 1000 − 0.95·3 = 997.15`; `f_n.x = 997.15 − 30.9 = 966.25`; `f_q.x = floor(966.25) = 966`, so `S = −34`; `cursor_world_aim = (966 + 40, 500) = (1006, 500)` and `aim = (1006 − 1000, 500 − 465) = (6, 35)`, against `(40, 35)` when settled: the aim point has moved 34 px (= 30.9 lag + 2.85 tick phase + 0.25 floor, 30.9 + 2.85 = 33.75 → 34) while the mouse was still, all of it follow geometry, none of it the lean. At 144 fps with the same `α_draw` = 0.05, `lag_ss` = 31.8, `f_n.x = 997.15 − 31.8 = 965.35`, `f_q.x = 965`, `S = −35`, `aim = (5, 35)`; the fixed "32 px" of the earlier text does not occur at any shipped rate *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; G4 recompute)*. *Aim origin (user ruling 2026-10-27, design-review decision D10, confirmed)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the origin subtracted is Player Controller's fixed reference chest `AIM_ORIGIN = floor(pos) + (0, AIM_ORIGIN_Y)` (PC-5), independent of facing and sheet, while the beam is still drawn from the true chest point; it is normative, not illustrative: the earlier "indifferent to which origin the Controller subtracts" tail note and the chest-based subtraction are removed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4)*.

**D.15 Flash-count predicate**

The `flash_count` formula is defined as:

`Y_ref` is a **running extremum with direction tracking** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier `Y_ref` = "the level at the last transition" scored a smooth 10 Hz sine between 0.2 and 0.9 as about one transition, because no single sample ever sat 0.10 away from the level at the previous trigger while the series was still moving)*. State per series: `dir ∈ {UNSET, RISING, FALLING}`, `Y_ref`, and while `UNSET` also `Y_lo` and `Y_hi`; at the first sample `dir = UNSET`, `Y_lo = Y_hi = Y_0`. For each sample `Y_i` with `Θ = 0.10 · Y_scale`: **`UNSET`**: update `Y_lo = min(Y_lo, Y_i)` and `Y_hi = max(Y_hi, Y_i)`; if `Y_i − Y_lo ≥ Θ` then a transition fires, `dir ← RISING`, `Y_ref ← Y_i`; else if `Y_hi − Y_i ≥ Θ` then a transition fires, `dir ← FALLING`, `Y_ref ← Y_i`. **`RISING`**: `Y_ref ← max(Y_ref, Y_i)`; if `Y_ref − Y_i ≥ Θ` the series has moved `Θ` against the direction: a transition fires, `dir ← FALLING`, `Y_ref ← Y_i`. **`FALLING`**: `Y_ref ← min(Y_ref, Y_i)`; if `Y_i − Y_ref ≥ Θ` a transition fires, `dir ← RISING`, `Y_ref ← Y_i`. Every counted transition therefore reverses the sign of the previous one by construction, and a slow drift that never reverses by `Θ` counts once, at its start. `F(window) = ceil(n_transitions / 2)` over any sliding **half-open** 1 s window `[t, t + 1 s)` (`ceil`, not `floor`: with `floor` a 3.5 Hz flicker, 7 transitions per second, scored `F = 3` and passed; with `ceil` it scores 4 and fails; a single step change scores 1) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; constraint `F ≤ F_MAX` evaluated per light, per region (3 × 3 grid over the displayed picture at the current field — a ninth of it; 213 × 120 base px only at the default 640×360 field), and on the frame mean

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| Y_i | float | 0 – 1 | analytic per-light model (the clamp) or the displayed-picture scan (verification) | **Linear relative luminance** `Y = 0.2126 R + 0.7152 G + 0.0722 B` of linear (not sRGB-encoded) RGB, scene-referred and clamped to 0 – 1, of the light's contribution, region mean or frame mean at frame i *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| Y_ref | float | 0 – 1 | calculated (running extremum) | The running **extremum in the current direction** (the maximum while `RISING`, the minimum while `FALLING`), reset to `Y_i` when a transition fires; not the level at the previous trigger *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| dir, Y_lo, Y_hi | enum, float, float | `{UNSET, RISING, FALLING}`; 0 – 1 | calculated | Direction state; `Y_lo` / `Y_hi` are tracked only until the first transition |
| Y_scale | float | 1.0 (frame/region) or `max(peak of the light, 0.05)` | fixed | Denominator of the 10 % rule. Per light it is `max(peak of the light, 0.05)`: the 0.05 floor stops a very dim light (peak below 0.05) from making a ripple smaller than `Θ` = 0.005 a "transition". A light that is off (peak 0) has no changes and scores `F` = 0 trivially; the floor does not exempt a real swing (a 0.04 swing of a dim light is 80 % of its `Y_scale` and counts). The per-light rule is deliberately stricter than WCAG 2.3.1's 10 % of maximum luminance; the region and frame-mean tests use `Y_scale` = 1.0, the WCAG-aligned measure, so a dim light that counts at element level adds only its area-weighted change at region level *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4)* |
| F_MAX (registry: `FLASH_MAX_PER_S`) | int | 3 | ux-designer (decided; WCAG 2.3.1-style) | Flashes allowed per second |
| region | int pair | a ninth of the displayed picture at the current field (213 × 120 base px only at the default 640×360 field; 128 × 72 at the 384×216 field of `k_z` = 5 at 1080p) | derived at runtime (= field / 3 per axis; registry `flash_governor`) | Regional ceiling area *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from 2d-lighting-and-occlusion and perception-filter)* |
| n_transitions | int | 0 – unbounded | calculated | Counted sign-reversing transitions in the window |
| F | int | 0 – unbounded | calculated | `ceil(n/2)`: conservative flash count in the window *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** Compliant lights: the beacon sweep (one bright pass per 2–3 s → at most 2 transitions in any 1 s window, `F ≤ 1`), the cryo gutter (one dip per 10–20 s, `F ≤ 1`); the ceiling allows up to 6 transitions per second (`F = 3`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Two individually compliant lights overlapping a region can sum to `F > 3` in that region; the regional and frame-mean checks exist for exactly that, and the lighting modifier contract (Lighting GDD) is what clamps — the pipeline hosts the measurement (flash scan, spike step). The **deployable flicker is unspecified** and must be given a number that passes (routed, Section C PR-13).
**Area weighting, exemptions and the strict set** (user ruling 2026-10-27, D91 / X9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: for a region `r` of area `A_r` screen px (a ninth of the displayed picture) an element `e` that swings its own luminance by `ΔY_e` over `a_e` px moves the region mean by `Δȳ_r = (a_e / A_r) · ΔY_e`, and the region (or frame-mean) test is `Δȳ_r ≥ 0.10` with `Y_scale = 1`. Hence an element of full swing (`ΔY_e = 1`) is a region transition only if `a_e ≥ 0.10 · A_r`, and a swing of 0.4 only if `a_e ≥ 0.25 · A_r`. The element-level count (per light, per mark) uses the unweighted rule above with that element's own `Y_scale`. *Exempt from the element-level count only:* sub-threshold digit swaps and prompt show-starts (PR-13 g); they still add to `Δȳ_r`. *Strict, no exemption, no area weighting at element level:* the Alarm Red mark, and every source while reduce flashing is on. **Worked example** (1920×1080, `k_z = H = 3`; the numeral cell size is illustrative — the HUD owns the real one): `A_r` = 640 × 360 = 230 400 screen px; a numeral cell of 12 × 8 HUD px is 36 × 24 = 864 screen px, so one full-swing digit swap moves the region mean by 864 / 230 400 = 0.0038 and is not a region transition; 27 such cells swapping together (23 328 px ≥ 0.10 · 230 400 = 23 040) would be, which is why simultaneous swaps stay on the shared 500 ms grid (30 ticks, at most 2 transitions per numeral per second, `F = 1`). At the 384×216 field of `k_z` = 5 the region is 9 216 base px and the same threshold is 922 base px. **Constraint satisfied:** `F ≤ F_MAX` per light, per region and on the frame mean holds with or without the exemptions, because an exemption removes only the element-level count of a source that the region and frame sums still bound.

**Example:** A light alternating 0.9 / 0.4 every 100 ms: 10 transitions/s → `F = 5 > 3` → non-compliant; the clamp holds it at the last compliant level and diagnostics record `(light_id, F, window_start_tick)`. The same light at 250 ms per state: 4 transitions → `F = 2` → passes; a 3 Hz light (6 transitions) → `F = 3` → passes; a 3.5 Hz flicker (7 transitions) → `F = 4` → non-compliant; a single 3 transitions/s fixture → `F = 2` → passes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **Smooth waveforms** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: a 10 Hz sine between 0.2 and 0.9 (peak-to-peak 0.7 ≥ `Θ`) reverses at every half-cycle, so 20 transitions/s and `F = 10` at any frame rate (60 fps: a transition every 3 frames; 144 fps: every 7.2 frames on average) → non-compliant; a 3 Hz sine of the same range gives 6 transitions in every half-open 1 s window and `F = 3` → passes; a sine whose peak-to-peak is below `Θ` never fires after the first sample and gives `F = 0`. **Y_scale in the small-swing example**: a series stepping 0.50 → 0.59 → 0.50 is `F = 0` only as a frame or region mean (`Y_scale = 1`, `Θ = 0.10`, swing 0.09); as a single light with peak 0.59 (`Y_scale = 0.59`, `Θ = 0.059`) the same 0.09 swings are transitions: `n = 2`, `F = 1`. **Y_scale floor:** a dim light of peak 0.04 stepping 0.0355 ↔ 0.0400 (swing 0.0045 < `Θ` = 0.005) is `F` = 0; the same peak swinging 0.00 ↔ 0.04 every 250 ms is `n` = 4, `F` = 2 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4)*.

**D.16 Draw-call budget shape per viewport (art bible 8.6, cited)**

The `draw_budget` formula is defined as:

`D_world = D_floor + D_marks + D_ysort + D_caps + D_fx`; `D_root = D_hud + D_composite + D_filter + D_hold + D_L3` *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)*; `D_world + D_root ≤ D_BUDGET` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from chart-table-web-map-and-menus)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| D_floor | int | ≈ 4 | 8.6 (`rendering_quadrant_size = 16`) | Floor `TileMapLayer` |
| D_marks | int | 4 – 20 | 8.6 | Floor trails and stains |
| D_ysort | int | 50 – 300 (now **including** pipes + emissive 10 – 40, Section C PR-5) | 8.6; NOT SOURCEABLE until step 5 | The single multiplier |
| D_caps | int | ≈ 4 | 8.6 | Cap `TileMapLayer` |
| D_fx | int | ≤ 22 | 8.6 (fringe ≤ 10, ≤ 12 live particle emitters) | Effects |
| D_hud | int | 20 – 60 | 8.6 | HUD on the root |
| D_filter, D_hold | int | 1 (0 at MVP: route PER_ELEMENT, container slot `NOT_USED`), 0–1 | PR-10, PR-3 | Container material pass; hold quad. The container composite is its own term `D_composite` = 1 (probed 2026-10-28: counted on the root, one per container; ADR-0008 s7.1); `D_root` cap 63 (62 expected with a null slot), 93 with the chart open, the latter holding only while the HUD is hidden under a full-bleed plate |
| D_composite | int | 1 (one per `SubViewportContainer`; there is one) | ADR-0008 § 7.1 | Container composite, counted on the root *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)* |
| D_L3 | int | 0, or ≈ 15 – 30 while the Chart Table is open | Chart Table ASK-CT-12 | Layer-3 draw calls, counted only while the chart is open |
| D_BUDGET | int | 1000 | 8.6 | Frame budget |

**Output Range:** `D_world ≈ 72 – 350` **at the default field and at every zoom-in step** (the content in view does not grow), `D_root ≈ 22 – 63` with the chart table closed (≈ 36 – 93 while it is open); the sum sits well inside 1000 unless `D_ysort` blows past 300, which is the trigger for D.13. **Caps per field** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the single `D_world ≤ 350` cap cannot hold at the zoom-out steps given this GDD's own `D_ysort` range (4× the content at the Deck's 1× is up to ≈ 1 200 Y-sorted draws, 9× at 1× on 1080p up to ≈ 2 700), so the caps are: default and zoom-in steps `D_world ≤ 350`, `D_root ≤ 63`, sum ≤ 1000; **zoom-out steps** `D_world + D_root ≤ 1000` alone (so `D_world ≤ 937` with the chart closed at the `D_root` cap of 63, 938 at the MVP expected 62) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)* **and** a P99 total-frame-time ceiling (the Performance block). If a zoom-out step cannot meet both on RM-DESKTOP or RM-DECK at the spike (R12), the lever is the ladder's bottom — the lowest step(s) that fail are **withheld** (the field is capped by draw count) and the decision is recorded; *Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20)* (design-review decision D5): **the lowest failing step(s) are withheld rather than cutting content**, so the content and costs of a lit field are never traded away to keep a step; the withheld bound is recorded against Open Question 21, and "failing" is judged on the named machines RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK against the P99 ceilings of the Performance block (R12; Open Question 20) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*. Measured **per viewport** with `Viewport.get_render_info(RENDER_INFO_TYPE_CANVAS, RENDER_INFO_DRAW_CALLS_IN_FRAME)` `[BIN]` so the HUD's number is never confused with the sorted layer's. Zoomed out, `D_ysort` scales roughly with visible content — at the Deck's 1× field (D.5) about 4× the default content is in view, and at 1× on 1080p about 9×, so step 5 must be run at the largest ladder field of each target, not only at 1080p default (routed to technical-artist) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check)*.
**Example:** Spike room at 1080p default: `4 + 10 + 180 + 4 + 15 = 213` world, `40 + 1 (composite) = 41` root → 254 of 1000.
**Pass A / pass B cost at the ladder ends** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (lit pixels = the world viewport's raster; pass A renders the logical field, pass B the picture, so A saves `k_z²`):

| Target (`k_base`) | Default field: A / B lit px | Top zoom-in step: A / B | Bottom step (1×): A / B |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1080p (3) | 640×360 = 230 400 / 2 073 600 | 384×216 = 82 944 / 2 073 600 | 1920×1080 = 2 073 600 / 2 073 600 |
| 1440p (4) | 230 400 / 3 686 400 | 426×240 = 102 240 / 3 686 400 | 2560×1440 = 3 686 400 / 3 686 400 |
| 2160p (6) | 230 400 / 8 294 400 | 480×270 = 129 600 / 8 294 400 | 3840×2160 = 8 294 400 / 8 294 400 |
| Deck `expand` (2) | 640×400 = 256 000 / 1 024 000 | 320×200 = 64 000 / 1 024 000 | 1280×800 = 1 024 000 / 1 024 000 |

At the bottom step pass A costs **as much as pass B in lit pixels and render-target memory** (ratio `k_z² = 1`) plus the container composite, so the 1× step is costed at B's number whichever mode ships, and any decision that rests on A's saving is made at the default field and the zoom-in steps only. Render-target memory scales with lit pixels (8.6: ≈ 3 MB at 230 400 px under A and ≈ 22 MB at 2 073 600 under B at 1080p). Pass B at 1440p and 2160p at 1× is not measured by the spike plan as written and is added to steps 2 and 5.

**Predicted-withheld steps and the withholding mechanism** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: by this GDD's own arithmetic the withholding of D5 is the **expected** outcome at the large targets, not an edge case. `D_ysort` scales with the field's area relative to the default 640×360 (230 400 px): ratio `ρ = field_w·field_h / 230 400`; the non-Y-sort part of `D_world` is 12 – 50 (33 in the spike room); the cap is `D_world ≤ 938` (sum ≤ 1000 with the MVP expected `D_root` = 62; 937 at the `D_root` cap of 63, which moves only the 180 case, 5.03× to 5.02×) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)*. The largest admissible ratio is `ρ_max = (938 − non_ysort) / D_ysort`: **18.5×** at `D_ysort` 50 (best case), **5.03×** at 180 (the spike room of the Example), **2.96×** at 300 (the top of the range). Predicted **withheld** steps (the draw cap only; P99 may withhold more):

| Target (`k_base`) | Step: ρ | `D_ysort` 50 | `D_ysort` 180 (spike room) | `D_ysort` 300 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1080p (3) | 1×: 9; 2×: 2.25 | none | **1×** (180 × 9 + 33 = 1 653 > 938) | **1×** |
| 1440p (4) | 1×: 16; 2×: 4; 3×: 1.78 | none | **1×** | **1×, 2×** |
| 2160p (6) | 1×: 36; 2×: 9; 3×: 4; 4×: 2.25 | **1×** | **1×, 2×** | **1×, 2×, 3×** |
| Deck `expand` (2) | 1×: 4.44 | none | none (P99 decides) | **1×** — the Deck has no zoom-out and a ladder of `[2, 4]`: default plus two zoom-in steps |

**Mechanism.** Withholding is **static, not runtime**: a data table `ZOOM_MIN_K` keyed by `(k_base, target class)` (the target class being the display class the build was measured on: desktop 1080p / 1440p / 2160p, handheld) holds the lowest permitted `k_z`, set from the spike's measured table and never changed by a live measurement (a mid-session ladder change would be a player-visible surprise); the effective ladder is `[ZOOM_MIN_K, k_base + 2]` (default `ZOOM_MIN_K` = 1 until the spike fills it). On a `RESCALING` that changes `k_base` the clamp of D.5 becomes `k_z ← clamp(k_z, ZOOM_MIN_K(k_base_new), k_base_new + 2)`. A press below the effective ladder is refused like a press beyond either end, with `diag.zoom_step_refused = {reason: WITHHELD, k_z, ZOOM_MIN_K}`; `k_z` is never saved, so no persisted value can fall below the table. Every ladder AC below is **parameterised on the effective ladder** (the full ladder is the case `ZOOM_MIN_K` = 1). Default D5 stays as applied (confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20)); the Deck consequence (a handheld with `k_base` 2 loses its only zoom-out in the worst case) is accepted with it, and the alternative (cutting `D_ysort` content) is rejected by D5 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R20)*.

**D.17 Repeated-pose frames**

The `repeated_pose_fraction` formula is defined as:

without interpolation `R_0 = max(0, 1 − f_tick / f_render)`; with interpolation and quantum `Q` at speed `v`: `P_move = min(1, v / (Q · f_render))`, `R = 1 − P_move`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| f_tick | float | 60 Hz | kernel CR-1 | Tick rate |
| f_render | float | 30 – 240 Hz | engine | Render rate |
| v | float | 0 – v_max (base px/s) | Player Controller | Avatar speed |
| Q | float | 1 or 1/k | D.9 | Camera quantum |
| R_0, R | float | 0 – 1 | calculated | Fraction of frames that show the same pose as the previous frame |

**Output Range:** At 144 fps without interpolation 58.3 % of frames repeat (visible judder). With `tick_alpha` interpolation and `Q = 1` the repeat fraction is set by the speed: a 1 px/tick body (60 px/s) moves on 41.7 % of frames and repeats on 58.3 % — the same number, because interpolation cannot create motion smaller than one base px (snap is a floor, not a filter); at `v_walk = 120` it repeats on 16.7 %, at `v_run = 180` on 0 % (≥ 1 px every frame) *(re-run 2026-10-26 — consistency check)*. With `Q = 1/k` at `k = 3` the camera moves every frame above 48 px/s. At 60 fps every speed ≥ 60 px/s moves every frame. This is the honest promise: "base-pixel steps at interpolated timing, never twice in a frame at f_render ≥ 60 (below that 1–2 base px per frame at 30–59 fps), never backward" — step 12 measures it *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4)*.
**Example:** 144 fps, `v = v_walk = 120`, `Q = 1`: `P_move = 120/144 = 0.833`, `R = 0.167`. Same at `Q = 1/3`: `P_move = min(1, 120/48) = 1`, `R = 0`. At `v = 60` (1 px/tick): `P_move = 0.417`, `R = 0.583`.

**D.18 Visual latency of continuous motion**

The `motion_latency` formula is defined as:

`latency = (1 − tick_alpha_draw) · tick_duration + L_render`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| tick_alpha_draw | float | 0 – 1 | kernel D.3, sampled when the frame is composed | Interpolation fraction at draw time |
| tick_duration | float | 16.67 ms | kernel D.1 | One tick |
| L_render | float | 0 – frame_duration | kernel D.4 | Wait from compose to present |
| latency | float | ≈ 0 – 33 ms | calculated | How far behind the committed state a moving sprite is drawn |

**Output Range:** With tick-before-render at 60 fps, `tick_alpha_draw ≈ 0` → one full tick (≈ 17 ms) plus `L_render` — the price of forbidding extrapolation (Pillar 1, kernel D.3). Discrete committed state (a light toggled, a trail appearing) and look-ahead (raw aim, D.7) carry no tick. At 144 fps the average `tick_alpha_draw ≈ 0.5` → ≈ 8 ms + `L_render`.
**Example:** 60 fps, `tick_alpha_draw = 0.05`, `L_render = 5 ms` → `0.95 × 16.67 + 5 ≈ 20.8 ms`.

## Edge Cases

*Each case states the exact outcome and what diagnostics record (the Debug Overlay's DIAGNOSTIC surface, Section C row 11; `diag.*` names are provisional until the pipeline, as owner, freezes them under registry `diag_panel_contract`)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from debug-overlay-true-values)*

**Window, scale and aspect**

- **If the window is 1366×768 under `keep`**: `k = 2`; picture 1280×720 centred; bars 43/43 left/right and 24/24 top/bottom, true black, outside the world viewport. Under `expand`: logical 682×384 (even), a 2-screen-px residual bar split 1/1, 14.2 × 8.0 tiles. Diagnostics record `diag.k = 2`, `diag.picture`, `diag.bars` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *D.1–D.3.*
- **If the window is 1365×768 (odd width)**: under `keep` bars 42 left / 43 right (floor/ceil split), 24/24; under `expand` logical 682×384 and a 1-screen-px residual bar split 0/1 — the logical size is always an even integer, so no odd or half base column is ever rendered or handed to the engine; what the engine draws in the residual under `keep` or `expand` is step 11's (screenshot), no longer a question about a non-integer base *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *D.2–D.3.*
- **If the window is 1600×900 under `keep`**: `k = 2`, bars 160/160 and 90/90 — 36 % of the screen black; the player is told in Settings that `expand` removes the bars once it ships. Under `expand`: 800×450, 16.7 × 9.4 tiles, inside the 960×540 cap. *The creative director recommended accepting the information gap and the user ratified it on 2026-10-26 (Open Question 9).*
- **If the window is 3440×1440 under `keep`**: `k = 4`, bars 440/440 left/right, none top/bottom. Under `expand`: 860×360, 17.9 × 7.5 tiles — the narrow cone's 8-tile reach fits entirely at ≈ 9 tiles half-width where 16:9 clips it at 6.7; recorded as the information caveat. Pass-B lit pixels 4.95 M (post-spike measurement).
- **If the window is 1280×800 (Steam Deck) under `keep`**: `k = 2`, bars 40 top / 40 bottom. Under `expand`: 640×400, 20 extra rows above and below; HUD anchors to the 640×400 picture; `H_default = 3` (D.4). Diagnostics log `screen_get_dpi()`, `screen_get_scale()`, `get_final_transform()` for the Deck's Linux session (step 11).
- **If the window is smaller than 1280×720** (user drags it, or a 1024×768 display): the window is refused below the minimum (`Window.min_size`); if the OS still delivers a smaller size (a display change), `k` is clamped to 2 and the picture is cropped centred rather than scaled below 2× — the only case where picture edges leave the window. Diagnostics record `diag.window_below_min = true` and the delivered size. *PR-1.*
- **If a resize arrives mid-frame**: the current frame completes at the old `k`; the next frame is one `RESCALING` frame (held, re-derive `k`, `H`, logical size, bars, limits, `world_to_hud`), then `PRESENTING`. No frame ever carries a fractional transform (step 11 screenshots the first frame after every change). A continuous edge drag is a sequence of one-frame `RESCALING`s debounced to the final size (→ ADR). Diagnostics record each `size_changed` with old and new `k`.
- **If a resize arrives mid-tick**: nothing — the sim never sees the window; `RESCALING` is a pipeline state and the kernel's tick is unaffected (CR-11). The first `_process` after the resize re-derives; `present_time` keeps advancing.
- **If fullscreen is toggled during breach**: identical to any resize — one `RESCALING` frame, no sim effect, no camera reaction beyond re-derivation; the camera pose is unchanged (the picture re-centres on the same `pose_q`). The beacon and vents keep their phase on `present_time`. Diagnostics record the mode change and that no cut fired (`diag.cut_reason` empty).
- **If the DPI changes** (`dpi_changed`, moving the window between monitors): `k` is re-derived from the new window **pixel** size only; DPI never enters D.1; `content_scale_factor` stays 1.0. If the pixel size did not change, nothing changes. Diagnostics record the DPI event and the (unchanged) `k`.
- **If `k_base` changes while at a ladder step** (a resize while `k_z ≠ k_base`): `k_base` is re-derived in the same `RESCALING` frame and the ladder `[1, k_base_new + 2]` rebuilt; `k_z` is kept if it is still on the ladder, else clamped to the nearer end; the step persists (it is never a hold); the field and the solver's `s` recompute. Example: 1920×1080 → 1280×720 while at `k_z = 2` keeps `k_z = 2`, which is now the default (`diag.zoom_step = 0`); the same resize while at `k_z = 5` clamps to 4 (`diag.zoom_step = +2`). Diagnostics record `diag.k_base`, `diag.k_z`, `diag.zoom_step = k_z − k_base`. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check.)*

**Zoom**

- **If the zoom input is pressed during a cut**: the cut completes first (the solver reset is one frame); the zoom step applies on the following frame as its own one-frame `RESCALING`. Two cuts in two frames are acceptable; a cut and a scale change in the same frame are not (two transforms to screenshot). Diagnostics record both with their frame numbers.
- **If `zoom_in` or `zoom_out` is pressed during `LOADING`**: the press is discarded (edge-triggered, never queued); the first `RUNNING` frame is the cut to the avatar at `k_base` after a re-wake, or at the pre-load `k_z` after an in-session load (zoom is pipeline state, never saved, reset only on re-wake); a press after that steps normally. The player never sees a zoomed re-wake frame. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check.)*
- **If `zoom_in` is pressed at `k_base + 2`, or `zoom_out` at `k_z = 1`**: ignored — no `RESCALING`, no cut; `diag.zoom_step_refused` increments. *(Added 2026-10-26 — consistency check.)*
- **If re-wake occurs while `k_z ≠ k_base`**: the re-wake `CUT` resets `k_z` to `k_base` so the cryo bay frames identically to the first wake; `diag.zoom_reset = REWAKE`. A body transfer, transit or in-session load leaves `k_z` unchanged across its cut. *(Added 2026-10-26 — consistency check.)*
- **If `zoom_out` is pressed down to 1× on a 2× world** (720p, Deck, manual 2×): `k_z = 1`, field = the full picture at 1 screen px per base px (Deck under `expand`: 1280×800 base = 26.7 × 16.7 tiles); 5.6 tier-2 marks are defined to survive 50 % so readability holds; the on-device acuity check is the accessibility-specialist's. **Draw-call consequence:** about 4× the default content is in view, so the Y-sorted layer's ≈ 300-draw ceiling (D.16) is tested at the zoomed Deck field, not at 1080p default — spike step 5 must include it (routed to technical-artist). **Lit pixels:** under pass B unchanged (the window is the window); under pass A — the default and the fallback — the world viewport renders at logical size, so the zoomed Deck target is 1280×800 texels = **4× the lit pixels of 640×400**, and light and shadow cost scales with it — the second reason steps 3 and 5 must be run at the largest ladder field of each target (1× on 1080p is 9× the default field). Diagnostics record `diag.k_z = 1`, the per-viewport draw count and the lit-pixel count. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check.)*
- **If a zoom step is pressed with reduced motion on**: the step still happens — it is a cut, not a motion, and it is the player's input. Look-ahead is already 0 under reduced motion, so the zoomed frame is centred on the avatar.
- **If a zoom step is pressed during a pause**: the press is discarded (the chart table owns input while paused and the world viewport is not re-rendering); the frozen frame stays at its current `k_z` (the world simply stopped, 7.4), and that `k_z` persists on resume with no `RESCALING`. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check.)*

**Follow, cuts and target discontinuities**

- **If the follow target teleports on re-wake**: `P_jump` fires (D.10) → cut; the solver resets to the target with `L = 0` (eased in at the slew rate, so the first frame does not depend on the aim at that instant) and jolt cleared *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; framing is identical to the first wake (same room, same lamp, same `pose_q` offset) — snap, not ease. Diagnostics record `diag.cut_reason = REWAKE` (the `LOADING → RUNNING` trigger) and the pose.
- **If the follow target teleports on pod arrival**: the arrival is a body transfer and a scene load → cut through `TRANSIT_HOLD`'s black; the first `PRESENTING` frame is already composed at the new body's frame. `diag.cut_reason = BODY_TRANSFER`.
- **If the avatar's body changes without a load** (weld merges the avatar's section into the ship; detach puts the avatar on a new body): the solver **rebases** (`BODY_TRANSFER`): `f` and its history shift by `frame_delta_px` into the new body frame and the lag and `L` are kept, so the avatar's screen position is unchanged and there is no cut *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; where the hull jump cannot be guaranteed zero it is a hard cut, never a pan. The detached section translates without rotation in the interior view. Body supplies `frame_delta_px` in `BODY_TRANSFER` and the pipeline decides (BDY-34); diagnostics record the old and new body IDs and `frame_delta_px`. Where Web Map & Transit cannot guarantee `docked(a, b, frame_shift_px)` before a PLAYER-origin weld, the pipeline hides every non-zero `delta_abs` with its C5 camera cut, so the player never sees the hull jump (BDY-27) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from ship-body-and-interior-physics)*.
- **If a detach moves the camera's *limits* rather than its body** (the avatar stays on the ship, a section leaves): no cut; the `PROJECTOR` updates the limits rect the solver reads (`Camera2D.limit_*` stays at its defaults) on the next tick's notification *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; if the current pose is now outside the rect, the solver enters `CLAMPED` and the eased rect edge `rect_eff` carries the pose to the new edge along the D.6 exponential, 95 % in `t_95 = 0.54 s` with zero overshoot (D.8b: the pose is clamped against `rect_eff`, never popped) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Diagnostics record the limit change.
- **If the target exceeds the pan ceiling for exactly `T_PAN` minus one frame**: no cut; D.8 holds the avatar inside the inner box and D.6 eases. The predicate is "held continuously for ≥ `T_PAN`" in seconds — at 60 fps that is 6 frames (5 do not fire), at 144 fps 15 (14 do not), at 30 fps 3 (2 do not). Diagnostics record the peak `v_follow` and duration as `diag.pan_near_miss`.
- **If the lag is exactly `LAG_CUT` (48 px)**: no cut — the predicate is strictly greater. At 48.01 the solver cuts. Diagnostics record the lag sample. The invariant `LAG_CUT > v_max·τ_follow` (48 > 32.4) means a legal run never approaches it; if Player Controller raises `v_max` to 200 px/s or more at `τ = 0.18` and `L_MAX = 72`, Section G's inner-box check fails the build (the inner-box invariant binds before the 266 px/s lag-cut bound).
- **If `prev == curr` on the first frame after a restore**: `p = floor(prev)` for every `tick_alpha` — the correct outcome (nothing moved). The camera's `P_jump` sees the target jump from its pre-load position → cut (`LOADING → RUNNING` also forces it). Diagnostics record `diag.cut_reason = LOAD`.
- **If the kernel's `snap_baseline` did not run on a moving read view at the transition into `RUNNING`** *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync: `snap_baseline` is the only restore-continuity mechanism, ADR-0008 § 6.1; there is no owner-side `post_restore` step)*: the first frame after a load interpolates from the pre-load `previous` toward the restored `current` — a sprite drawn partway between two unrelated places for up to one tick, and the camera cuts anyway. This is a defect, not a tolerated state: a debug assertion in the pipeline root checks, on the first `RUNNING` frame after `LOADING`, that every moving read view reports `previous == current`, and diagnostics name the offending block (`diag.stale_previous = [block_id]`). The kernel's `snap_baseline` on every transition into `RUNNING` is the required step (ADR-0001 § 4).
- **If the first `RUNNING` frame after any `LOADING` is not yet filtered for the restored sanity level**: it is not presented — the layer-1 black hold stays up across any rebuild (per-element Filter materials re-bound; the mask re-allocated if route (a) ships) until the frame is drawn through the Perception Filter for the restored sanity; no unfiltered frame is ever presented, because one clean frame would be a free oracle about a hallucination (Save/Load SL-24, H4; the Filter's `first_frame_filtered` counter) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from save-load-and-versioned-format)*.
- **If the avatar and the aim stop** (a still scene): each pose term settles per axis once within `SETTLE_EPS` (D.6, D.7), so `pose_q` is constant after at most **`t_settle = max(t_settle_f, t_settle_L)`** (`t_settle_f` ≈ 0.75 s from the 32.4 px run lag; `t_settle_L` ≈ 2.5 s after a full 144 px aim reversal: 0.6 s at the slew clamp plus `τ_lookahead·ln(L_SLEW_MAX·τ_lookahead/SETTLE_EPS)` = 0.4 × ln(57.6/0.5) = 1.9 s; the final base pixel may land inside that window, as the last step of a motion the avatar or the player's aim caused) and equals `floor(target + L_target)`, approached from any of the four directions and with `L_MAX = 0`. Diagnostics record `diag.pose_settled_at` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If `dt` is 0, negative or larger than `DT_MAX`, or `τ_follow_eff` is 0** (a hitch, a double-delivered frame, rigid follow): `dt ≤ 0` skips the solver update (no `0/0`, no NaN in the state); `dt` is clamped to `DT_MAX` = 0.05 s, the remaining real time arriving as a target discontinuity that D.10 treats (a hitch recovery is a named trigger of `P_jump` / `P_pan`); `τ_follow_eff ≤ τ_EPS` sets `a = 1` (rigid follow). Diagnostics record `diag.dt_clamped` and `diag.solver_skipped` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If a zoom step is pressed while running at `v_max`**: the solver rescales `d = target − f` by `τ_follow_eff(new)/τ_follow_eff(old)` and `L` by `s_new/s_old` on the `RESCALING` frame (D.6), so the avatar's offset from picture centre in screen px is unchanged for `s ≤ 1`, the inner box does not engage and no cut fires *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If `tick_alpha` is reported > 1 or < 0**: impossible by kernel D.3 (clamped at the driver); the pipeline asserts `0 ≤ tick_alpha ≤ 1` in debug and, in release, clamps and increments `diag.tick_alpha_out_of_range`. A non-zero counter is a kernel defect.

**Jolt**

- **If two jolt events arrive in one frame or overlap**: amplitude is the **max**, not the sum (`|j| ≤ JOLT_MAX = 2` always); the decay restarts from the later event. Diagnostics record both event IDs and the chosen `J_0`.
- **If a jolt event arrives with reduced motion on**: `J_0 = 0`; the event is logged (`diag.jolt_suppressed = true`) so audio and the HUD glyph still carry it. Also at MVP the knob default is 0 regardless.
- **If a jolt is active during a cut**: cleared by the reset (D.10). The cut frame has `j = 0`.
