- **If a jolt is requested by anything other than a P1 physical event** (Attention, Sanity, horror presentation): refused at the API — the jolt entry point accepts only tagged physical events; a debug assertion fires and diagnostics record the caller (`diag.jolt_refused = [caller]`). This is C1's anchor enforced.

**Look-ahead**

- **If the mouse is still while the lean swings, or while the avatar moves**: while the lean swings the aim is constant, because `mouse_to_world_aim` has no `L`, clamp or jolt in it (D.14b). A still cursor offset of 24, 48 or 72 base px from picture centre, a still avatar and `L` forced from 0 to 72 give the same committed `aim_angle` on every frame; a still cursor and an avatar at a **steady** run (follow lag settled) give an aim shifted by `S`, constant only at a constant `α_draw` (60 fps), otherwise within `ceil(v/TICK_RATE)` = 3 px (D.14b), but while the lag is changing (run start, stop, a turn) the aim point moves with `f_q` by at most `|ΔS| ≤ 36 px` (lag change ≤ 32.4 px — the dt→0 upper bound, 30.9 px at 60 fps and 31.8 at 144 — plus ≤ 3 px of tick phase and 1 px of floor; D.14b) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling R4)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; G4 recompute)*. Using `mouse_to_world_view` for aim would feed the lean back into the beam and is a lint failure. Diagnostics record `diag.aim_pose_read = [caller]` if aim code reads the view mapping *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If the cursor crosses the avatar** (aim length below `LEAN_R0·s`): the lean magnitude `m` falls to 0 smoothly (D.7), so the frame returns to centre instead of swinging by `2·L_MAX`; the direction `û` holds its previous value inside `ε` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If the player runs while aiming back at a pursuer** (aim opposite to the run, `m = 1`, `W_RUN` = 0.5): `L_target = (0, 0)`, so the frame is centred on the follow term (the avatar sits `lag_∞` = 32.4 px off-centre, inside the box) instead of leaning 72 px backward; aiming forward keeps the full 72 px lean; a diagonal aim gives the blended vector of D.7 (`|L| = 50.9` for a perpendicular aim). When the run stops or the avatar drops to a walk, `r` drops to 0 (the Controller's `loco_state` leaves `RUN`) and `L` eases to the pure-aim target at `τ_lookahead` under the slew clamp (the ease, not a ramp on `w`, is what prevents a pop); no cut predicate reads `L`. A run into a wall (no committed movement for 3 ticks, `moved` false) gives `w` = 0 the same way. A **diagonal** run or walk gives the same `w` and a constant `û_v` on every tick (the octant centre), where the displacement-keyed form jittered *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Reduced motion: `L_MAX = 0`, so no weighting *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **If the beam is off**: look-ahead follows the aim exactly as when it is on (reconciliation 3) — toggling the beam never moves the camera. Diagnostics record nothing special.
- **If the mouse aim flicks 180° in one frame**: `L_target` reverses; the slew clamp (D.7) limits the first frames to `144 × dt` px (2.4 px at 60 fps); 95 % of the reversal completes in ≈ 1.4 s; no cut predicate reads look-ahead, so no cut. The reversal is player-caused, satisfying C-R's 100 ms rule. Diagnostics record `diag.lookahead_slew_clamped = true` for the clamped frames.
- **If the aim is a zero vector or NaN** (no input yet, a disconnected device): `|aim| < ε` → `û` holds its previous value; on the very first frame of a session `û` defaults to the avatar's facing (down-screen south if none) and `L_target = L_MAX · û`. NaN is treated as zero. Diagnostics increment `diag.aim_invalid`.
- **If reduced motion is on**: `L_MAX = 0`, so `L_target = 0` and `L` eases to zero at the slew clamp when the setting is toggled mid-play; thereafter the camera is centred. Tremor, hairlines and audio carry the beam's forward information.
- **If look-ahead plus lag would push the avatar outside the inner box** (a 40 px target jump **south** while aiming north, opposite the lean: 40 + 72 = 112 > 108; a north jump gives 32 and does not engage the box) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: D.8 pins the pose at the box edge for the first frames; the avatar stays inside the central 60 %; no cut (the jump is below `JUMP_CUT`). Diagnostics record `diag.inner_box_engaged = true`.

**Frame rate**

- **If the render rate is 30 fps**: two ticks per frame; `tick_alpha` clamps at 1 after the second; a 1 px/tick body shows 2-px steps; input-to-pixel worst case 50 ms (kernel D.4); the solver's `T_PAN` is 3 frames; `present_time` advances 33 ms per frame. The sim is unchanged (`state_hash` identical across caps — the acceptance test).
- **If the render rate is 144 fps**: ticks on ≈ 42 % of frames; with interpolation and `Q = 1`, at `v_walk = 120` 16.7 % of frames repeat the pose (D.17) *(re-run 2026-10-26 — consistency check)* — honest, not a bug; never a backward or doubled step (step 12). `T_PAN` is 15 frames. If testers name the stutter, the `Q = 1/k` path removes it above 48 px/s.
- **If the panel is 40 Hz or 90 Hz** (Steam Deck options): rates not multiples of 60; the interpolation path keeps steps whole-base-pixel (alternating 1 and 2 base px for a 1 px/tick body at 40 fps) — at 40 fps `tick_alpha` alternates ≈ 0.5 / 1.0, at 90 fps ≈ 0.33 / 0.67 / 1.0. Spiked at both caps (step 13). Diagnostics record `Engine.get_frames_per_second()` and the repeated-pose count.
- **If the render rate falls below 20 fps**: the kernel runs up to `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` ticks then lets sim time lag (CR-17); a catch-up burst is at most `MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` × 3 px = 12 px, below `JUMP_CUT`, so it eases under D.6/D.8 and never cuts; a ≥ 48 px displacement in one frame needs a named source (a weld delta, a Body shove, a restore) and cuts (`P_jump`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The pipeline never compensates visually. Diagnostics record frame time, ticks per frame and the kernel's lag counter.

**Sorting and drawing**

- **If two Y-sorted items have the same sort origin** (two actors on one row; an actor on the first legal row south of a wall): the key is `(sort_y, class_rank, entity_id)` (PR-5, D.12), so an actor sorts after a wall, prop, door or machinery node of equal `sort_y` and draws over its face at every `x`, and two actors on one row order by `actor_id`. The order is deterministic across runs and across a save and reload, and independent of `x`: an actor walking past a prop's `x` on an equal row never changes the draw order (→ the tie-break ADR for the mechanism, `[UNV]`). Diagnostics do not record ties (they are normal) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If an actor stands exactly on a wall footprint line** (`y_foot = y_0 + 24`, the footprint's south edge): this row is the first legal row south of the wall (the footprint rows are collision); `sort_y` equal → tie → class rank → actor over face. If physics ever places a foot box overlapping a SOLID rect (`solid_in`) the actor is behind the wall by D.12 and visibly wrong — a Ship Body collision defect, caught by the debug tree-walk's "actor inside footprint" check; a foot box inside a passable door's gap is **not** that defect and never fires it. Diagnostics record `diag.actor_in_footprint` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If an actor walks through a door or doorway** (`area > 0`): the door sorts by its footprint's north edge (PR-5, D.12), so the actor is behind the frame while `y_foot < y_0`, over it while `y_0 ≤ y_foot`, and its head is visible throughout the crossing from either side; the door cannot close on the actor (Layout LY-11), so the sort switch to `y_0 + 24` never happens with an actor inside. A door that is closed (`area = 0`) is a wall. Diagnostics record nothing (a normal case) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **If a prop is taller than the 72 px face** (`f > 72`, e.g. a 96-px-tall machinery node placed flush against a wall): its top rows overlap the cap band (D.12 cap rows), and because caps draw above the Y-sort band the cap is drawn over the prop's top — visibly wrong. Outcome: the kit rule "face height ≤ 72 for anything flush against a wall" is enforced by the asset checklist (8.9 addition, routed to art-director/level-designer); at runtime the debug tree-walk flags any Y-sorted item whose top row is above `sort_y − 72` while within 24 px of a wall footprint (`diag.cap_overlap = [item]`). Machinery nodes' own caps (96×96 cells) are part of the node sprite, not the cap layer, so a node *not* against a wall is fine.
- **If a horror segment is rotated under nearest sampling**: pass A — stair-stepped on the base grid, accepted; pass B — angled k×k blocks, tolerated for horrors and web only (8.4); the rotation is quantised to 15° (a test) and stepped at 12 fps. If the step-2 review rejects the pass-B look, 8.10's remedies apply (24-angle sets or a base-resolution sub-viewport for horrors → ADR). A human-built node with `rotation ≠ 0` is a lint failure, not a tolerated state (`diag.rotated_human_geometry = [node]`).
- **If the z-band window would overflow** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the bands are world-anchored, so the count depends on the height of the union rect of the bodies drawn (loaded plus docked / detaching), not the field (D.13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `N_bands_body` ≤ 889 at `BAND_H = 24` (a body up to 21 336 rows); a taller body, or a `BAND_H` small enough to exceed the window (`BAND_H` 1 on a 2 880-row body), **refuses the fallback** — the Y-sort node stays whole, `diag.zband_overflow` records the body and the count, and nothing is clamped (a clamped band would mis-order walls against actors). Section G flags `BAND_H < 12` as unsafe for this reason.
- **If the Y-sorted layer exceeds ≈ 300 draws at the spike**: the D.13 fallback is built behind a flag and its **go/no-go runs**: draw calls and CPU with it on must both be lower than with it off at the largest ladder field of each target; if so it is enabled (wall faces and the wall-mounted layer leave the Y-sort node, nothing visible changes), otherwise it is withdrawn and the lever is the per-field draw cap (D.16). Diagnostics record per-viewport draw counts and CPU before and after *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

**Filter, layers and the HUD**

- **If the Perception Filter pass runs with no truth-channel mask available** (route (a) built but its mask viewport failed to allocate): the pass **is not applied** — the pipeline root sets the container's material to null and the world is shown unfiltered rather than letting trails, sprites, beams or the true cone edge be touched (Pillar 1: a lie that corrupts the truth channel is the one failure the game cannot have). Per-element Filter materials inside layer 0 still run. Diagnostics record `diag.filter_pass_disabled = NO_MASK`; the Debug Overlay shows it in red. **At MVP (route PER_ELEMENT) the container material is null by design**: `filter_route = PER_ELEMENT`, container state `NOT_USED`, not shown red; `NO_MASK` (red) applies only when route (a) is built and its mask fails to allocate (registry `filter_route`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from perception-filter)*.
- **If a HUD element is accidentally parented into the world `SubViewport`**: it is lit, glowed, filtered and scrolls with the camera — immediately visible; the debug tree-walk lints "any `Control` or node in the `hud` group under the world viewport" and fails the dev build (`diag.hud_in_world = [node]`). Release builds have no automatic remedy; the lint is in CI.
- **If a stray script sets `Camera2D.zoom` to anything but `(1,1)`**: the debug tree-walk (every frame behind the console toggle, and always at startup) resets it to `(1,1)`, logs the writer's stack and increments `diag.camera_zoom_violation`; the one frame rendered at the wrong zoom is accepted in dev and impossible in release because the startup assertion fails the build if any script references `Camera2D.zoom` as a writer (lint). Zoom-out is a logical-size change, never this property.
- **If `physics_interpolation` is turned on by a setting or a scene**: the startup assertion fails (`physics/common/physics_interpolation == false` is asserted); if toggled at runtime, the tree-walk logs `diag.second_interpolator = PHYSICS_INTERPOLATION` and the pipeline root sets `physics_interpolation_mode = Node.PHYSICS_INTERPOLATION_MODE_OFF` on its presentation roots (children inherit) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0009 sync)*. The symptom it prevents: a sprite swimming 1–3 render px against the camera at 144 fps while looking fine at 60.
- **If the letterbox bars are touched by a post-process** (a Filter pass or a `BackBufferCopy` sized to the window instead of the picture): the bars are outside the world viewport and the container, so a correctly placed pass cannot reach them; if a pass is found to cover the window, it is a defect — the startup assertion checks the container's rect equals the picture rect, and the debug tree-walk flags any `CanvasItem` on the root whose rect exceeds the picture (`diag.bar_overdraw = [node]`). Bars stay true black; the optional Graphite hairline is a HUD element inside the picture edge, never in the bar.
- **If the HR-15 ambient-floor step is raised to its maximum**: hull geometry only brightens by the measured floor + `HR15_STEP_MAX` (3) steps (Lighting's material) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from 2d-lighting-and-occlusion)*; web, horrors and filaments stay beam-only; the HUD is untouched; the pipeline changes nothing — recorded here because it is the one brightness control the player has and a tester will look for a gamma slider that does not exist (`diag` records the HR-15 step in the frame header).

**Flash clamp and photosensitivity**

- **If a light's flash count would exceed `F_MAX = 3` in any 1 s window**: Lighting's modifier contract holds the light at its last compliant luminance until the window admits the next transition (the clamp *delays* transitions, it never brightens or darkens beyond the requested levels); the pipeline's flash scan records `(light_id, F, window_start_tick, clamped_transitions)`. The alarm beacon and the cryo gutter never trigger it; an unspecified deployable flicker might — hence the routed number.
- **If two individually compliant lights overlap one region (a ninth of the displayed picture at the current field) and their sum exceeds the regional ceiling**: the regional check catches it; the newer light's transitions are delayed first (it is the one that pushed the region over). Diagnostics record `(region_index, F_region, light_ids)`. The frame-mean check is evaluated after the regional one and catches the case where many regions are each just under.
- **If "reduce flashing" and "reduced motion" are both on**: both apply — deployables steady, arcs held as glow, beacon static mark + sound + HUD glyph, cryo gutter kept (flashing); look-ahead 0, jolt 0, world effects per HR-8 (motion). Neither touches trail propagation or `present_time`. No conflict: one is luminance, the other is motion.
- **If a full-frame flash is requested by any layer** (a hypothetical "white-out" effect): refused by construction — no API exists to write the whole frame's luminance; a shader that attempts it on the container violates PR-10 ("only adds violet/flesh at 4.4's places") and the flash scan's frame-mean check records it as a defect (`diag.frame_mean_flash`).

**Startup and configuration**

- **If `project.godot` is missing a required key** (the `[display]` block, `default_texture_filter`, either `snap_2d_*` key, `hdr_2d`, `use_debanding`, the default clear colour, the two window size overrides, the HDR-output flag) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the debug startup assertion fails loudly with the key name, and the headless gdUnit4 parse test fails in CI before any window exists. A release build cannot be produced from a tree whose CI is red. Diagnostics (if the dev build is run anyway) print the full read-back table: `content_scale_mode`, `content_scale_factor`, `Window.min_size`, the ADR-0008 s5.4 scale readbacks, world viewport filter/snap/hdr/debanding members, container `texture_filter`, `physics_interpolation`, `position_smoothing_enabled`, `Camera2D.zoom`, driver name.
- **If the world `SubViewport` does not read back the project's filter/snap settings** (propagation is NOT SOURCEABLE): the pipeline root sets the viewport's own members explicitly at boot regardless, so the read-back passes either way; step 11 records whether the explicit set was necessary.
- **If the driver reported at startup is not Vulkan**: the driver is pinned to Vulkan by ADR-0008 § 1 and § 5.1 and asserted by effective value (`ProjectSettings` in CI, the live driver name at boot); a reported driver other than Vulkan is a `StartupCheck` assertion failure, not an unmeasured case. The name is still logged (`diag.driver`); V-sync/swap-chain behaviour is measured on the pinned driver (step 13). *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)*

- **If the window is wider than 16:9 and `expand` is enabled**: the visible base field widens up to the 960×540 cap at the same integer `k`; the extra columns show whatever is there — honest void in every dark state, and extra lit floor only where loops are running (chiefly the stable home-ship). No information the beam has not paid for is revealed, because unlit geometry remains at the ambient floor and web content is never drawn unlit. Diagnostics record the expanded base size. *(Creative-director recommendation, ratified by the user 2026-10-26 — Open Question 9.)*

## Dependencies

**Upstream (this system depends on).** The systems index lists none, and that is correct for *data*: the pipeline consumes no game state of its own. It does depend on two contracts it must honour rather than systems it must wait for:

| Dependency | Nature | Interface | Status |
|------------|--------|-----------|--------|
| Command & Simulation Layer | Contract (hard) | The pipeline's camera and every visual system subscribe as `PRESENTATION`; read last-committed state through read views; interpolate with `tick_alpha` and previous/current values; never cause a tick; the Perception Filter hook is a `PRESENTATION` consumer of the filtered channel; the Debug Overlay is `DIAGNOSTIC` (CR-10, CR-11) | GDD designed (`design/gdd/command-simulation-layer.md`) |
| Art bible | Constraint (hard) | 640×360 integer-scaled base, 48 px tile, projection convention and pixel-size table (8.1, 8.2), project/import settings and the SubViewport architecture with pass A/B (8.4), HUD in the root viewport (7.0), HR-1/HR-2/HR-13 (HUD exempt from world light; opaque plates; no HUD shake/blur/scale change), the lighting-budget spike plan (8.12) | Approved (AD-ART-BIBLE 2026-10-25) |
| Godot 4.7.2 (pinned) | Engine | Stretch/scale modes, `SubViewport`, snap settings, texture filter defaults, `Camera2D`, `TileMapLayer` Y-sort, `CanvasLayer` — facts sourced from `docs/engine-reference/godot/` and the art bible's `[BIN]` class-dump notes; everything else NOT SOURCEABLE until the spike | Installed and matching the pin |

**Downstream (systems that depend on this one)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: rebuilt from the Interactions table; the old table listed ten of seventeen and carried a camera-drift clause that contradicted C1 and C9).* All hard unless stated: a visual system that bypassed the pipeline would be drawing off-grid or outside the layer model. **Bidirectional consistency** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The systems index names eleven dependents (IDX:63; Lighting, Controller, Interaction, Failure & Trail, Ship Body, Perception Filter, HUD, Chart Table, Debug Overlay, and the two Settings systems); this GDD finds twenty-two — eleven more (Modular Ship Assembly, In-World Instruments, Horror Entities, Web Map & Transit, Attention, Save/Load, Ship Section & Layout Data Model, Derelict Authoring, Emitter State, Suit & Equipment, Adaptive Audio) are flagged for the index (`/map-systems` update, a cross-GDD note); the Ship Body and Interaction edges are already added *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: five rows added: 18–22)*. Most designed GDDs in the table name this system in their own Dependencies or Cross-References, but **not all** (the claim that no other document needs correction was overclaimed): Adaptive Audio's own row is "consistent by absence" and routes a one-line row to this GDD (its RF-19, handled by row 22 here); and Ship Section & Layout's `creation_id` citation (its former L1227 text, now `entity_id`) and the wording of the `entity_id` namespace are Layout's to confirm (cross-GDD note); registry `referenced_by` lists for `camera_follow`, `speed_ceiling` and `camera_lookahead` need the entries and value changes in the registry requests below *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**What the pipeline refuses.** Any sprite or tile position that is not an integer in logical pixels (except web and horror rig segments, which may rotate off-grid by art bible 8.4); any HUD element drawn inside the world viewport; any camera motion that is not derived from committed simulation state plus `tick_alpha`; any post-process that reaches the HUD layer; and any dimming, blur or desaturation of the world under a menu (art bible 7.4 — the world freezes lit exactly as it was).

## Tuning Knobs

*Every knob traces to a Section D variable. **Set-once** knobs are fixed at authoring time and changing them invalidates assets or the sort contract; **build** knobs are flags decided by the spike and fixed per build; **runtime** knobs are tunable in data; **Settings** knobs are player-exposed through PR-14 (all integer or boolean). All defaults are provisional until the spike (8.12 steps 2, 5, 11–13); speeds marked † are the Player Controller GDD's values (registry `V_WALK` / `V_RUN`), not this system's knobs; length knobs marked ‡ scale by `s = field_h / 360` at a zoom-ladder step (D.5). *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check.)* Where this GDD's symbol differs from the registry's, the knob is tagged `(registry: NAME)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, registry alignment)*.

| Knob | Kind | Default | Safe range | D ref | Affects | Breaks too low | Breaks too high | Interactions |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Base resolution (registry: `BASE_RESOLUTION`) | set-once | 640×360 | fixed | D.1 | every scale, every asset, 7.1 typography | — | — | changing it reopens 7.1 and 8.1 (decided; not negotiable) |
| Tile size (registry: `TILE_PX`) | set-once | 48 px | fixed | D.12 | kit, footprints, helmet arithmetic | — | — | 8.2 (decided) |
| Projection convention | set-once | up-screen (faces occlude screen-north; `sort_y` = footprint south edge) | fixed — art bible 8.2 amended 2026-10-26 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | D.12 | every Y-sorted dependent, kit south walls | — | — | resolved; the spike's Y-sort room is the test (the literal reading would force a 96-row footprint) |
| Footprint / face / cap | set-once | 24 / 72 / 24 px | fixed | D.12 | sort rows, cap-above invariant | — | — | caps-above safe iff no flush item's face > 72 |
| Minimum window (registry: `MIN_WINDOW`) | set-once | 1280×720 | fixed | D.1 | `k ≥ 2`, HUD floor | below it `k = 1` and text falls under HR-12 | — | C-R |
| `CAP_W × CAP_H` (expand cap) | runtime | 960×540 | 640×360 – 960×540 | D.3 | field size on odd windows; information gap; pass-B cost | 640×360 = `keep` (bars everywhere) | > 960×540 gives odd-window players a frame wider than the zoom-out view | manual `k < k_auto` forces `keep` regardless |
| Aspect `keep` / `expand` | Settings (post-spike) | `keep` (spike) → `expand` | the two | D.2 / D.3 | bars vs wider field | — | — | creative-director's information caveat; Deck +40 rows |
| Integer world scale | Settings | Auto (= D.1) + list ≥ 2 | 2 – k_auto | D.1 | picture size; manual below Auto forces `keep` | 1 is not offered as a *setting* (it is a ladder step) | — | the zoom ladder `[1, k_auto + 2]` is built on the active `k_auto` |
| `H` (HUD scale) | Settings | `max(k, 3)` | `H_default` – `2·H_default`, offered only where the HUD's `bottom_row_fit` holds (`H_default` always — the fallback when `fit` is false; H 5 and 6 not offered at a 1280-px picture until CF-HS-11 is resolved) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from hud-and-suit-layer)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* | D.4 | cap height (labelled), HUD canvas, 7.0 size caps | < 3 falls under the 21 px floor — not offered | 2× exceeds 7.0's critical-strip cap knowingly (7.1) | Deck default 3× over a 2× world forces the two-scale wiring |
| Reading-face scale | Settings | 100 % | 80 – 200 % continuous | — | menus, logs, tooltips only | contrast floor at these sizes | layout overflow (Chart Table's) | the one continuous scale |
| Frame cap | Settings | off (0) | 30 / 60 / 120 / 144 / 240 / off | D.17, D.18 | repeated-pose fraction, latency `L_render` | 30: 2-px steps, 50 ms worst latency | — | V-sync off + cap = benchmarking mode |
| V-sync | Settings | enabled | enabled / mailbox / off | D.18 | tearing, `L_render` | off: tearing | — | never adaptive by default (driver-dependent) |

**Build-time invariants Section G asserts (fail the build when violated):** `LAG_CUT > v_max · τ_follow` (48 > 180 × 0.18 = 32.4); `INNER_BOX_FRAC · field_h > v_max · τ_follow_eff + L_MAX_eff` (INNER_BOX_FRAC = 0.30) **asserted at every reachable ladder field**, not only the default — with `τ_follow_eff = τ_follow × min(1, s)` and `L_MAX_eff = L_MAX × s` the inequality holds everywhere iff it holds at 360 rows for `s ≤ 1` (scale-free) and reads `36·s > 32.4` for `s > 1`: 360 → 108 > 104.4; 270 (k = 4 at 1080p) → 81 > 78.3; 216 (k = 5) → 64.8 > 62.6; 540 (k = 2) → 162 > 140.4; 480 (one step out at 1440p, `k_z` = 3) → 144 > 128.4; 720 (two steps out at 1440p, `k_z` = 2) → 216 > 176.4; 1080 (1× at 1080p) → 324 > 248.4; Deck 400 → 120 > 112.4; Deck 800 (1×) → 240 > 192.4; **the low-fps case** — the same two invariants are also evaluated with the lag measured after the update at `DT_MAX` and at 30 fps (`lag = v_max·τ_eff·h/(e^h − 1)` = 28.1 and 29.5 px at the default field, D.6), where every margin is strictly wider than with the `lag_∞` bound for **render rates of 15 fps and above**, so the build fails only if the `dt → 0` bound fails; **below about 15 fps the claim "margins only widen" is false** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the kernel caps catch-up at `n_max = MAX_TICKS_PER_FRAME` = 4 ticks, so the target moves up to `n_max · v_max/60` = 12 px per frame while `dt'` is clamped to `DT_MAX` = 0.05 s (`a = 1 − exp(−0.05/0.18) = 0.2427`), and the after-update lag is `12 · (1 − a)/a` = **37.4 px**; `37.4 + 72 = 109.4 > 108` by 1.4 px, so the inner box engages (`diag.inner_box_engaged`, a 1.4 px pin) in that regime and the build asserts the worst case `n_max·v_max/60·(1 − a)/a < LAG_CUT` (37.4 < 48, no cut) rather than `< INNER_BOX_FRAC·field_h − L_MAX_eff` (accepted engagement, documented, never a cut); `Z_WIN_LO + ceil(body_rows_max / BAND_H) + 2 ≤ Z_WIN_HI < range_z_max` with `body_rows_max` the tallest shippable body (2 880 rows → 132 ≤ 899) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from 2d-lighting-and-occlusion; rewritten 2026-10-27 — design review for world-anchored bands)*; `H_default ≥ 3`; `k_min = 2`; `Q ∈ {1, 1/k}`; `JOLT_MAX ≤ 2`; `F_MAX = 3`; `SETTLE_EPS ≤ 0.5·Q`; `LEAN_R1 > LEAN_R0 ≥ 8` (PC-5's mouse dead-zone, base px) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check.)*

## Visual/Audio Requirements

The pipeline draws nothing of its own and plays nothing. What it *requires* of visual systems is the contract in Section C: every presentation element lives in the world `SubViewport` or on the root HUD layer, never both; every sprite and tile is placed at integer base pixels by presentation code (the viewport snap flags are a safety net, not the mechanism); human-built geometry never rotates at runtime; horror rigs and web pieces rotate only in the quantised steps the art bible fixes; VFX and one-off sprites use even cell sizes or `centered = false` with an integer offset so no origin lands on a half pixel; hairlines are sprites, never 1-px line draws (4.7 draws lines without an antialiasing feather). Two art-bible rules become pipeline rules here: the only brightness control in the game is the bounded ambient-floor step implemented as the hull-material raise (HR-15; no full-frame gamma or brightness exists), and letterbox bars, where they occur, are true black outside the world and get a 1-HUD-px Graphite hairline at the viewport edge so a bar is never read as unlit hull (routed to art-director); the hairline is a layer-1 element, excluded from the HUD's critical-strip count *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from hud-and-suit-layer)*. The art bible's 8.2 projection wording carries the Y-sortable convention (height projects up-screen; faces occlude screen-north) since the 2026-10-26 amendment; the spike's Y-sort room remains its test *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Audio: none, except that the pipeline's render-rate loop is not the sim clock and audio cues keyed to tells must come from the simulation's notifications, never from frame events. No asset-spec flag: there are no assets.

## Game Feel

The camera is the one piece of this system the player feels, and its feel target is **absence**: a window bolted to the ship, not a lens that performs.

- **Stillness.** When the crewmate stops, the frame stops — within the follow time constant and then exactly: the per-axis settle (`SETTLE_EPS`, D.6/D.7) is the mechanism that makes "exactly" true, and the last base pixel can land up to ≈ 0.75 s after the stop (the follow term `f`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the look-ahead `L` keeps easing for up to ≈ 2.5 s after a full aim reversal, which is **player-caused** (the aim moved it) and is not the avatar's stop, so "the frame stops" is measured on `f` for a stop and on `L` only after the aim has stopped too; the settle is `max(t_settle_f, t_settle_L)` per axis *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. No bounce, no overshoot (the first-order follow cannot overshoot by construction), no idle drift, no breathing. The frame is the still thing readings move inside (art bible 7.3).
- **Follow.** First-order exponential follow, τ ≈ 0.18 s, no dead-zone by default (a dead-zone starts and stops discontinuously, which reads as "something happened"); the crewmate is held inside the central ~60 % of the frame at walking and running speed. Lag at run speed ≈ 0.6 tile.
- **Look-ahead.** The frame leans where the beam points: 1.5 tiles (72 px) along the aim direction by default, range 1.0–1.56 tiles (48–75 px), eased over τ ≈ 0.40 s from the *local raw* aim so a mouse flick does not yank the frame, with the mouse aim measured open-loop (the lean never turns the beam) and the lean scaled by aim magnitude *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; while running, half the lean follows the direction of movement (`LOOKAHEAD_RUN_WEIGHT` 0.5), so a runner aiming straight back sees a centred frame rather than a backward one *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. This buys back cone rim on the short axis exactly when the player is looking there — because they looked, not because the game did.
- **Motion quantum.** The camera moves in whole base pixels (MVP). Motion at walking speed is uniform stepping, which reads as smooth; the 1/k-screen-pixel quantum is the named upgrade if testers name the stutter, and needs art-director and ux-designer sign-off because it lets the hero sprite wobble ±0.5 base px against the frame.
- **Rhythm independence.** Identical camera constants in boarding and breach; the frantic feeling comes from more things moving in the world, never from the frame fighting the player (art bible 2.8). Identical input traces produce identical camera traces in calm, tell and breach — an acceptance criterion, not a hope.
- **Cuts, not pans.** Re-wake, pod arrival, a body transfer whose hull jump is not guaranteed zero and any follow failure (pan-speed ceiling exceeded, lag over one tile — `LAG_CUT` 48 px, a 48-px jump) are cuts; a weld or detach transfer is a rebase that moves nothing on screen *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The camera never pans across geography the player did not walk.
- **No shake in MVP.** A bounded physical jolt (≤ 2 base px, decayed, on hull separation / docking / detach only, never on Attention or sanity) exists as a knob defaulting to 0, to be revisited at VS-A once the HUD-still-over-moving-world read is tested. Hull stress goes into real needles, dust and hairlines.
- **Latency.** Input-to-pixel is the kernel's ≤ 1 tick plus one render frame; acknowledgement is same-frame on the presentation side. At 30 fps motion shows every second tick's pose honestly; at 144 and 90 fps interpolation with `tick_alpha` places each base-pixel step at the right instant and never twice in a frame; at 40 fps steps alternate 1 and 2 base px *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4)*.
- **Accessibility couplings.** Reduced motion disables look-ahead, zeroes the jolt and applies the HR-8 world-effect reductions listed in PR-14 and Tuning Knobs (static direction shapes, static alarm mark); it never suppresses trails, tremor, hairlines or audio. "Reduce flashing" is a separate toggle. Neither alters the tick.

*Anti-targets*: cinematic takeover, revealing pans, auto-aim, free-look, sanity wobble, handheld energy, Dutch tilt, non-integer or tweened zoom, sub-pixel camera position.

## UI Requirements

No player-facing UI of its own, but the pipeline defines three surfaces other systems' UI must fit:

1. **The HUD layer contract** (consumed by HUD & Suit Layer, Chart Table, Crew Strip, Interaction prompts): a root-viewport `CanvasLayer` outside the world `SubViewport`, integer-scaled at `H_default = max(k, 3)` — never below 3× on any display, player-raisable to 2×H in integer steps, labelled by resulting cap height; authored anchor-relative to the visible viewport edges with a 5 % safe margin in HUD pixels, surviving a fractional logical canvas (Steam Deck 1280×800 ÷ 3) and both `keep` and `expand` aspects; exempt by construction from world glow, ambient, occlusion, the Perception Filter pass, camera motion and jolt (HR-1, HR-13). World-anchored prompts project through the *unjolted* camera transform and are drawn only for lit objects; they never clamp to the frame edge. *Consequence routed to art-director*: on the Deck the 3× HUD over a 2× world exceeds 7.0's 12 % × 5 % critical-strip cap by default.
2. **The Settings surface** (consumed by Settings & Input Rebinding and Accessibility Settings — Alpha systems, but the hooks exist from MVP): display mode (fullscreen / borderless / windowed), resolution, world scale (Auto = largest integer that fits, plus an explicit integer list; manual scale below Auto forces `keep`), HUD scale, reading-face scale, aspect `keep` / `expand` once `expand` ships post-spike, frame cap and VSync (`ENABLED` default; `MAILBOX` as uncapped-no-tear; never `ADAPTIVE` by default), look-ahead strength (off / short / default; added to PR-14 by the amendment note below), reduce flashing (**default ON at first launch** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; jolt strength is hidden until the VS-A jolt decision — a setting for a feature that ships at 0 is not exposed), reduced motion, and the bounded HR-15 ambient-floor step. No non-integer world scale is offered anywhere. Minimum window 1280×720. A first-launch photosensitivity notice is shown and reachable from settings, and a first-launch **guide-only** black-level calibration screen (a barely-visible reference swatch on the real floor values; writes no game value — user decision 2026-10-26) precedes it.
3. **The diagnostic surface** (consumed by the Debug Overlay, dev builds only, `DIAGNOSTIC` class): current k and H, window and viewport sizes, letterbox remainder, pass A/B mode, camera state (follow / clamped / zoom step / cut reason), quantum, frame time, per-viewport draw calls (world and HUD counted separately via `Viewport.get_render_info`), flash-clamp engagements per light and per region, fractional-transform tree-walk findings, startup-assertion results including the rendering driver name. Absent from release builds.

> **📌 UX Flag — Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera**: this system has UI requirements (the settings surface and the HUD-layer contract). In Pre-Production, run `/ux-design` for the settings screen and the HUD layer spec before writing epics; stories should cite `design/ux/settings.md` and `design/ux/hud.md`, not this GDD directly. Noted for the systems index.

## Acceptance Criteria

*Every criterion is pass/fail without reading the rest of this document. Tags in brackets name the Core Rule (PR-n, camera clause Cn), state, edge case or Section D formula the criterion discharges. Evidence class per criterion: **(auto)** gdUnit4 test under `tests/unit/render/`, run headless by `commands.test` (exit 0 = pass; 100 = failures; 101 = passed but leaked orphan Nodes, which counts as a FAIL for this system) — only criteria that are pure functions of their inputs (`CameraSolver`, the Section D arithmetic, `project.godot` parsing, scene-tree walks over instantiated-but-unrendered scenes) may carry this tag, because the headless runner has no real rasteriser; **(lint)** a static scan run in CI; **(review)** a code-review checkbox; **(manual)** an instrumented play or screenshot on a dev build with retained evidence (PNG, frame-diff image or CSV) in `production/qa/evidence/render/`. Numbers derived from `v_walk`, `v_run`, `v_max` use the Player Controller GDD's values (`V_WALK` 2 px/tick = 120 base px/s, `V_RUN` = `v_max` 3 px/tick = 180 base px/s) and were re-run against them on 2026-10-26; at a zoom-ladder step the solver's length parameters scale by `s = field_h / 360` and the criteria below are stated at the 360-row field unless they say otherwise. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check.)* Items marked *(adopted — decision log 2026-10-26)* rest on the Section D amendment note or the zoom-on-2× decision and must be deleted — not quietly kept — if the log does not adopt them. All defaults are the Section G values and are provisional until the lighting spike (8.12 steps 2, 5, 11–13). Where a criterion says "screenshot" it means a lossless capture of the final composed frame at window resolution; where it says "layer capture" it means the same with every other layer hidden.*

**Test fixtures** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Criteria that name a fixture use these definitions, kept as a checked-in constant file under `tests/unit/render/fixtures/` (no inline magic numbers except boundary values): (1) **the spike room** is the Y-sort room of spike step 5 — 32 prompts-capable interactables, 1 wall run of 12 tiles with a face and cap, 6 props, 2 machinery nodes, 1 door with `area` 0 and 1 with `area > 0`, 3 actors on one row and 1 on the first legal row south of a wall, 8 pipe/emissive pieces, 15 effect draws — giving the Example's `D_world` of 213 (`4 + 10 + 180 + 4 + 15`) at the default field and `D_ysort` of 180 *(the object counts in this item are [PLACEHOLDER] proposals; the authoritative requirement is the draw total — `D_ysort` 180 and `D_world` 213 — which the authored room must reproduce, and spike step 5 replaces the counts)*; (2) **calm / tell / breach read-view fixtures** are three constant read-view tables (a stand-in for Attention and Horror, which are designed but have no runtime) holding the same body and actor and differing only in the Attention/horror blocks; (3) **the recorded 60 s input trace** is a checked-in table of `(t_us, move octant, run, aim, zoom press)`; (4) **a `PROJECTOR` stub** sets the limits rect and `frame_delta_px`; (5) **the injected monotonic clock** `Clock.now_us()` replaces wall time wherever a criterion measures an interval (the zoom rate limit, `T_PAN`, the 1 s flash window); (6) **the tooling stories** that deliver these (the fixture loader, the clock, the windowed-capture runner of Open Question 22) are `/create-stories` items under the render epic and block the criteria that use them.

### Scale & window

- **GIVEN** the `world_scale` function, **WHEN** evaluated for windows 1280×720, 1280×800, 1366×768, 1365×768, 1600×900, 1920×1080, 1920×1200, 2560×1080, 2560×1440, 3440×1440, 3840×2160 and 7680×4320, **THEN** `k` is 2, 2, 2, 2, 2, 3, 3, 3, 4, 4, 6 and 12 respectively, is an `int` in every case, and `content_scale_factor` reads 1.0 regardless of the `screen_get_dpi()` value supplied. (auto) [PR-1; D.1 `world_scale`]
- **GIVEN** a dev build whose pipeline root logs the ADR-0008 s5.4 readback chain on every `RESCALING` frame (world effective scale = root `get_final_transform` scale x `WorldContainer.get_global_transform` scale x `WorldViewport.get_stretch_transform` scale; HUD effective scale = root `get_final_transform` scale x `HudLayer.transform` scale), **WHEN** the window is set to 1280×720, 1280×800, 1366×768, 1920×1080, 2560×1440, 3440×1440 and 3840×2160 in turn, **THEN** the world scale reads exactly `(2,2)`, `(2,2)`, `(2,2)`, `(3,3)`, `(4,4)`, `(4,4)`, `(6,6)` and the HUD scale exactly `(3,3)`, `(3,3)`, `(3,3)`, `(3,3)`, `(4,4)`, `(4,4)`, `(6,6)` — no fractional component in any entry; log retained. (manual) [PR-1, PR-7, PR-12; D.1, D.4]
- **GIVEN** a test scene whose world content is a 1 base px black/white checkerboard filling the viewport, **WHEN** a screenshot is taken at each of the seven windows above, **THEN** every pixel inside the picture rect is exactly one of the two colours, every maximal run of one colour along any row or column is exactly `k` screen px long, and the picture contains no third colour value. (manual) [PR-1, PR-4 rule 1; D.1]
- **GIVEN** aspect `keep`, **WHEN** `letterbox_bars` is evaluated at 1600×900, 1365×768, 1366×768, 3440×1440, 1280×800 and 1920×1080, **THEN** (left, right, top, bottom) are (160, 160, 90, 90), (42, 43, 24, 24), (43, 43, 24, 24), (440, 440, 0, 0), (0, 0, 40, 40) and (0, 0, 0, 0), all `int`. (auto) [PR-2; D.2 `letterbox_bars`]
- **GIVEN** a 1600×900 window under `keep`, the Perception Filter pass forced to its maximum stage and a test-forced jolt `J_0 = 2` active, **WHEN** a screenshot is taken on 20 consecutive frames, **THEN** every pixel in the four bar rectangles is `(0, 0, 0)` on every frame, and the 1-HUD-px Graphite hairline, if present, lies inside the picture rect. (manual) [PR-2; D.2]
- **GIVEN** aspect `expand` with `CAP_W × CAP_H = 960×540`, **WHEN** `expanded_field` is evaluated at 3440×1440, 1280×800, 1600×900, 1366×768, 1365×768, 2560×1080 and a 3840×1080 window (`k = 3`), **THEN** `(L_w, L_h)` is (860, 360), (640, 400), (800, 450), (682, 384), (682, 384), (852, 360) and (960, 360) — every component even — the residual bars (left/right, top/bottom) are (0/0, 0/0) except (1/1, 0/0) at 1366×768, (0/1, 0/0) at 1365×768, (2/2, 0/0) at 2560×1080 and (480/480, 0/0) at 3840×1080, where the 960 cap binds (worked: k = floor(min(3840/640, 1080/360)) = min(6, 3) = 3; L_w = min(2·floor(3840/(2·3)), 960) = min(2·640, 960) = min(1280, 960) = 960; L_h = min(2·floor(1080/6), 540) = min(360, 540) = 360; residual_x = (3840 − 960·3)/2 = (3840 − 2880)/2 = 480 each side, residual_y = (1080 − 360·3)/2 = 0), and no case is below 640×360 or above 960×540. (auto) [PR-2; D.3 `expanded_field`] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4: AC recomputed from D.3)*
- **GIVEN** a 1920×1080 window with aspect set to `expand` and the integer world scale set manually to 2 (`k_auto = 3`), **WHEN** the pipeline re-derives, **THEN** the effective aspect is `keep`, the logical size is 640×360 and the bars are (320, 320, 180, 180); setting the scale back to Auto restores `expand` with logical 640×360 and no bars. (auto) [PR-2, PR-14; D.1, D.3]
- **GIVEN** the pipeline root booted, **WHEN** `Window.min_size` is read, **THEN** it is `(1280, 720)`; **GIVEN** a test harness that delivers a 1024×768 `size_changed`, **THEN** `k == 2`, `diag.window_below_min == true`, the delivered size is recorded, and the picture rect is 1280×720 centred on the window (edges outside it). (auto) [PR-1; Edge Cases "smaller than 1280×720"]
- **GIVEN** `PRESENTING` at 1920×1080, **WHEN** the window is resized to 2560×1440, toggled fullscreen, and moved between two monitors with different DPI but the same pixel size, **THEN** diagnostics shows exactly one `RESCALING` frame per `size_changed`, zero `RESCALING` frames for the DPI-only move, `k` is 3 → 4 → 4 → 4, and a screenshot of the first frame after each change has an integer final transform (checkerboard test passes on that frame). (manual) [PR-1, PR-2; States `RESCALING`; Edge Cases "DPI changes"]
- **GIVEN** a breach in progress with the beacon sweeping, **WHEN** fullscreen is toggled, **THEN** `diag.cut_reason` is empty for the `RESCALING` frame, `pose_q` is identical before and after, `present_time` advances by one render delta with no discontinuity, and the kernel's tick count is unaffected. (manual) [PR-2, PR-9; Edge Cases "fullscreen toggled during breach"]

### Layers & HUD exemption

- **GIVEN** `hud_scale`, **WHEN** evaluated for `k` = 2, 3, 4, 6 and 12, **THEN** `H_default` is 3, 3, 4, 6, 12; the offered set is `{H_default … 2·H_default}` in integer steps; `cap_px = 7·H` labels at 1080p read 21, 28, 35, 42 px; and `cap_px ≥ 21` for every `(W, H)` in the PR-1 table. (auto) [PR-12; D.4 `hud_scale`]
- **GIVEN** a 1280×800 window under `expand` with `H = 3`, **WHEN** the HUD canvas is computed, **THEN** it reads `(426.67, 266.67)` (±0.01); **WHEN** the HUD scene is laid out at that canvas and at 1920×1080 under `keep`, **THEN** a screenshot shows every HUD element fully inside the picture rect with no element nearer the picture edge than 5 % of the picture rounded up to whole HUD px (registry `safe_margin`): 22 × 14 HUD px at 1280×800, H 3; 32 × 18 HUD px at 1920×1080, H 3 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from hud-and-suit-layer)*. (auto for the canvas; manual for the layout) [PR-2, PR-12; D.4]
- **GIVEN** the world `SubViewportContainer` hidden and a test-forced jolt `J_0 = 2` fired, **WHEN** the HUD layer is captured on each of the following 20 frames with every HUD needle frozen (fixture readings), **THEN** the pixel diff between any two captures is empty. (manual) [PR-3, PR-6 C7; HR-13]
- **GIVEN** the Perception Filter pass forced to sanity stage 3 (its maximum), **WHEN** the HUD layer is captured and compared to a capture of the same HUD state with the pass disabled, **THEN** the pixel diff is empty. (manual) [PR-3, PR-10; HR-1]
- **GIVEN** world ambient at 0 % with every light off, then ambient at 100 % with every light on and glow enabled, **WHEN** the HUD layer is captured in each state (world container hidden), **THEN** the two captures are bit-identical. (manual) [PR-3; HR-1]
- **GIVEN** the pipeline in `TRANSIT_HOLD` and separately in `LOADING`, **WHEN** a screenshot is taken, **THEN** every pixel inside the picture rect that is not covered by a HUD element is `(0, 0, 0)`, the HUD is visible, and the root viewport's draw count exceeds the HUD-only count by exactly 1 (the hold quad). (manual) [PR-3; States `TRANSIT_HOLD`; D.16 `D_hold`]
- **GIVEN** `PRESENTING` with the beacon sweeping and dust moving, **WHEN** the kernel enters `PAUSED` and 5 s elapse, **THEN** `present_time` is unchanged to the microsecond, a screenshot of the picture is bit-identical to the last `PRESENTING` frame (no dim, blur or desaturation; no animation advanced), and a chart-table element animating on process time has advanced. On `PAUSED → RUNNING` the next frame's `present_time` differs from the frozen value by one render delta. (auto for `present_time`; manual for the screenshots) [PR-9; States `PAUSED_FROZEN`; art bible 7.4]
- **GIVEN** `PRESENTING` at 1920×1080 with `H = 3`, **WHEN** `zoom_out` is pressed once, **THEN** the HUD layer capture is bit-identical to the capture before the press and the HUD final transform still reads `(3,3)` while the world's reads `(2,2)` *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check)*. (manual) [PR-12, PR-6 C6; D.5]

### Pixel integrity & motion

- **GIVEN** a static room with the sim held (no committed change for the test window), the camera target stationary and a declared list of animating sources (each as a base-px rect: light flicker, scrolling medium, web drift), **WHEN** 120 consecutive frames are captured at 60 fps and again at 144 fps, **THEN** every pixel that differs between any two consecutive frames lies inside a declared rect, and the count of differing pixels outside every declared rect is 0 in both runs; diff images retained. (manual) [PR-4; Player Fantasy design test 2 (static scene)]
- **GIVEN** the same room with a floor landmark pixel and an actor walked across it at `v_walk` and then at `v_run` while the camera follows, **WHEN** per frame the screen-px offset between the actor's foot-row anchor and the landmark is recorded, **THEN** every change in that offset between consecutive frames is a multiple of `k` (0 or ±k, never ±1 … ±(k−1)), and the total change over the walk equals `k ×` the committed base-px displacement; CSV retained. (manual) [PR-4 rules 2–3, PR-6 C3; Player Fantasy design test 2 (followed actor); D.9]
- **GIVEN** `quantise`, **WHEN** evaluated with `prev = 100, curr = 102, tick_alpha = 0.42` and `0.6`, **THEN** `p` is 100 and 101; **WHEN** evaluated with `c = 311.7` at `Q = 1` and `Q = 1/3`, **THEN** `pose_q` is 311 and 311.667 (±1e-6) with `o` = 0 and −5 screen px respectively. (auto) [PR-6 C3; D.9 `quantise`]
- **GIVEN** a read view whose committed position advances exactly 1 base px per tick and frame-time sequences for 144, 90, 60, 40 and 30 fps, **WHEN** `p` is computed per frame for 10 s, **THEN** (a) at 144, 90 and 60 fps (f_render ≥ f_tick = 60) every consecutive difference is 0 or +1 — never 2, never negative (exactly +1 every frame at 60 fps; ticks per frame 0 or 1 at 144 and 90); (b) at 40 fps the difference is +1 or +2 in strict alternation (ticks per frame 1, 2, 1, 2 — 1.5 on average, so `p` steps 1 then 2), never 0, never above 2, never negative; (c) at 30 fps it is exactly +2 every frame; (d) at every rate the sum of differences equals the ticks elapsed. General rule: for a 1 px/tick body the per-frame step is the number of ticks committed in the frame, at most ceil(60 / f_render), so "0 or +1" holds exactly when f_render ≥ 60. (auto) [PR-4 rule 3, PR-11; D.9] *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4: AC recomputed from the PR-11 table)*
- **GIVEN** `repeated_pose_fraction`, **WHEN** evaluated, **THEN** `R_0` at `f_render = 144` is 0.583 (±0.001); `R` at `v = 120, Q = 1, f_render = 144` is 0.167 (±0.001); at `Q = 1/3` is 0; at `v = 180, Q = 1` is 0; at `v = 60, f_render = 144` is 0.583; at `v = 60, f_render = 60` is 0 *(re-run 2026-10-26 — consistency check)*. (auto) [D.17 `repeated_pose_fraction`]
- **GIVEN** a dev build at a 144 fps cap with interpolation and `Q = 1`, **WHEN** an actor moves at `v_walk = 120` for 10 s and the frame counter records frames whose `p` equals the previous frame's, **THEN** the repeated-pose count is ≤ 0.167 × frames + 2 % *(re-run 2026-10-26 — consistency check)* and no frame shows a backward or 2-px step. (manual) [PR-11; D.17]
- **GIVEN** the kernel's G3 fixture with the pipeline root, camera host and one presentation subscriber attached, and a scripted 10 s input replay, **WHEN** the run is repeated at `max_fps` 30, 60, 144 and 0 (uncapped), **THEN** `state_hash(ALL_SIM)` after the replay is identical across all four runs, the kernel tick count is identical, and the pipeline's `diag.ticks_per_frame` histogram differs (proving the render rate actually varied). (auto; runs in the kernel suite with the pipeline present) [PR-11; kernel G3, CR-11]
- **GIVEN** `motion_latency`, **WHEN** evaluated with `tick_alpha_draw = 0.05, L_render = 5 ms`, **THEN** 20.8 ms (±0.1); **GIVEN** a dev build at 60 fps with a frame-stamp overlay, **WHEN** 100 continuous-motion samples are taken, **THEN** the 95th-percentile **software latency** (from the tick commit's timestamp to the timestamp at which the frame that draws it is composed, read from the injected monotonic clock) is ≤ 33.3 ms (one tick plus one frame). This criterion measures software latency only: it makes **no claim about perceived or photon latency**, which also includes `L_render`'s present wait, the display's scan-out and its own processing and which no build-side timestamp can see. (auto for the formula; manual for the measurement) [PR-11; D.18 `motion_latency`] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **GIVEN** the booted scene, **WHEN** the project setting and the tree are read, **THEN** `physics/common/physics_interpolation == false`, every presentation root has `physics_interpolation_mode == Node.PHYSICS_INTERPOLATION_MODE_OFF` and every node under one inherits it (read after the first `process_frame`) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0009 sync)*, and the world `Camera2D` (if the variant has one; otherwise the world tree contains no `Camera2D` and the world viewport's `canvas_transform` is written by the camera host alone) has `position_smoothing_enabled == false` and `limit_smoothed == false`. (auto) [PR-4 rule 8] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **GIVEN** every `Sprite2D` and `AnimatedSprite2D` under the world `SubViewport`, **WHEN** scanned, **THEN** each has either even texture (or frame) width and height with `centered == true`, or `centered == false` with an integer `offset`; zero exceptions. (lint) [PR-4 rule 6]
- **GIVEN** a horror rig fixture driven for 600 ticks, **WHEN** segment rotations are read each tick, **THEN** every rotation is an integer multiple of 15° and the rotation changes only on ticks that are multiples of 5. (auto) [PR-4 rule 5]
- **GIVEN** the scripts and scenes under the world `SubViewport`, **WHEN** scanned for `draw_line`, `draw_polyline`, `draw_rect` with `filled = false`, `Line2D` and `draw_primitive`, **THEN** zero matches outside the dev-only debug-draw node, and every match inside it passes a `width ≥ 1` argument. (lint) [PR-4 rule 10, PR-3 (e)]
- **GIVEN** the presentation source files (every script registered as `PRESENTATION`, `INPUT_SURFACE` or `DIAGNOSTIC`), **WHEN** scanned, **THEN** the only property writes target `CanvasItem`, `Material`, `Light2D`, particle or audio members; zero writes to `Camera2D.*`, `Viewport.*`, `SubViewport.*`, `SubViewportContainer.*` or `CanvasLayer.*` members outside the pipeline root and the camera host; and zero reads of `Time.get_ticks_*`, `Time.get_unix_time` or the kernel tick counter used as an animation phase. (lint) [PR-8 items 4, 6; PR-9]
- **GIVEN** a dev build with the fractional-transform tree-walk enabled, **WHEN** 1 000 frames of play across a walk, a run, a cut and a zoom step are recorded, **THEN** the walk reports zero `CanvasItem`s in the world viewport whose `position` has a fractional part after presentation has written it, outside the web and horror-rig groups. (manual) [PR-15 (4), PR-4 rule 7]
- **GIVEN** the pipeline root, **WHEN** a test driver reports `tick_alpha = 1.2` and then `−0.1`, **THEN** in a debug build an assertion fires each time; in a release build the value is clamped to 1.0 and 0.0 and `diag.tick_alpha_out_of_range` increments by 2. (auto) [PR-11; Edge Cases "`tick_alpha` reported > 1 or < 0"]

### Camera follow, look-ahead, cuts

- **GIVEN** `follow` with `τ_follow = 0.18`, **WHEN** the per-frame factor is computed for `dt = 1/60` and `1/144`, **THEN** `a` is 0.0884 and 0.0378 (±0.0001); **WHEN** a synthetic 40 px single-frame target step is applied at 60 fps, **THEN** `f` moves 3.54 px (±0.02) on frame 1, 17.05 px (±0.1) after 6 frames (0.1 s), 37.93 px (±0.1) on frame 32 and 38.12 px (±0.1) on frame 33 — the 38.0 px level is first met on frame 33, not at `t_95 = 0.54 s` (frame 32.4). (auto) [PR-6 C3; D.6 `follow`] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (17.3 → 17.05; the 38.0 px check passes only from frame 33)
- **GIVEN** `CameraSolver.solve` as a `RefCounted` with no scene tree and a scripted **continuous** target trace (idle 0.5 s, a constant 180 px/s run for 1.5 s, then stopped for 4 s; a fixed aim north; no discontinuity), **WHEN** it is driven once with `dt = 1/60` and once with `dt = 1/144`, **THEN** at every multiple of 1/12 s (the only wall times at which both frame grids have a frame: 5 frames at 60 fps, 12 at 144) `|pose_q60 − pose_q144| ≤ 1` base px, `|f60 − f144| ≤ 1.0` px, `|L60 − L144| ≤ 0.05` px, the lists of cuts (frame time, reason) are identical, and both runs end on the same `pose_q`. The contract is a bound, not identity: a one-frame step or an aim flick is a sampled discontinuity whose response depends on `dt`, and is excluded here (the step response is pinned at one fps in the follow criterion above). (auto) [PR-6 C3; D.6, D.7, D.10] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (the 1/60 s sample grid does not land on 144 fps frames; identity replaced by a bound)
- **GIVEN** the solver at rest and a step input of 100 px on one axis at `t = 0`, **WHEN** 600 frames run at 60 fps, **THEN** on every frame `f` has not passed the target (zero overshoot; the sign test is replaced because the settle makes the difference exactly 0), `|target − f|` is non-increasing, `|target − f| ≤ 5 px` from frame 33 (4.71 px; frame 32 is 5.17 px), and from the first frame with `|target − f| < SETTLE_EPS` onward `f == target` exactly. (auto) [PR-6 C3; D.6] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **GIVEN** a target that moves at constant velocity for 1 s then stops, **WHEN** the per-frame camera velocity sign is recorded, **THEN** it never changes sign within any 100 ms window in which the target's velocity sign did not change. (auto) [PR-6 C3; C-R "never reverses within 100 ms unless the player did"]
- **GIVEN** a target at constant 180 px/s (`v_run`) and separately 120 px/s (`v_walk`) for 2 s at the 360-row field, **WHEN** the lag `|target_n − f_n|` is read **after the update** at `t = 2 s`, **THEN** at 60 fps it is 30.9 and 20.6 px (±0.3) and at 144 fps 31.8 and 21.2 px (±0.3); at every rate in {30, 40, 60, 90, 144, 240} fps and at `DT_MAX` it is ≤ `lag_∞` = 32.4 and 21.6 px (28.1 / 18.7 ±0.3 at `DT_MAX`, 29.5 / 19.7 at 30 fps) and decreases monotonically as the rate falls; no cut fired; **GIVEN** the same at the 270-row field (`s = 0.75`), **THEN** at 60 fps 22.8 and 15.2 px (±0.3), and ≤ 24.3 and 16.2 px at every rate; **GIVEN** the 540-row field (`s = 1.5`), **THEN** `τ_follow_eff = 0.18 s` and the 60-fps lag is 30.9 px (±0.3). ### Photosensitivity

- **GIVEN** `flash_count` on a synthetic luminance series, **WHEN** a light alternates 0.9 / 0.4 every 100 ms, **THEN** `F = 5` in a 1 s window (non-compliant); at 250 ms per state `F = 2`; a frame-mean series (`Y_scale = 1`) stepping 0.50 → 0.59 → 0.50 (9 % swings) yields `F = 0`, and the same series on a single light of peak 0.59 (`Y_scale = 0.59`) yields `F = 1` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; a **smooth 10 Hz sine between 0.2 and 0.9 sampled at 60 fps and again at 144 fps yields `F ≥ 5` at both (non-compliant; the running-extremum predicate gives 10), and a 3 Hz sine of the same range yields `F = 3` at both (passes)**, the sine fixtures are generated from `sin(2π f t)` at the sample times, not hand-stepped *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; a 2.5 s beacon sweep yields `F ≤ 1`; a 3 transitions/s fixture yields `F = 2`, a 6 transitions/s fixture `F = 3` (passes) and a 3.5 Hz flicker (7 transitions/s) `F = 4` (fails); three `Y_scale`-floor fixtures recomputed from D.15 (`Y_scale = max(peak, 0.05)`, `Θ = 0.10·Y_scale`): (i) an off light (constant Y = 0, peak 0; `Y_scale` = 0.05, `Θ` = 0.005) never moves, so `n_transitions` = 0 and `F` = 0; (ii) a dim light of peak 0.04 alternating 0.0355 / 0.0400 every 100 ms (swing 0.0045; `Y_scale` = max(0.04, 0.05) = 0.05, `Θ` = 0.005; 0.0045 < 0.005) never fires, `n` = 0, `F` = 0 — without the floor `Y_scale` would be 0.04, `Θ` = 0.004, every swing would count, `n` = 10 per second and `F` = 5, which is exactly what the floor prevents; (iii) the same dim light (peak 0.04) alternating 0.00 / 0.04 every 250 ms (swing 0.04 ≥ 0.005) fires at every state change, `n` = 4 in a half-open 1 s window, `F` = ceil(4/2) = 2 (passes) — and at 100 ms per state `n` = 10, `F` = 5 (fails): the floor suppresses only sub-0.005 ripple and never hides a real swing *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4: fixture recomputed from D.15; formula unchanged)*; `Y` is computed from linear RGB (a mid-grey sRGB 0.5 is `Y` = 0.214, not 0.5). (auto) [PR-13 (a); D.15 `flash_count`] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **GIVEN** the 2.8 breach composition (beacon, vents, deployables, arcs) running 60 s on a dev build with the flash scan enabled, **WHEN** the scan reports per light, per each of the 9 regions (a ninth of the displayed picture at the current field; 213×120 base px at the default field) and on the frame mean, **THEN** every counter reports zero violations of `F_MAX = 3`; CSV `(light_id | region_index, F, window_start_tick)` retained. (manual) [PR-13 (a)(b)(f); D.15]
- **GIVEN** the 2.4 lost state for 60 s, **WHEN** the same scan runs, **THEN** zero violations. (manual) [PR-13 (f)]
- **GIVEN** a fixture light requesting 10 transitions/s, **WHEN** 2 s run, **THEN** the light's rendered luminance never changes more than 3 opposing pairs per rolling second, the clamp only delays transitions (no emitted level outside the requested pair), and diagnostics records `(light_id, F, window_start_tick, clamped_transitions)`. (auto with a Lighting stub) [PR-13 (a); Edge Cases "flash count would exceed"]
- **GIVEN** two lights each at `F = 2` overlapping one region, **WHEN** scanned, **THEN** the regional check reports `F_region > 3`, the newer light's transitions are delayed first, and diagnostics records `(region_index, F_region, light_ids)`. (auto with a Lighting stub) [PR-13 (b); D.15]
- **GIVEN** a shader fixture that writes the whole container to white for one frame, **WHEN** the scan runs, **THEN** `diag.frame_mean_flash` records the frame. (auto with a windowed harness; else manual) [PR-13 (b); Edge Cases "full-frame flash requested"]
- **GIVEN** a fresh user profile, **WHEN** the game first boots and the Settings data is read, **THEN** reduce flashing is ON and reduced motion is OFF, the breach composition runs in the reduce-flashing form below without the player touching a setting, and turning the toggle off restores deployable flicker, arcs and the beacon sweep inside the `F_MAX` clamp. (manual) [PR-13 (c)(e); PR-14] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **GIVEN** the area-weighted rule of D.15 at 1920×1080, `k_z = H = 3`, **WHEN** one 12 × 8 HUD-px numeral cell swaps full-swing 2 times in 1 s, and separately 27 such cells swap together, **THEN** the first is not a region transition (region-mean change 0.0038 per swap) and is exempt from the element-level count with reduce flashing off, the second is one counted region transition per swap, and with reduce flashing ON the exemption is suspended and the first case is counted per element (`F = 1`); an Alarm Red mark requested at 4 transitions/s is held to `F ≤ 3` per element in every setting. (auto with a HUD stub) [PR-13 (g); D.15] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **GIVEN** "reduce flashing" on and reduced motion off, **WHEN** the breach composition runs, **THEN** deployables are steady, arcs are held as glow, the beacon shows a static mark with its sound and HUD glyph, the cryo gutter still dips, look-ahead is still active and the sim `state_hash` after 600 ticks equals the toggle-off run's. (manual for the look; auto for the hash) [PR-13 (c), PR-14]
- **GIVEN** the Settings data and the root scene, **WHEN** scanned, **THEN** no option named gamma, brightness, contrast, sharpening or filter exists, no `Environment` adjustment or `CanvasModulate` is attached at the root, and the only brightness control is the HR-15 step with range 0–3. (lint) [PR-3 (b), PR-13 (d), PR-14]

### Startup assertions & lints

- **GIVEN** a world viewport rendering a flat fill of each palette entry at `k_z` = 3, **WHEN** the displayed picture is read back (a screenshot of the unlit, unfiltered layer 0 on a dev build), **THEN** every pixel of every fill equals its palette value exactly (no ±1 LSB dither) under the project's `use_debanding` setting (required `false`); if the spike enables debanding, the same readback passes with it on. - **GIVEN** a fixture copy of `project.godot` with exactly one required key either **removed (for a key whose required value differs from the engine default)** or **set to a wrong value (for every key, including those whose required value equals the engine default, where removal is a no-op and cannot fail)**, repeated once per key in the list above, **WHEN** the parse test and the startup assertion run against it, **THEN** each run fails, the failure message names exactly that key, and the dev build halts before any window is presented *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. ### Performance budget

Measured by the pipeline's diagnostics timer and `get_render_info` on the two reference machines at their native displays (RM-DESKTOP at 1920×1080, RM-DECK at 1280×800) under `RENDER_MODE = A` (pass B where a criterion says so). **Reference machines and build (delegated ruling R12; recorded in `project.yaml` `qa.min_spec`)**: **RM-DESKTOP** is a minimum-spec desktop — Windows 10/11 64-bit, 6-core/6-thread CPU of the Intel Core i5-9400F / AMD Ryzen 5 2600 class, 8 GB RAM, GPU of the NVIDIA GeForce GTX 1050 Ti 4 GB / AMD Radeon RX 570 4 GB class (Vulkan 1.2+), SATA SSD, 1920×1080 at 60 Hz, P99 CPU frame-time ceiling 16.6 ms; **RM-DECK** is a Steam Deck LCD (2022), SteamOS stable, 1280×800 at 60 Hz panel, 30 fps target, P99 CPU frame-time ceiling 33.3 ms. The developer's machine is not a reference machine; its figures are indicative only. Every wall-clock figure is a **release export** (Godot 4.7.2 release export template, not the editor and not a debug template), renderer `forward_plus`, vsync on, 120-frame warm-up. **Each figure below is pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK; until the measurement build exists a performance criterion is neither a pass nor a fail**, and "NOT ASSESSED" is a gate verdict word, not an AC state, and is not used for a criterion. **These budgets are routed to the technical-director** for ratification at the spike *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*. **Measurement build, P99 and vsync** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*: every wall-clock figure is measured in the build above with the **startup read-back (renderer, driver name; PR-15 (2)) recorded with each result**; a figure from a different configuration is not comparable. **P99 is computed over the frame times of the sampled frames after discarding a warm-up of 120 frames**, from the pipeline's diagnostics timer, and each total-frame-time criterion also reports **the count of missed vsyncs** (a presented frame whose interval exceeded 1.5× the display's refresh interval) over the sample and is judged against a **P99 headroom**: pass needs **P99 ≤ 90 % of the ceiling** (headroom ≥ 1.7 ms on RM-DESKTOP, ≥ 3.3 ms on RM-DECK), the headroom reported with each result. Under vsync the P99 of the *CPU* frame time, not of the presented interval, is the figure compared with the ceiling; the presented interval is reported through the missed-vsync count. **Every figure below is this section's proposal, not a Section D value — the GDD gives no CPU or GPU budget for the pipeline itself.** A miss logs and is raised to the technical-director; it does not fail the suite until the lighting spike (8.12 steps 2, 5, 11–13) has confirmed or replaced the figure. Lighting's cost is excluded from the pipeline-cost figures — it has its own GDD and budget — but **not** from the total-frame-time criteria below, which measure everything running together *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

- **GIVEN** the spike room with 32 active prompts, the tree-walk off and the Filter material null, **WHEN** 1 000 frames run, **THEN** the pipeline root's `_process` wall time (solver, `present_time`, `world_to_hud` for all prompts, state machine) is ≤ 0.5 ms at P99, of which `CameraSolver.solve` is ≤ 0.05 ms. [PR-6 C3, C11; D.6–D.11, D.14]
- **GIVEN** the same scene, **WHEN** the container composite (the ×k upscale of the world texture, Filter null) is timed on the GPU at 1920×1080 and 3840×2160, **THEN** it is ≤ 0.3 ms and ≤ 1.0 ms respectively at P99; with the Filter pass at maximum the added cost is the Filter GDD's budget, reported separately. [PR-7, PR-10]
- **GIVEN** a `size_changed`, **WHEN** the `RESCALING` frame runs, **THEN** re-derivation (`k`, `H`, logical size, bars, limits, `world_to_hud`, viewport reallocation, HUD re-anchor) completes inside that one frame — the following frame is `PRESENTING` at the new `k` — and the re-derivation's CPU time is ≤ 4 ms; a 2 s continuous edge drag produces no frame at a fractional transform. [PR-2; States `RESCALING`; Edge Cases "resize arrives mid-frame"]
- **GIVEN** a dev build with the fractional-transform tree-walk enabled over the spike room, **WHEN** 1 000 frames run, **THEN** the walk adds ≤ 2.0 ms per frame at P99 (dev-only cost; absent in release). [PR-15 (4)]
- **GIVEN** `RENDER_MODE = A` at 1920×1080, **WHEN** render-target memory is sampled, **THEN** the world viewport's targets total ≤ 3 MB; under `B` ≤ 22 MB (art bible 8.6's figures, cited not derived). [PR-7]
- **GIVEN** the D.16 shape, **WHEN** draw calls are read per viewport in the spike room, **THEN** at the default field and every zoom-in step `D_world ≤ 350` and `D_root ≤ 63` (chart table closed; ≤ 93 while open) *(Amended 2026-10-29 — ADR-0008 sync)* with the sum ≤ 1000, and at every zoom-out step (the Deck's 1280×800 field, 1920×1080 at 1×, and the bottom step of 1440p and 2160p) `D_world + D_root ≤ 1000` (counted under "Y-sort & draw calls"). [D.16] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **GIVEN** the spike room with Lighting, the Filter and the HUD running, **WHEN** the total frame time is sampled for 3 000 frames at each of: 1080p default and 1× under pass A and under pass B on RM-DESKTOP, and 1280×800 default and 1× under pass A on RM-DECK, **THEN** (per the measurement build, P99 and vsync rules above: warm-up discarded, CPU-frame P99, missed-vsync count and the headroom reported) the P99 CPU frame time is ≤ 14.9 ms on RM-DESKTOP (90 % of its 16.6 ms ceiling, headroom ≥ 1.7 ms; 8.12 step 2's budget) and ≤ 30.0 ms on RM-DECK (90 % of 33.3 ms, headroom ≥ 3.3 ms; the 30 fps floor of PR-11), at every field; a miss at a zoom-out step withholds that step (Open Question 21; default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): withhold, never cut content) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; CSV retained. (manual) pending spike measurement on RM-DESKTOP / RM-DECK [PR-7, D.16] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*
- **GIVEN** the pass A / pass B table of D.16, **WHEN** the lit-pixel counts are read from the world viewport at each cell, **THEN** they equal the table (±1 row of letterbox residual), and at the bottom step A's count equals B's. (auto for the arithmetic; manual for the read-back) [D.16] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### Gaps / untestable as written

- **Headless runner.** *(A `/test-setup` story for a windowed CI runner — a GPU or software-rasteriser runner that can capture frames — is the fix and is routed to the producer and the lead-programmer; Open Question 22.)* `commands.test` runs `--headless` with no rasteriser, so every pixel-level criterion (frame diffs, checkerboard, HUD-layer diffs, anchor-pixel identity, band width, draw-call counts, the 60 s flash scan) is `(manual)` with retained evidence until a windowed CI runner exists. The `(auto)` set is confined to pure functions, scene-tree walks over unrendered scenes, `project.godot` parsing and lints. A windowed runner would promote roughly 20 criteria above to `(auto)`; recorded as a `/test-setup` item.
- **Open Question 1 (SubViewport wiring).** The `get_final_transform()` criteria are deliberately wiring-agnostic, but *how* the Deck reaches `(2,2)` world + `(3,3)` HUD in one frame, how a ladder step reaches `picture / k_z`, and the `1/k` quantum's container offset depend on wiring (i) or (ii); wiring (i) is the leading candidate (PR-7). Until the render-pipeline ADR lands only those criteria (and `world_to_hud`, `RESCALING`, the `Q = 1/k` path) are gated — the solver, sort and placement criteria are not; the HUD ≠ k case is MVP, so the ADR is the first implementation-story gate, not a defect in this GDD *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **Player Controller GDD (landed).** Every number derived from `v_walk` / `v_run` / `v_max` was re-run on 2026-10-26 against the Controller GDD's `V_WALK` 2 / `V_RUN` 3 px/tick (120 / 180 px/s): `lag_∞ = v·τ_follow` = 21.6 / 32.4 px (the dt→0 bound; 20.6 / 30.9 measured after the update at 60 fps), `J_pan` 70.6 px (the 40 px catch-up burst now sits below the 225 px/s ceiling, so a 47 px burst is the worked near-miss), D.17's walk repeat fraction 16.7 % at 144 fps, and the `LAG_CUT > v_max·τ_follow` margin 48 > 32.4. If the Controller changes its knobs the criteria are re-run by the auto suite, not re-derived by hand. *(Amended 2026-10-26 — consistency check.)*
- **Ship Body & Interior Physics GDD (designed).** The body-transfer rebase (keeps the avatar's screen position), the `PROJECTOR`-set limits rect and the "camera reads the body-local position pair" criteria rest on BDY-14 (`y_foot`), BDY-22/23/27/34 (`BODY_TRANSFER`, `frame_delta_px`); fixtures stand in for Body until its implementation stories land *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **Perception Filter GDD (designed) and the mask route.** The anchor-pixel criterion assumes route (a) (a mask viewport) or (b) (per-element materials); the sampling method differs (mask coverage vs per-node material presence). "Sanity stage 3" is this GDD's name for the Filter's maximum; the Filter GDD must confirm the stage count.
- **Lighting GDD (designed).** The per-light flash clamp and regional delay-ordering criteria run against a Lighting stub; the clamp lives in Lighting's modifier contract. The HR-15 criterion depends on the hull material Lighting defines.
- **Deployable flicker (Open Question 6).** No number exists; the breach-composition scan can only pass once one is authored. Until then the scan's deployable row is `not gated (blocked: flicker unspecified)`, not a pass *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: "NOT ASSESSED" is a gate verdict word, not an AC state)*.
- **Identical-input-trace test needs two things that do not exist:** a recorded input-trace format for the presentation side, and a way to force calm / tell / breach as read-view fixtures — which depends on the Attention and Horror GDDs. The criterion is written against stand-in fixtures.
- **Determinism tolerance.** D.6 is exact only for a target held constant through the frame, and two `exp()` steps are not bit-equal to one. The criterion asserts a bound on a continuous trace — `|pose_q60 − pose_q144| ≤ 1` base px, `|f60 − f144| ≤ 1.0`, compared at multiples of 1/12 s (the only wall times at which a 60 fps and a 144 fps grid both have a frame) — and excludes discontinuities; the gameplay-programmer should confirm the 1 px tolerance or name a different one *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **What the engine draws in the residual of an awkward window (1365×768) is NOT SOURCEABLE.** The logical size is even by construction (D.3), so the question is only the residual strip; the criterion asserts the GDD's promise (an even integer base size and a letterboxed residual: 0 to `2k − 1` px where the cap does not bind, `(W − CAP_W·k)/2` per side where it does, D.3), and step 11's screenshot may force a rewrite of the residual, not of the field *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G4)*.
- **Y-sort tie-break mechanism (→ ADR).** The "identical on 10 runs" and class-rank criteria hold only if the ADR delivers the `(sort_y, class_rank, entity_id)` key (child order maintained by the pipeline root, or a painter's-rank `z_index`); Godot's tree-order tie rule is `[UNV]` and has no comparator hook *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **`diag.*` names are provisional.** Every criterion that reads a diagnostics key (`cut_reason`, `inner_box_engaged`, `camera_zoom_violation`, …) depends on names Section E marks provisional; the pipeline, as owner, freezes them under the Debug Overlay's channel-registration convention (registry `diag_panel_contract`: owners freeze their own diagnostic names; the Overlay restates none as a fact). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from debug-overlay-true-values)*
- **Performance figures are unsourced.** The GDD gives no pipeline CPU/GPU budget; the block above proposes 0.5 ms / 0.05 ms / 0.3 ms / 4 ms / 2 ms and cites 8.6 for memory. All are provisional until the spike; a miss logs, it does not fail.
- **`state_hash` across fps caps 30 / 60 / 144 / 0.** Written above under Pixel integrity & motion, but it is the kernel's G3 criterion (PR-11 says so) and runs in the kernel suite with the pipeline attached; a headless run cannot vary a real render rate, so the fps variation is a synthetic frame-time sequence fed to the driver — the on-build confirmation is spike step 13.
- **Release-build reactions** ("dev build halts", "release returns to the chart table") are observable only in a dev build or an export; the criteria test the dev halt and the export's file list, not the chart-table return.
- **Playtest metric (> 50 % away from default zoom).** A reporting criterion, not pass/fail; the game-designer owns the threshold's consequence.
- **Test directory and parse keys.** The `(auto)` test directory `tests/unit/render/` was accepted from Section H on 2026-10-26 (the kernel uses `tests/unit/kernel/`; the directory-structure doc should confirm the sibling). The `project.godot` parse test checks `physics/common/physics_interpolation = false`, which the Section H acceptance note pins into PR-15 (1) alongside the keys added by the design review *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.
- **Camera quantum `1/k` (Open Question 11).** The D.9 `o` offset criterion is `(auto)` on the formula only; the `1/k` path's container clipping and pass-B behaviour are `[UNV]` and untested until the quantum is adopted.
